```markdown
# Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit — Beyond the Vale of Madness (GURPS) as Benchmark

## 1. Executive Summary

This audit inventories all mechanical demands actually exercised or assumed by Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS (a solo “choose-your-path” dungeon crawl with 2 featured combats), then evaluates whether the current Tiwas baseline (Canonical Core + Ruled non-canonical subsystems in the supplied corpus) can support those demands without inventing new Tiwas mechanics.

Bottom line A partial Tiwas playtest is possible now, but a full-fidelity playtest of the adventure’s mechanical intent is blocked primarily by missing Tiwas content-authoring layers (S-12 creatureNPC stats, equipment tagsitems, and especially magic) and by incomplete S-3 gated-tier effect enumeration (needed for fearterror, darkness penalties, grapples, corrosive ongoing damage, etc., unless treated as pure narrative).

### Key blockers (highest impact)
 Blocker  Type  Why it blocks 
---------
 S-12 creatureNPC stat blocks not table-ready  Missing subsystemcontent (per corpus Part C.1)  Adventure requires at least two hostile monsters with tactical behavior Ice Troll and Blood Man (and implied wolvesghouls). Without S-12 content, Tiwas cannot run the fights mechanically. 
 Magic  spells (pyromancer pregens reference GURPS Magic)  Missing subsystem  The adventure explicitly offers a spellcasting pregen and refers to GURPS Magic effects; Tiwas baseline has no magic implementation. 
 S-3 gated-tier effect contents not enumerated  Design-stage dependency  Many adventure outcomes imply conditions (fear), positioning, grapplehold, ongoing corrosive damage, darkness combat penalties, etc. Tiwas has the menu skeleton (DEC-023+) but not the concrete effect list for these. 
 Equipmentencumbrancewealth economy  Missing subsystem (mostly)  Adventure uses ST requirements, weapon damage profiles, weights, loot value, and carrying limits for tapestries. Tiwas has no canonical equipment economyencumbrance rules in provided baseline. 

### What is playtestable now (useful benchmark)
- Core Test Transaction (DEC-006) for climbing, search, willpower, perception-like checks, tracking, forced entry, etc.
- Difficulty grades as additive effective-skill modifiers (DEC-063–066).
- Opposed contests (S-1, DEC-013), applicable to stealth vs perception in principle (but requires statted opponents).
- HPMPPhysical Energy cost + Overflow (DEC-007 + DEC-007.A) as a harsh risk model for repeated tests and “exhaustion”.

---

## 2. Audit Objective and Method

### Objective
Use the adventure as an external benchmark to determine whether Tiwas currently provides the mechanics needed to run the adventure’s full mechanical surface area, and to identify which Tiwas subsystems are blocking that.

### Method (systems-engineering style)
1. Extract mechanical requirements from the adventure text (paragraph triggers, roll calls, combat procedures, item mechanics, environmental hazards, and any implied mechanics such as darkness penalties and fear).
2. Normalize resolution treat every GURPS skillattribute roll as requiring a standard Tiwas Core Test (DEC-006), not a GURPS-like engine.
3. For each requirement, assess
   - Tiwas capability (Canonical  Ruled  Open)
   - Readiness category (per prompt)
   - Blocking significance
   - Remaining work (without inventing mechanics; only identify missing decisionscontent)

Authoritative sources used
- Adventure parsed PDF text provided (19 pages of content; includes pregens and treasure table).
- Tiwas baseline `Tiwas-Alpha-Playtest-Corpus-2026-09-01.md` (Core Locked + Ruled DEC material; explicit “not table-ready” list).

---

## 6. Missing  Design-Stage Dependencies (Consolidated)

### 6.1 Hard missing (cannot run “as written” mechanically)
1. S-12 creature stat blocks for
   - Ice Troll (including cold-regeneration concept)
   - Blood Man (night vision + grapple + corrosive seep)
2. Magic subsystem (for Enfys pregen and magic items)
3. Equipment subsystem (weapons, damage, armor, ranged combat, ammo, shields)

### 6.2 Design-stage (architecture exists; concrete contentrules missing)
1. S-3 gated-tier effect enumeration (ConditionPositionEquipmentDefenseLocation tiers)
2. Fear and lighting as conditionstags and their mechanical effects
3. Defense mitigation semantics (how S-6 reducesnegates which Effects)
4. Environmental hazard modeling (coldexhaustion) beyond “systemic threats exist”

## 7. Adventure Path Dependency Analysis

### 7.1 Critical path nodes that force missing Tiwas systems
 Adventure junction  Mechanical gate  Tiwas blocker  Notes 
------------
 Cave route → Ice Troll encounter  Combat (#17) or stealth contest (#44)  S-12 creatures; equipmentcombat resolution  Even sneaking requires troll Perception-equivalent value. 
 Castle basement → Blood Man  Fright check + combat + darkness penalty (#38)  Fear subsystem; lightingvision; S-12 creature; grappleongoing damage  This is one of the adventure’s “setpiece” mechanics. 
 “First Aid restores HP”  Healing roll (#21#37)  S-11 exists but is Extended Test; mapping differs  Requires adaptation decision treat as one-interval rest or a different effect 
 Loot carry limits  ST thresholds (#51)  Encumbrance  carry model missing  Could be handled as narrative gating, but it is explicitly mechanical in text. 

### 7.2 Non-critical but still assumed mechanics
- Lockpicking  forced entry can be run as Core Tests, but require skill mapping.
- Tactical map option is optional; can be omitted but then you lose part of the adventure’s assumed combat mode.

---

## 8. Tiwas Readiness Assessment (Against This Benchmark)

### 8.1 What Tiwas can already benchmark well here
- High-frequency risky testing the adventure’s many small checks are a good stress test of Cost=Roll and Overflow immutability, and will rapidly surface whether players “spam tests” or become resource-starved.
- Difficulty handling many “-3-5untrained” style calls are a good benchmark for S-8’s effective-skill modifiers.

### 8.2 What this benchmark shows Tiwas is not ready to validate
- Creature combat loop without S-12 and an equipmentattack model, the adventure cannot validate Tiwas combat as a product experience.
- Conditions as gameplay feardarknessgrapplingongoing damage are central to the Blood Man scene; Tiwas cannot currently express these as settled mechanics.

---

## 9. Development Priorities Exposed by the Adventure

Ranked by “unblocks full playtest of this specific module”

1. S-12 tabletop creature stat-block method + author the two monsters
   - Minimum deliverable simple GM-run stat blocks with Tiwas Skill values needed for contests and effects.
2. Equipment + attack payload model (even a minimal alpha layer)
   - Must support melee weapon attack, ranged crossbow (single bolt), basic armor notion (even if tag-only), shield handling if relevant.
3. S-3 gated-tier effect content enumeration (at least for)
   - Fearterror condition
   - Darknessvisibility condition
   - GrappleBreak Hold
   - Ongoing damage (corrosive “per time-step”) or a Tiwas-native equivalent
4. Defense mitigation semantics (S-6 concretization)
   - Define what outcomes a successful defense produces for Injury and Conditions.
5. Environmental hazard procedure (coldexhaustion)
   - Could be expressed as a hazard repeatedly forcing Body-domain tests, with Overflow doing the “damage” work; but needs a settled cadencetrigger policy.

Magic is also major, but optional if you agree the benchmark run will use a non-magical Tiwas character rather than the provided Enfys pregen.

---

## 10. Conclusions

- Playtestable Now (partial) exploration checks, navigation, climbing, search, locked doors, and resource attrition using the Core Test and S-8 difficulty.
- Not fully playtestable the adventure’s defining scenes (Ice Troll and Blood Man) cannot be run mechanically in Tiwas without S-12 content, an equipmentcombat payload layer, and conditiongrappledarknessfear effect definitions.
- The module is therefore a strong benchmark for prioritizing S-12 creature authoring + minimal combatequipment + concrete condition effects.

## 12. Explicit Assumptions  Unresolved Questions

1. Difficulty tier numeric values are not shown in the provided excerpt; this audit assumes they exist but requires a mapping policy from adventure modifiers (-3, -5, etc.) to Tiwas named grades.
2. Combat time model (roundsseconds) is not defined in Tiwas baseline; therefore “per second” ongoing damage (Blood Seep) cannot be represented without new mechanics.
3. The audit treats GURPS “damage dice” outputs as requiring an adaptation decision (how to express hazardattack magnitude in Tiwas) because Tiwas baseline does not define a dice-based damage model.
4. Skill cataloging Tiwas allows creating skills via the cap formula mechanism, but no canonical skill list exists; mapping “ClimbingLockpickingetc.” to Tiwas skills is treated as adaptation content work, not a missing Tiwas rule.
5. Using pregenerated characters the provided pregens are GURPS-specific; Tiwas would require Tiwas-native character builds. This audit treats that as adaptation content, except for the spellcaster whose functionality depends on missing magic rules.

If you provide (a) the S-8 difficulty tier numeric modifier table, and (b) any existing draft for a minimal Tiwas combat payload model (even if non-canonical), I can re-run the assessment to separate “missing subsystem” from “mapping required” more sharply for combat, hazards, and equipment.
```

## btvmadness-adapt-report-chatgpt-5-5-high-1.md
> **LLM attribution:** ChatGPT 5.5-high - report 1

```markdown
# Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit  
## *Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS* as a Tiwas Benchmark

**Audit role:** Tiwas TTRPG Systems Readiness Auditor and Adaptation Analyst  
**Adventure source:** *Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS*, v1.2, supplied parsed PDF text/images  
**Tiwas source:** *Tiwas — Alpha-Playtest Corpus (One-Off, GM Table-Ready)*, 2026-09-01  
**Audit posture:** Mechanical readiness benchmark, not a conversion document

---

# 1. Executive Summary

## 1.1 Overall Finding

**The adventure is not currently fully playtestable in Tiwas.**

Tiwas can already support the **core transactional act of making a roll-under d100 Skill test**, including resource cost, Overflow, Failure XP, Recovery, and Advanced Skill triggers. Therefore, many of the adventure’s ordinary GURPS skill and attribute rolls can be mechanically represented as **Tiwas Core Tests**, provided adventure-specific Tiwas skill mappings are authored.

However, the adventure makes heavy use of systems that are either **missing**, **only partially ruled**, or **not content-complete** in the current Tiwas baseline:

* combat attack/damage resolution;
* weapon damage and damage types;
* monster stat blocks and creature templates;
* tactical movement / map combat / reach / turn sequencing;
* armor and shield interaction with damage;
* fall damage and environmental hazards;
* fatigue / exhaustion / hypothermia;
* grappling and ongoing corrosive absorption;
* fright/fear consequences;
* equipment, encumbrance, carrying capacity, and item stats;
* magic, enchantment, powers, and GURPS-style advantages;
* healing amounts and immediate First Aid outcomes;
* economy / loot conversion / advancement reward mapping.

The adventure is therefore useful as a **diagnostic benchmark**: it exposes that Tiwas’ Core Test architecture is mature enough for binary task checks, but the surrounding adventure-facing subsystems required by a fantasy dungeon/survival scenario are not yet sufficiently complete for a full adventure playtest.

## 1.2 Positive Readiness

The following Tiwas functions are **Playtestable Now**, subject to adaptation mapping where noted:

| Function | Tiwas readiness |
|---|---|
| d100 roll-under task resolution | **Playtestable Now** — Canonical / Locked Core Test |
| failure XP and advancement from failed rolls | **Playtestable Now** — Canonical / Locked |
| resource cost and Overflow | **Playtestable Now** — Canonical / Locked |
| ordinary binary task checks | **Adaptation Mapping Required** — underlying Core Test exists |
| opposed contests | **Playtestable Now** for primitive; **Adaptation Mapping Required** for skills/NPC values |
| HP = 0 incapacitation | **Playtestable Now** as Non-canonical designer ruling |
| paragraph state flags / adventure variables | **Adventure Content Only** |
| General XP awards | **Playtestable Now** as Core economy; reward conversion required |

## 1.3 Critical Blockers

| Critical blocker | Responsible Tiwas area | Why it blocks the adventure |
|---|---|---|
| No complete combat loop | S-1/S-3/S-4/S-5/S-6/S-7/S-12 plus missing attack/damage content | Ice troll and blood man encounters require attacks, defenses, damage, tactics, monster stats, retreat, and death/incapacitation. |
| No non-Overflow damage magnitude system | S-3/S-4 incomplete for damage magnitude | Adventure uses explicit damage dice for weapons, falls, traps, claws, corrosive seep, and hypothermia. Tiwas has HP and Overflow but no general mapped damage amounts. |
| No creature stat blocks/templates | S-12 Open / content not table-ready | Ice troll and blood man require Tiwas attributes, skills, HP, defenses, attacks, traits, and special abilities. |
| Equipment and weapons not established | Reserved / missing subsystem, S-5 partial armor only | Adventure depends on swords, mace, dagger, crossbow, armor, shield, rope, pitons, torches, lockpicking with dagger, item weight, ST requirements, and encumbrance. |
| Environmental hazard model absent | Reserved / missing subsystem; S-2 notes systemic hazards but no mechanics | Cold, hypothermia, falls, icy stairs, cliffs, snow digging, and darkness are mechanically active. |
| Fatigue/exhaustion not available | Missing subsystem / no separate fatigue economy | Adventure repeatedly subtracts FP and uses FP = 0 collapse/death. Tiwas has Physical Energy but no fixed attritional fatigue system. |
| Magic/special abilities absent | Reserved / missing subsystem | Pregenerated spellcaster, enchanted dagger, powerstone, Night Vision, Fearlessness, Luck, Regeneration, Regrowth, Injury Tolerance, etc. are not supported as Tiwas mechanics. |

## 1.4 Best Immediate Use of the Adventure

The adventure is suitable now for a **limited alpha diagnostic**, not a full playtest:

1. Use it to test **ordinary Core Tests** for noncombat choices.
2. Use it to test **resource cost, Overflow, Failure XP, and Recovery** during exploration checks.
3. Use it to identify where Tiwas needs formal subsystem completion before fantasy adventure play.
4. Do **not** use it yet as a full Tiwas combat/adventure validation scenario.

---

# 2. Audit Objective and Method

## 2.1 Objective

The objective is to use *Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS* as an external practical benchmark for Tiwas readiness.

The audit asks:

> What mechanical functions does the adventure require, and can Tiwas currently perform those functions using the current Tiwas baseline?

This audit does **not** attempt to preserve GURPS procedures. GURPS is treated only as the source adventure’s expression of mechanical demand.

## 2.2 Method

The audit followed this procedure:

1. Identify every meaningful mechanical requirement in the adventure text.
2. Convert ordinary GURPS attribute/skill rolls into the requirement:  
   **“perform a standard Tiwas Core Test.”**
3. Classify each requirement against the Tiwas baseline:
   * Canonical / Locked;
   * Non-canonical designer ruling;
   * Design-stage / Open;
   * Missing;
   * Adventure content only.
4. Determine whether the requirement is:
   * Playtestable Now;
   * Adaptation Mapping Required;
   * Design-Stage Dependency;
   * Missing Subsystem;
   * Adventure Content Only.
5. Identify whether each gap blocks:
   * full adventure playtesting;
   * only specific optional branches;
   * only exact conversion fidelity;
   * only campaign/economy follow-up.

## 2.3 Important Interpretive Constraint

All ordinary GURPS skill rolls and attribute rolls are treated as requiring the **standard Tiwas Core Test**.

Therefore:

* GURPS `DX roll`, `ST roll`, `Will roll`, `Merchant roll`, etc. are **not** imported as GURPS mechanics.
* Each such roll must be mapped to an appropriate Tiwas Skill.
* The Tiwas Core Test transaction remains intact:
  1. Roll d100.
  2. Determine resource domain.
  3. Determine success/failure.
  4. Pay Cost equal to natural roll.
  5. Apply Overflow if needed.
  6. Resolve Failure XP.
  7. Resolve failed Double / Advanced Skill effects.
  8. Recover resource.
  9. End test.

## 2.4 Status Caveat

The supplied Tiwas Alpha-Playtest Corpus is itself explicitly **not canonical** and does not promote any rule. It compiles:

* Canonical / Locked rules;
* Non-canonical designer rulings;
* Open or non-table-ready items.

This audit respects the status labels in that corpus.

---

# 3. Source Adventure Mechanical Inventory

This section records the adventure’s mechanical demands before assessing Tiwas compatibility.

## 3.1 Navigation, State, and Solo-Adventure Procedure

---

# 4. System-by-System Tiwas Compatibility Assessment

## 4.1 Core d100 Resolution and Ordinary Tests

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Resolve ordinary skill/attribute checks | Will, DX, ST, Climbing, Search, Merchant, Tracking, etc. | Tiwas Core Test resolves roll-under Skill tests with d100, cost, Overflow, XP, Recovery | Canonical / Locked: DEC-001–DEC-012 | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Major but tractable | Author Tiwas skill mapping for each adventure test. Attribute-only GURPS rolls cannot be rolled directly as GURPS attributes; they need Tiwas Skill mappings. |
| Preserve resource cost on every test | Every Core Test adaptation | Cost equals natural roll; PE or MP depending on skill domain | Canonical / Locked: DEC-006/007 | **Playtestable Now** | Systemically important | Must decide whether each mapped skill is Body- or Mind-rooted. |
| Handle failed rolls and advancement | Any failed check | Failure XP, Skill Roll Pool, General XP, Advanced Skill creation | Canonical / Locked: DEC-009–DEC-012 | **Playtestable Now** | Positive readiness | No adventure-specific blocker, but playtest logs should record XP effects. |
| Handle GURPS unskilled defaults | DX-5, IQ-4, ST-4, etc. | Tiwas has Skill starting values and difficulty modifiers, but no GURPS default conversion | Core skill architecture Canonical; difficulty Non-canonical; exact mapping absent | **Adaptation Mapping Required / Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Need policy for untrained attempts: existing Tier-1 skill, authored Advanced Skill, zero skill, or difficulty penalty. |

### Assessment

The **Core Test transaction itself is ready**. The adventure’s many binary exploration checks can exercise it immediately once a Tiwas adaptation author decides which Tiwas Skill each check uses.

However, Tiwas does not currently provide a complete canonical skill dictionary matching adventure activities like Lockpicking, Climbing, Survival, Physician, or Tactics. That is not a failure of the Core Test, but it is an adaptation-content requirement.

---

## 4.2 Difficulty, Modifiers, and Defaults

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Easier/harder task modifiers | #18 +2, #27 +3, #35 -3, #58 -2, etc. | S-8 establishes named difficulty tiers with fixed additive Skill-side modifiers | Non-canonical designer ruling: DEC-063–DEC-066 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Supplied corpus does not provide the actual fixed modifier table. Cannot consistently map GURPS numeric modifiers. |
| Apply modifier to success and XP | All modified checks | Effective Skill used for success, Failure XP, and Skill Roll Pool cascade entry | Non-canonical: DEC-064/065 | **Playtestable Now in architecture; not complete in values** | Moderate | Needs numeric grade values and adventure-specific difficulty mapping. |
| Encumbrance penalty to Climbing | #15 | Tiwas encumbrance is Reserved / not defined | Reserved by DEC-015 | **Missing Subsystem** | Moderate | Need encumbrance/load subsystem or adventure-specific substitute. |
| Darkness attack penalty | #38 | Difficulty can modify Skill side, but attack/darkness combat interaction not defined | S-8 partial; combat missing | **Design-Stage Dependency / Missing Combat Dependency** | Major in blood man fight | Need darkness/visibility condition and combat attack mapping. |

### Assessment

S-8 gives Tiwas the **shape** of a difficulty system but not enough supplied mechanical detail to execute all adventure modifiers. The adventure is modifier-heavy, so a full playtest needs S-8’s fixed modifier table and a conversion policy.

---

## 4.3 Opposed Contests

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sneak past ice troll | #44 Quick Contest of Stealth vs Perception 12 | S-1 Universal Opposed Contest | Canonical / Locked: DEC-013 | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Major for cave path | Need Tiwas Stealth-equivalent Skill, troll Perception-equivalent Skill/stat, and Quality mode selection. |
| Mutual success tie-breaking | Any opposed contest | S-1 Quality comparison by Margin/Blackjack/Hybrid | Canonical / Locked | **Playtestable Now** | Low/moderate | Need select Quality mode; likely Margin for stealth/precision. |
| Mutual failure | Any opposed contest | Repeat contest; third-party adjudication exists if relevant | S-1 Canonical; S-8 DEC-043 non-canonical for third-party adjudication | **Playtestable Now** | Low | #44 can use repeat-on-mutual-failure without third party. |
| Combat opposition | #17, #38 | S-1 can be primitive for opposed actions, but complete attack/defense/effect/magnitude loop absent | Partial: S-1/S-3/S-6 | **Design-Stage Dependency / Missing Subsystem** | Critical | Need combat procedure, NPC stats, damage/effect magnitude, equipment, defense mitigation. |

### Assessment

The opposed-contest primitive is ready. The adventure’s **stealth contest** is a good candidate for immediate S-1 testing once NPC values are authored. Combat cannot be made ready merely by invoking S-1 because the downstream combat payload is not complete.

---

## 4.4 S-2 Location Index and Hit/Impact Location

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Determine location for attacks/effects | Armor coverage, Blood Seep large-area injury, possible wounds | Zero-Step Tier-1 Location Index provider exists | Canonical / Locked: DEC-014 | **Partial / Design-Stage Dependency** | Major for armor/wounds | Zero-Step gives a number, but the full anatomical mapping and exact ranges are not fully supplied in executable form. |
| Non-attack physical hazards produce location | Falls, traps, environmental injury | DEC-037 governs failed-roll provenance for non-attack Location Index | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Moderate | Only matters if the adapted hazard uses location-referencing Effects. |
| Systemic hazards need no location | Extreme temperature, poison-like hazards | DEC-037 exempts systemic threats such as Extreme Temperature | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Playtestable as principle; mechanics missing** | Major for cold | Direct HP/conditions are allowed, but actual cold/hypothermia mechanics are absent. |
| Armor location check at Tier 0 | Armor coverage | S-5 uses single-purpose Zero-Step read for armor coverage | Non-canonical DEC-062 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major for combat/falls | Needs armor tags, coverage templates, and damage/effect interaction. |

### Assessment

Tiwas has an elegant **Location Index provider**, but the adventure requires more than a number. It needs anatomical mapping, armor coverage, injury consequences, and creature templates. Those remain incomplete or content-dependent.

---

## 4.5 S-3 Effects and Outcome Payloads

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Inflict injury from attacks/hazards | Combat, falls, traps, corrosive seep, hypothermia | Base-tier Inflict Injury exists as HP-only Effect | Non-canonical DEC-023 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | No supplied rule defines HP damage magnitude for a successful attack/effect in adventure terms. |
| Force retreat / compel yield | Troll may retreat at #17 | Open Retreat / Compel Yield is base-tier Effect | Non-canonical DEC-023 | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Moderate | Troll retreat is adventure-authored morale behavior, not necessarily S-3 Effect use. |
| Conditions | Darkness, wounded, grappled, fear, hypothermia | Condition tier exists structurally | Non-canonical DEC-023; contents not enumerated | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical for blood man/fear/cold | Specific Condition Effects are not fully enumerated; grapple/darkness/fear/hypothermia not table-ready. |
| Equipment effects | Sunder, disarm, armor bypass | Equipment tier / Sunder exists partially | Non-canonical DEC-028/060 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Moderate | Adventure does not require Sunder, but equipment interaction is broad. |
| Position effects | Tactical starting positions, movement, retreat | Position tier exists structurally | Non-canonical DEC-023; contents open | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major in combat | Position effects not enumerated; tactical subsystem absent. |

### Assessment

S-3 is a major exposed dependency. It provides a **menu architecture**, but the adventure needs concrete effect payloads: damage amounts, conditions, grapples, position shifts, and equipment interactions. Those are not sufficiently established.

---

## 4.6 S-4 Injury, Wounds, and Damage Consequences

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HP damage | All combat/hazard damage | HP exists; Injury = HP damage | HP Canonical via derived stats; Injury terminology Non-canonical DEC-032 | **Partial** | Critical | HP pool exists, but external damage magnitude procedure is missing. |
| Wounded state | Blood Seep requires target wounded and grappled | Wound = localized lasting numerical state; Wounded Condition via S-3 | Non-canonical DEC-032–035 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major for blood man | Wound consequences are OPEN-007 / not table-ready. |
| Damage types | cut/cr/cor | No complete Tiwas damage type taxonomy supplied | Missing / S-3 gated contents incomplete | **Missing Subsystem** | Critical for adventure fidelity | Need damage Tags/types and interactions. |
| Large-Area Injury | Blood Seep uses GURPS large-area injury | No Tiwas equivalent | Missing | **Missing Subsystem** | Major | Need area/coverage damage rules or adaptation replacement. |
| Consciousness after injury | #15, #52, #17 | HP = 0 forced incapacitation exists | Non-canonical DEC-052 | **Playtestable Now for HP=0 only** | Major | No separate consciousness checks, shock, or partial unconsciousness system. |
| Death | #17, #38, #52 | Permanent loss branches exist | Non-canonical DEC-054/055 | **Partial** | Moderate | Adventure often declares death narratively; Tiwas death requires failed revival attempts or voluntary permanent loss. |

### Assessment

Tiwas has HP and incapacitation but lacks the adventure-facing damage machinery. This is one of the largest blockers.

---

## 4.7 S-5 Armor, Shields, DR, and Protection

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Numeric DR reduces damage | Monster DR, armor on pregens, “soft armor protecting” | S-5 explicitly uses Tags/Traits only; no numeric soak pool | Non-canonical DEC-058 | **Design-Stage Dependency / Not directly compatible** | Critical for combat fidelity | Need Tag-based armor content and interaction with injury magnitude. Numeric DR is not imported. |
| Shield as defense/protection | Medium shield #13; Block on character sheet | S-6 Active Defense exists; equipment shield content absent | Non-canonical S-6; equipment missing | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Need shield Tags, defense Skill mapping, mitigation rule. |
| Armor coverage by location | Character armor locations, large-area injury | S-5 location-bound coverage | Non-canonical DEC-062 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Need coverage templates and anatomical mapping ranges. |
| Armor bypass | Possible in Tiwas, not explicit in adventure | S-5 Bypass relation exists | Non-canonical DEC-059 | **Playtestable only after Tag content** | Low for this adventure | Not directly required unless adaptation uses it. |
| Natural DR / monster hide | Ice troll DR 2, blood man DR 1 | S-5 is armor Tags/Traits for defensive gear; natural defenses not content-authored | Partial / Missing creature traits | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Need creature trait implementation or natural armor Tags. |

### Assessment

The adventure’s GURPS DR model cannot be imported. Tiwas’ armor direction is Tag-based, which is legitimate but not yet content-complete enough to express the adventure’s protective gear and natural armor.

---

## 4.8 S-6 Active Defense

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Dodge, Parry, Block | Combat stat blocks and pregens | S-6 defender rolls voluntary Active Defense as Core Test | Non-canonical DEC-044–050 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical in combat | Need actual defensive Skills, equipment mappings, and mitigation effect. |
| No defense after All-Out Attack | Ice troll #17 | No Tiwas action-state model for All-Out Attack supplied | Missing combat/action subsystem | **Missing Subsystem** | Moderate/major | Need action economy and defensive availability rules. |
| Defense against each effect | Blood man claws + grapple/seep; possible multiple effects | S-6 separate mitigation per effect | Non-canonical DEC-049 | **Architecturally Playtestable; payload missing** | Major | Needs effect definitions and mitigation magnitude. |
| Defender may decline | General combat | S-6 allows voluntary decline | Non-canonical DEC-046 | **Playtestable Now** | Low | Works once attacks/effects exist. |

### Assessment

S-6 supplies the **defensive architecture** but not enough concrete implementation to run the adventure’s combats. The biggest missing piece is not who rolls defense, but what defense mechanically mitigates when attack damage/effects are not defined.

---

## 4.9 S-7 Incapacitation and Death

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HP depletion causes incapacitation | Combat and hazard endings | HP = 0 forced incapacitation, no roll | Non-canonical DEC-052 | **Playtestable Now** | Important positive readiness | Needs HP damage source to matter. |
| Death/permanent loss | #17, #38, #52 | Death occurs after failed revival attempts or voluntary choice | Non-canonical DEC-054/055 | **Partial** | Moderate | Adventure’s instant death endings are adventure content; alignment decision needed. |
| Wounds do not cause incapacitation | Blood man wounds | Tiwas incap is HP-driven only | Non-canonical DEC-053 | **Playtestable Now** | Useful boundary | Blood man’s “wounded and grappled” trigger still needs wound/grapple rules. |

### Assessment

S-7 is one of the more usable non-canonical subsystems. It does not unblock combat by itself, but once HP damage exists, incapacitation can be tested.

---

## 4.10 S-8 Difficulty and Third-Party Adjudication

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Difficulty grades | Modified checks throughout | Named tiers with fixed additive Skill-side modifiers | Non-canonical DEC-063 | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Fixed modifier values are not supplied in the corpus. |
| Effective Skill for XP/cascade | Any modified check | Effective Skill drives success, Failure XP, Skill Roll Pool entry; Cap clamp applies | Non-canonical DEC-064/065 | **Playtestable Once Values Exist** | Moderate | Need actual values and adaptation mapping. |
| No stakes gate | Adventure rolls are frequent and meaningful | Stakes Gate rejected | Non-canonical DEC-051 | **Playtestable Now** | Positive | Supports rolling as written after mapping. |
| Third-party adjudication | Not materially used | DEC-043 exists | Non-canonical | **Not relevant** | None | No adventure dependency except hypothetical arbitration. |

### Assessment

The adventure heavily stresses S-8. The architecture is accepted, but the absence of an executable difficulty table is a practical blocker for full readiness.

---

## 4.11 S-9/S-10 Extended Tests

| Adventure requirement | Where used | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Readiness | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Prolonged effort over time | Digging through ice/snow #10/#21; possible healing/rest | Margin-accumulation Extended Tests | Non-canonical DEC-067–070 | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Moderate | Adventure mostly uses single checks plus FP costs, not formal multi-interval progress. |
| Completion target | Any adapted extended effort | GM discretion, no formula | Non-canonical DEC-070 | **Playtestable Now by GM discretion** | Low/moderate | Need adventure-specific targets if converted to Extended Tests. |
| Failed interval consequences | Extended Tests | Failures cost resources and give XP, but no progress loss | Non-canonical DEC-068 | **Playtestable Now** | Low | Usable if designer chooses Extended Test model. |

### Assessment

S-9/S-10 are not a major blocker because the adventure does not deeply require extended-test mechanics. They could be useful for adapting digging or healing, but that would be an adaptation decision.

# 5. Playtestability Matrix

## 5.1 Classification Key

| Classification | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Playtestable Now** | Tiwas currently provides sufficient mechanics. |
| **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Tiwas has the underlying mechanic, but adventure-specific mapping/content must be authored. |
| **Design-Stage Dependency** | Tiwas has a ruling direction or partial subsystem, but not enough executable detail for dependable play. |
| **Missing Subsystem** | No adequate Tiwas mechanism currently exists. |
| **Adventure Content Only** | Setting, pathing, item identity, NPC name, treasure list, etc.; not a Tiwas mechanical gap. |

## 5.2 Matrix by Tiwas System

| Tiwas area | Adventure exercises it? | Readiness | Blocking significance | Notes |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Core d100 / Core Test | Yes, heavily | **Playtestable Now** | Positive foundation | All ordinary rolls become Core Tests. |
| Skill mapping | Yes | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Major | Need Tiwas skills for Climbing, Lockpicking, Survival, Search, etc. |
| Resource Cost / Overflow | Yes, every test | **Playtestable Now** | Positive foundation | Must be preserved; may make exploration checks dangerous. |
| Failure XP / Advanced Skills | Yes, indirectly | **Playtestable Now** | Low/positive | Adventure can stress advancement during failed checks. |
| S-1 Opposed Contest | Yes, #44 and combat conceptually | **Playtestable Now / Adaptation Mapping Required** | Moderate | Stealth contest can work once NPC values exist. |
| S-2 Location Index | Yes, via armor/injury needs | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Zero-Step exists; anatomical mapping/content incomplete. |
| S-3 Effects | Yes, combat/conditions | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Effect structure exists; damage/condition payloads incomplete. |
| S-4 Injury/Wounds | Yes | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | HP exists; wound consequences and damage magnitude absent. |
| S-5 Armor | Yes | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major/critical | Tags-only armor direction conflicts with direct DR import; content absent. |
| S-6 Defense | Yes | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Defense architecture exists; mitigation/equipment/combat details absent. |
| S-7 Incapacitation/Death | Yes | **Playtestable Now / Partial** | Moderate | HP=0 incap works; adventure instant death alignment unresolved. |
| S-8 Difficulty | Yes, heavily | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Fixed modifier table not supplied. |
| S-9/S-10 Extended Tests | Lightly | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Low/moderate | Useful for digging/healing if designer chooses. |
| S-11 Healing | Yes | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Major | Immediate First Aid HP restoration not ready. |
| S-12 Creatures | Yes, critically | **Missing Content / Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Ice troll and blood man not available as Tiwas creatures. |
| Equipment/weapons | Yes, heavily | **Missing Subsystem** | Critical | Needed for almost every combat/exploration branch. |
| Encumbrance/load | Yes | **Missing Subsystem** | Moderate | Climbing and treasure carry use it. |
| Environmental hazards | Yes, heavily | **Missing Subsystem** | Critical/major | Cold, falls, darkness, hypothermia, exhaustion. |
| Fatigue/exhaustion | Yes | **Missing Subsystem** | Critical/major | Adventure uses FP loss and FP=0 collapse. |
| Magic/special abilities | Yes, if pregens used | **Missing Subsystem** | Major/critical | Enfys and monster traits depend on it. |
| Economy/loot | Yes | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Low/moderate | Can track source values but Tiwas economy not established. |
| Advancement rewards | Yes | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Moderate | Need character-point to General XP mapping. |

---

# 6. Missing / Design-Stage Dependencies

## 6.1 Critical Missing Subsystems

| Missing subsystem | Adventure evidence | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| Combat attack/damage loop | #17 ice troll; #38 blood man | Adventure contains two major combats with monster attacks, PC attacks, defenses, tactics, death/incapacitation. |
| General damage magnitude | 1d-1, 2d-2, 1d-4, 1d-3, 1d-2, 1d, 1d+2, corrosive per second | Tiwas has HP but no general way to assign non-Overflow damage amounts. |
| Creature stat blocks/templates | Ice troll and blood man stat blocks | S-12 contains no table-ready creatures. |
| Weapon and equipment mechanics | Mace, dagger, sword, crossbow, shield, rope, torch, armor | Equipment is reserved except partial armor rulings. |
| Environmental hazards | Cold, cliffs, icy stairs, darkness, wind, hypothermia | Exploration branches depend on hazard consequences. |
| Fatigue/exhaustion | FP loss at #10, #19, #21; collapse at FP 0 | Tiwas has PE/MP Core resources but no GURPS-style attritional fatigue. |
| Magic/special abilities | Enfys spells, Shatterproof dagger, troll regeneration | Reserved; no subsystem available. |
| Grappling | Blood man must wound and grapple victim | No complete grapple/hold subsystem. |
| Fright/fear consequences | Fright Checks #38, #58 | No Tiwas fear table/condition consequence. |

## 6.2 Design-Stage or Partial Dependencies

| Dependency | Current Tiwas status | Adventure impact |
|---|---|---|
| S-3 Effect contents | Menu structure ruled; gated-tier contents not fully enumerated | Blocks concrete conditions, position effects, equipment effects, wounds. |
| S-4 Wound consequences | OPEN-007 not table-ready | Blocks localized lasting injuries and “wounded” mechanics beyond label. |
| S-5 Armor | Tags/Traits architecture ruled | Cannot directly express numeric DR; needs content and damage interaction. |
| S-6 Defense | Architecture ruled | Needs defensive skill mappings and mitigation procedure. |
| S-8 Difficulty | Architecture ruled | Needs fixed modifier values. |
| S-11 Healing | Direction ruled | Needs immediate First Aid and HP restoration mapping. |
| S-12 Creature content | Rulings only; no stat blocks | Blocks monsters. |
| Location anatomy | Zero-Step locked; anatomical mapping/ranges not fully executable from supplied corpus | Blocks reliable armor/location play. |

## 6.3 Adaptation-Content Dependencies

These are not necessarily Tiwas system gaps, but must be authored before play:

| Adaptation content | Examples |
|---|---|
| Skill mapping table | Climbing → Tiwas Skill; Lockpicking → Tiwas Skill; Survival → Tiwas Skill, etc. |
| Difficulty mapping | GURPS +2/-3/etc. → Tiwas named difficulty grades once S-8 values exist |
| Monster conversion | Ice troll and blood man as Tiwas entities |
| Treasure conversion | GURPS dollar values → Tiwas economy or abstract loot score |
| Reward conversion | GURPS character points → Tiwas General XP |
| Equipment tags | Antique mace, glass dagger, shield, crossbow, armor, torches |
| Environmental consequence mapping | Falls, cold, darkness, hypothermia, pit jumps |

| Ending/reward | #60 | Loot values, rescue reward, advancement | **Adaptation Mapping Required** |

## 7.2 Shortest Low-Combat Diagnostic Route

A designer could construct a limited diagnostic route that avoids full combat, for example:

`#1 → #19 → #3 → #43 → #14 → #33 → #23 → #55 → #46 → #53 → #60`

This still exercises:

* Survival / cold;
* Search;
* Merchant;
* DX jump;
* corrosive injury;
* loot tracking;
* reward/ending.

Even this route is **not fully supported** because it includes environmental damage, possible FP/hypothermia, jump/fall damage, and corrosive damage.

## 7.3 Combat-Heavy Diagnostic Route

The troll route:

`#1 → #12 → #9 → #31 → #8/#41 → #44/#17 → #21`

This route stresses:

* stealth opposed contest;
* monster detection;
* tactical combat;
* attacks and defenses;
* monster morale;
* HP damage;
* regeneration/natural DR;
* healing;
* FP cost from digging.

This route is currently **blocked at multiple critical layers**.

## 7.4 Blood Man Route

The blood man route:

`#33 → #55 → #7/#46 → #38 → #48 → #56/#60`

This route stresses:

* darkness and light source handling;
* Fright Checks;
* tactical positioning via Tactics;
* attack penalty from darkness;
* grappling;
* corrosive ongoing damage;
* Injury Tolerance / Night Vision;
* Will to search gore;
* treasure and ending.

This is the single densest subsystem stress test in the adventure and is **not currently Tiwas-playtestable** except as a gap demonstration.

---

# 8. Tiwas Readiness Assessment

## 8.1 Full Adventure Readiness

**Classification:** **Not Ready for Full Tiwas Playtest**

Reason:

The adventure’s complete mechanical envelope requires multiple systems that Tiwas does not yet provide in executable form. The Core Test is ready, but the adventure is not merely a sequence of binary checks. It is a fantasy survival/combat scenario with equipment, monsters, hazards, damage, and magic.

## 8.2 Partial Diagnostic Readiness

**Classification:** **Partially Playtestable as an Alpha Diagnostic**

Tiwas can currently test:

* Core Test transaction under repeated exploration checks;
* resource-domain selection pressure;
* Overflow consequences from tests;
* Failure XP and skill advancement;
* simple branch-state recording;
* S-1 opposed contest primitive in stealth, if NPC values are authored;
* HP = 0 incapacitation once damage exists or is manually assigned for test purposes.

However, such a session must be explicitly labeled as a **partial diagnostic**, not a full adventure conversion.

## 8.3 Canonical Readiness vs Non-Canonical Readiness

| Layer | Readiness |
|---|---|
| Canonical Core | Strong for task resolution; insufficient alone for adventure play |
| Non-canonical subsystems | Several directions exist, but many are partial or content-incomplete |
| Open / missing systems | Numerous critical blockers for fantasy adventure play |

## 8.4 Systems the Adventure Does *Not* Strongly Require

The adventure does **not** materially require:

| System | Reason |
|---|---|
| Third-party adjudication | No meaningful third-party contest arbitration appears. |
| Deep campaign procedure | Adventure is a solo one-shot. |
| Social influence system | Social rewards are branch outcomes, not negotiated mechanically. |
| Crafting | Not used. |
| Large-scale travel rules beyond cold Survival | Travel is mostly narrative except #19. |
| Complex rest scheduling | Healing appears as immediate First Aid, not long rest management. |

---

# 9. Development Priorities Exposed by the Adventure

## Priority 1 — Combat Payload and Damage Magnitude

**Responsible systems:** S-3, S-4, S-6, S-7, equipment, S-12

Tiwas needs a complete way to answer:

* When an attack succeeds, what happens?
* How much HP damage occurs?
* How are weapon Tags or creature attacks expressed?
* How does armor reduce, redirect, block, or alter the effect?
* How does Active Defense mitigate an already-applied Effect?
* How do multiple attacks/effects per turn work?

Without this, neither the ice troll nor blood man can be run.

## Priority 2 — Creature / NPC Content Authoring

**Responsible system:** S-12

The adventure requires at least:

* Ice Troll;
* Blood Man;
* injured hunter as noncombat NPC;
* optional implied tundra wolves/grave ghouls only if expanded.

Tiwas needs a table-ready creature stat-block format and content authoring standard.

## Priority 3 — Equipment, Weapons, Armor, and Shields

**Responsible systems:** Equipment subsystem, S-5, S-6

The adventure exposes the need for:

* weapon Tags and attack profiles;
* shields as defensive equipment;
* armor coverage Tags;
* tool permissions and bonuses;
* item weight/load interaction;
* weapon prerequisites or equivalents;
* light sources.

The S-5 Tags-only armor ruling is directionally useful but insufficient by itself.

## Priority 4 — Environmental Hazards and Fatigue

**Responsible systems:** Environmental hazards, Conditions, possible fatigue-as-Condition policy

Needed for:

* extreme cold;
* hypothermia;
* fixed exertion costs;
* falls;
* slippery terrain;
* darkness;
* wind gusts;
* collapse from exhaustion.

This adventure is an excellent benchmark for whether Tiwas can support survival exploration.

## Priority 5 — Difficulty Table and Skill Mapping Policy

**Responsible system:** S-8 plus skill taxonomy/adaptation guidance

The adventure has many numeric GURPS modifiers and unskilled defaults. Tiwas needs:

* fixed named difficulty modifier values;
* a policy for converting adventure difficulty statements;
* a policy for untrained attempts;
* recommended mapping from common fantasy tasks to Tiwas Skills.

## Priority 6 — Healing and First Aid

**Responsible system:** S-11

Needed for:

* post-combat bandaging;
* immediate HP restoration;
* stabilizing/rescuing injured NPCs;
* possible spell healing if magic is added.

S-11’s Extended Test framing is useful but does not yet satisfy the adventure’s immediate First Aid use.

## Priority 7 — Conditions: Darkness, Fear, Grappled, Wounded, Hypothermia

**Responsible systems:** S-3 Condition tier, S-4, environmental hazards, combat

The blood man encounter especially requires concrete condition handling.

## Priority 8 — Magic and Special Traits

**Responsible systems:** Magic/special abilities, creature traits, item enchantments

Not needed for every route, but necessary if using the supplied Enfys character or preserving monster traits.

## Priority 9 — Economy and Advancement Mapping

**Responsible systems:** Economy/equipment; General XP reward policy

Needed for campaign carry-forward, not for immediate mechanical survival.

---

# 10. Conclusions

## 10.1 Primary Conclusion

*Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS* is an effective Tiwas readiness benchmark because it immediately exposes the boundary between Tiwas’ strong Core Test architecture and its incomplete adventure-facing subsystems.

The Core is ready. The adventure is not.

## 10.2 What Can Be Playtested Now

Tiwas can currently playtest:

* ordinary exploration checks as Core Tests;
* success/failure branching;
* resource cost and Overflow from tests;
* Failure XP and Advanced Skill creation;
* simple opposed contests;
* state flags as adventure content;
* HP = 0 incapacitation once damage is available.

## 10.3 What Cannot Be Fully Playtested

Tiwas cannot yet fully playtest:

* the ice troll encounter;
* the blood man encounter;
* weapon damage;
* armor/DR interactions;
* falling and environmental damage;
* fatigue loss and collapse;
* Fright Check consequences;
* grappling and corrosive seep;
* magic/spellcasting;
* pregenerated GURPS characters as mechanical equivalents;
* treasure economy and character-point reward conversion.

## 10.4 Diagnostic Value

The adventure strongly suggests that Tiwas’ next development phase should focus on:

1. combat/damage/effect payloads;
2. equipment/weapons/armor;
3. creature stat blocks;
4. environmental hazards/fatigue;
5. difficulty values and common skill mapping;
6. healing and conditions.

Until those are complete, the adventure should be used as a **requirements checklist**, not as a full conversion target.
```
