| #60 | Loot sale, rescue reward, character-point reward |
| Treasure table | Monetary values |
| Pregenerated character sheets | Equipment, armor, weapons, traits, spells, skills, encumbrance, wealth/status |

## 11.2 Tiwas Evidence

| Tiwas source item | Relevance |
|---|---|
| DEC-001–DEC-012 | Core d100, Core Test, resource cost, Overflow, XP, Advanced Skills |
| DEC-013 | S-1 Universal Opposed Contest |
| DEC-014 | Zero-Step Location Index provider |
| DEC-015 | Reserved systems list: armor, defense, wounds, equipment, encumbrance, environmental hazards, magic, NPCs, etc. |
| DEC-016 | Core architectural invariants |
| DEC-023–DEC-031 | S-3 Effect menu/gating; one Effect per win; auto-apply; base Injury/Open Retreat |
| DEC-032–DEC-037, DEC-041–DEC-042 | S-4 Injury/Wounds/Location handling |
| DEC-043, DEC-051, DEC-063–DEC-066 | S-8 difficulty and adjudication |
| DEC-044–DEC-050 | S-6 Active Defense |
| DEC-052–DEC-057 | S-7 Incapacitation/Death |
| DEC-058–DEC-062 | S-5 Armor Tags/Traits |
| DEC-067–DEC-070 | S-9/S-10 Extended Tests |
| DEC-071–DEC-074 | S-11 Rest/Healing |
| DEC-076–DEC-077 | S-12 creature/campaign content rulings; no table-ready stat blocks |
| Part C, C.1 | Creature/campaign content not table-ready |
| Part C, C.3 | S-3 gated-tier Effect contents not fully enumerated |
| Part C, OPEN-007 | Wound consequences not table-ready |

---

# 12. Explicit Assumptions / Unresolved Questions

## 12.1 Assumptions Made

1. **Ordinary GURPS rolls become Tiwas Core Tests.**  
   No GURPS roll-under, 3d6, Quick Contest, Fright Check, or default mechanic is imported.

2. **A Tiwas “attribute roll” requires a Skill mapping.**  
   The supplied Tiwas Core defines Skill tests. Therefore GURPS DX/ST/IQ/Will rolls must be mapped to appropriate Tiwas Skills rather than rolled directly as GURPS attributes.

3. **Adventure damage expressions are requirements, not usable Tiwas mechanics.**  
   Damage such as `1d-3 cut` or `1d+2 cut` indicates that the adventure needs a damage system. It is not automatically imported into Tiwas.

4. **State words such as `DARKNESS`, `AMBUSHED`, and `RESCUER` are adventure content unless their effects require Tiwas mechanics.**

5. **The supplied Tiwas corpus is used as the status baseline.**  
   Where it says material is non-canonical or open, this audit treats it accordingly.

6. **The source wording “gain 2 characters” at #60 is not corrected silently.**  
   It appears to mean “gain 2 character points,” but the audit treats that as unresolved source wording.

## 12.2 Unresolved Questions for Tiwa

| Question | Why it matters |
|---|---|
| What are the fixed numeric S-8 difficulty modifiers? | Required for nearly every modified adventure test. |
| How should common fantasy tasks map to Tiwas Skills? | Required for Climbing, Survival, Lockpicking, First Aid, Merchant, Search, Tactics, etc. |
| How does Tiwas assign HP damage from a successful attack or hazard? | Critical blocker for combat and environmental play. |
| How does Active Defense mitigate an applied Effect numerically or categorically? | S-6 architecture exists but adventure combat needs concrete output. |
| Are fatigue/exhaustion effects modeled as PE loss, HP damage, Conditions, or something else? | Adventure uses FP loss and FP = 0 collapse. |
| How should falls and hypothermia be represented? | Frequent exploration hazards. |
| What is the table-ready creature stat-block format? | Needed for ice troll and blood man. |
| How are weapons represented? | Needed for every combat-capable character and treasure weapon. |
| How are shields represented? | Needed for medium shield and Block-like defense. |
| How should immediate First Aid healing work? | Adventure restores HP immediately after troll fight. |
| Will Tiwas have a fear/fright condition system? | Required by #38 and #58. |
| How are magic, enchantments, and powers intended to integrate with the Core Test resource economy? | Required for Enfys and enchanted items. |
| Should adventure loot values map directly to Tiwas economy, or become abstract treasure ratings? | Needed for #60 reward and loot threshold. |
| What General XP amount corresponds to the adventure’s character-point award? | Needed for advancement. |

## 12.3 Final Audit Statement

The adventure is an excellent benchmark for Tiwas because it demands a complete fantasy-adventure mechanical stack. Current Tiwas can support the **roll transaction layer**, but not yet the full **adventure execution layer**.

**Recommended next action:** complete a minimal fantasy-adventure readiness slice consisting of:

1. task skill mapping + S-8 numeric difficulty values;
2. basic combat attack/effect/damage loop;
3. weapon/armor/shield Tags and payloads;
4. two creature templates: Ice Troll and Blood Man;
5. fall/cold/darkness/fatigue hazard rules;
6. immediate First Aid / healing rule;
7. reward conversion to General XP.

Only after that slice exists should *Beyond the Vale of Madness* be attempted as a full Tiwas playtest.
```

## btvmadness-adapt-report-chatgpt-5-6-1.md
> **LLM attribution:** ChatGPT 5.6 - report 1

```markdown
# Tiwas TTRPG — *Beyond the Vale of Madness* Adaptation Readiness Audit

# 1. Executive Summary

## 1.1 Finding

**Beyond the Vale of Madness is an excellent readiness benchmark for Tiwas because it is mechanically small but touches a surprisingly broad cross-section of RPG subsystems.**

The adventure contains:

* ordinary skill/attribute tests;
* difficulty modifiers;
* opposed contests;
* combat;
* attacks and defensive resolution;
* damage;
* HP;
* armour/DR;
* grappling;
* tactical positioning;
* falling and environmental hazards;
* fatigue/exhaustion;
* healing;
* incapacitation/death;
* fear/fright;
* equipment;
* encumbrance/carrying;
* weapons;
* ranged weapons;
* special abilities;
* magic;
* NPC/creature construction;
* creature regeneration;
* conditions/status-like effects;
* time/duration;
* rewards/economy;
* character advancement;
* adventure-state tracking.

That makes it substantially more useful than an adventure containing only generic skill checks.

### Overall readiness conclusion

**Tiwas cannot currently run the entire adventure as a clean, mechanically complete Tiwas playtest without adaptation work.**

However, the result is **much better than the historical Canonical v2.1 status would suggest**, because the 1 September Alpha corpus contains a large set of subsequently ruled non-canonical subsystems.

In particular, the following are now sufficiently specified for alpha table use:

* Core Test;
* S-1 opposed contests;
* S-2 Zero-Step/location framework;
* S-3 base Effect architecture;
* S-4 injury/wound direction;
* S-5 armour direction;
* S-6 active defence;
* S-7 incapacitation/death direction;
* S-8 difficulty;
* S-9/S-10 Extended Tests;
* S-11 Rest/healing.

The Alpha corpus explicitly describes itself as a one-off table-ready assembly of currently Locked and Ruled material, while retaining the distinction that the ruled material is **non-canonical**. 

Therefore the benchmark is now showing something important:

> **The remaining problem is no longer simply “Tiwas lacks many RPG subsystems.” It is that some systems are mechanically ruled but still lack complete content, implementation, or adaptation interfaces.**

---

# 2. Audit Objective and Method

## 2.1 Benchmark principle

The adventure is being treated as a **black-box requirements test**.

The question is not:

> “How do we translate GURPS?”

The question is:

> **“If this adventure were handed to a Tiwas GM today, what rules would the GM need in order to resolve every mechanical situation presented by the adventure?”**

This is the correct benchmark because the adventure is already a functioning published scenario.

The adventure itself describes *The Castle of Madness* as an introductory solo adventure for GURPS Fantasy/Dungeon Fantasy and provides two pregenerated characters. 

---

# 3. Source Adventure Mechanical Inventory

## 3.1 Core mechanical requirements identified

|  # | Adventure system requirement   | Evidence in adventure                                                                  | Tiwas requirement                      |
| -: | ------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
|  1 | Ordinary skill tests           | DX, Will, Search, Survival, Climbing, Lockpicking, Merchant, Tracking, First Aid, etc. | Core Test                              |
|  2 | Attribute-based fallback tests | DX-5, ST-4, IQ-4, etc.                                                                 | Core Test with Tiwas task mapping      |
|  3 | Test modifiers                 | +2, +3, -2, -3, -5, -8, etc.                                                           | Difficulty / Skill-side modification   |
|  4 | Opposed contest                | Stealth vs Perception                                                                  | S-1                                    |
|  5 | Combat                         | Ice troll and Blood Man encounters                                                     | Combat architecture                    |
|  6 | Attack resolution              | Claws, maw, weapons                                                                    | Combat + S-3                           |
|  7 | Defence                        | Dodge, Parry; explicit All-Out Attack/no defence                                       | S-6                                    |
|  8 | Damage                         | Crushing, cutting, corrosive damage                                                    | Injury / damage system                 |
|  9 | HP                             | Characters and creatures possess HP                                                    | Core HP + harm/death                   |
| 10 | Armour                         | DR and location-specific armour                                                        | S-5                                    |
| 11 | Hit location                   | Body armour lists torso/arms/hands/feet; Blood Seep uses locations                     | S-2                                    |
| 12 | Falling                        | Multiple specified fall damages                                                        | Hazard + damage                        |
| 13 | Fatigue                        | FP deductions and collapse/death                                                       | Resource/exhaustion                    |
| 14 | Healing                        | First Aid/Physician restores HP                                                        | S-11                                   |
| 15 | Incapacitation                 | unconscious/dead outcomes                                                              | S-7                                    |
| 16 | Fear                           | Fright Checks                                                                          | Conditions / special mental resolution |
| 17 | Grappling                      | Blood Man's Blood Seep requires grapple                                                | Combat/Effect/Condition                |
| 18 | Tactical combat                | Battle maps, starting positions, movement                                              | Time/action/tactical combat            |
| 19 | Ranged combat                  | Crossbow and throwing weapons                                                          | Equipment/combat                       |
| 20 | Equipment                      | Weapons, armour, rope, grapple, torches, backpacks                                     | Equipment                              |
| 21 | Encumbrance                    | Climbing penalties per encumbrance level                                               | Encumbrance                            |
| 22 | Carrying capacity              | ST threshold for tapestry                                                              | Equipment/encumbrance                  |
| 23 | Magic                          | Enfys has spells and magical items                                                     | Magic/special abilities                |
| 24 | Creature abilities             | Regeneration, Regrowth, Night Vision, etc.                                             | Conditions/abilities/magic grammar     |
| 25 | NPC stat blocks                | Ice Troll, Blood Man, pregens                                                          | S-12                                   |
| 26 | Temporal effects               | Regeneration per minute, Blood Seep per second, spell durations                        | Time/action framework                  |
| 27 | Rewards                        | Treasure values, $500 reward, character advancement                                    | Economy/progression                    |
| 28 | Adventure state flags          | DARKNESS, AMBUSHED, RESCUER                                                            | Adventure-state implementation         |
| 29 | Tactical/behaviour rules       | Troll retreat/fight-to-death behaviour                                                 | NPC/GM procedure                       |
| 30 | Character traits               | Advantages, disadvantages, racial traits                                               | Character/ability system               |

---

# 4. System-by-System Compatibility Assessment

## 4.1 Ordinary Skill Resolution

### Adventure requirement

The adventure repeatedly instructs the player to make a named skill roll.

Examples include:

* Will;
* DX;
* Climbing;
* Survival;
* Search;
* Lockpicking;
* Tracking;
* First Aid;
* Physician;
* Merchant;
* Stealth;
* Tactics.

The adventure also supplies attribute-based fallbacks when the relevant skill is absent. For example, Lockpicking becomes DX-3 and Search becomes Perception-5. 

### Tiwas status

**Playtestable Now — Core Resolution**

Every such test can use the Tiwas Core Test.

The canonical transaction already establishes:

1. roll;
2. determine resource domain;
3. success/failure;
4. pay natural-roll cost;
5. resolve Overflow;
6. resolve Failure XP;
7. resolve failed Double;
8. recover;
9. end test.



### Important limitation

The **resolution engine is ready**.

The **adventure-to-Tiwas skill taxonomy is not automatically solved**.

This is an adaptation-content problem rather than evidence that the Core Test is missing.

**Readiness: 🟢 Playtestable Now, with adaptation mapping.**

---

# 5. Difficulty Modifiers

The adventure uses explicit numerical modifiers such as:

* +2 Lockpicking;
* +3 Tracking;
* -2 Fright Check;
* -3 Climbing;
* -5 Search;
* -8 climbing fallback;
* encumbrance penalties.

Tiwas now has a ruled S-8 architecture in which difficulty modifies **effective Skill**, never the natural die. The effective Skill is used for success/failure, Failure XP and the Skill Roll Pool cascade. Grades are symmetric. 

### Result

The *function* is available.

What remains is the **actual Tiwas difficulty-grade numerical table/interface** needed to translate the adventure's explicit GURPS modifiers.

Therefore:

**Readiness: 🟡 Adaptation Mapping Required**

This does **not** require importing GURPS's difficulty system.

---

# 6. Opposed Tests

The adventure explicitly uses a Quick Contest:

> Stealth vs distracted creature's Perception 12.



Tiwas S-1 is directly applicable.

S-1 independently resolves both participants' Core Tests and uses the success/failure matrix, with Quality resolving success/success outcomes. 

### Result

The adventure's Stealth-vs-Perception situation is a very strong direct test of S-1.

**Readiness: 🟢 Playtestable Now**

This is one of the clearest successes of the benchmark.

---

# 7. Combat

## 7.1 Requirement

The adventure contains two explicit combat encounters.

The ice troll encounter allows either abstract combat or tactical combat using the supplied map. 

The Blood Man encounter likewise supports tactical combat and specifies attacks, grappling, damage and special abilities. 

## 7.2 Tiwas status

This is the largest remaining integration problem.

Although S-1, S-3, S-5 and S-6 now have ruled architectures, **the complete Combat subsystem is not equivalent to having those individual rulings**.

The Alpha corpus provides:

* Effect application;
* Active Defense;
* armour interaction;
* injury/wound architecture.

But the complete combat procedure still needs a coherent operational interface for:

* declaring attacks;
* determining the appropriate attacking Skill;
* tactical sequencing;
* movement;
* range;
* weapon interaction;
* combat timing;
* grappling;
* ongoing combat effects.

### Result

**Readiness: 🔴 Design-Stage / Integration Dependency**

Combat is therefore the **primary benchmark blocker**.

---

# 8. Damage and Harm

The adventure contains numerous explicit damage events:

* falling;
* weapon attacks;
* claws;
* maw;
* corrosive Blood Seep;
* environmental injury.

For example, failed climbing can produce `2d-2` crushing damage, while the Blood Man's Blood Seep produces ongoing corrosive damage. 

Tiwas now has the important architectural distinction:

* **Injury = HP damage**
* **Wound = localized lasting numerical state.**

The S-4 ruling explicitly separates those tracks. 

However, the exact adaptation of GURPS damage dice/types to Tiwas's Effect architecture is not established by the adventure audit.

### Result

**Readiness: 🟡 Design-stage integration**

The underlying Tiwas harm architecture exists substantially, but the complete operational damage model required by combat and hazards is not demonstrated by the available table-ready corpus.

---

# 9. Armour

The adventure explicitly uses armour and DR.

The Blood Man has DR 1 and the Ice Troll DR 2. The pregenerated characters have location-specific armour such as torso, arms, hands and feet protection. 

Tiwas S-5 now has a ruled armour architecture:

* Armor Tags;
* location-bound protection;
* shared individual-creature anatomy;
* Zero-Step support for Tier-0 armour checks;
* Armor Bypass and Sunder interactions.



### Result

The **architecture is substantially ready for alpha use**, but the adventure still requires actual Tiwas armour items/tags.

**Readiness: 🟡 Adaptation Mapping / Content**

---

# 10. Hit Location

This adventure provides a particularly useful hit-location test because armour coverage is explicitly anatomical.

Tiwas now has Zero-Step as the canonical Tier-1 numeric provider, but the current corpus makes clear that location is not simply synonymous with the entire hit-location subsystem. 

The Alpha corpus subsequently records anatomical mapping and armour-location rulings, including the shared individual-creature anatomy. 

### Result

This adventure can exercise S-2/S-5, but it will require the actual anatomical mapping and tier policy to be instantiated in the adaptation.

**Readiness: 🟡 Adaptation/Integration**

---

# 11. Defence

The GURPS adventure explicitly uses Dodge and Parry, and specifically states that the Ice Troll may All-Out Attack with two claws **but no defence**. 

Tiwas S-6 is now ruled as:

* Active Defense;
* defender rolls;
* voluntary;
* uncapped;
* post-hoc mitigation;
* separate Defense roll per Effect;
* full Core Test consequences.



This is a major compatibility result.

### Important distinction

GURPS's **Dodge/Parry statistics do not transfer**.

But the adventure's underlying requirement—

> “the target gets an opportunity to mitigate an incoming combat consequence”

—is now represented by Tiwas S-6.

### Result

**Readiness: 🟢 Playtestable Now at subsystem level**

**Adaptation content still required:** define the relevant Tiwas defensive Skill(s)/Effects for the adventure.

---

# 12. Falling and Environmental Hazards

The adventure contains numerous physical hazards:

* cliffs;
* icy stairs;
* falling;
* cold;
* exhaustion;
* frozen passages;
* dangerous terrain.

For example, failure on the icy cliff causes 2d-2 damage, and failure on icy stairs causes 1d-2 damage. 

Tiwas has an important S-2 ruling for physical risks: a failed governing Core Test can provide the Location Index, while systemic threats can apply direct HP/Conditions without location. 

### Result

The **resolution architecture exists**.

The complete environmental-hazard grammar remains an integration/content dependency.

**Readiness: 🟡 Design-stage integration**

---

# 13. Fatigue and Resource Depletion

The adventure repeatedly uses GURPS FP:

* subtract 1 FP during the trek;
* subtract 2 FP breaking through ice;
* FP reaching zero causes collapse/death.



Tiwas has a fundamentally different resource model:

* Physical Energy;
* Mental Points;
* natural-roll resource cost;
* Overflow → HP;
* Recovery after every test.

This is not a simple one-for-one FP conversion.

### Important finding

The adventure's fatigue system is **not actually required as a separate Tiwas subsystem** if its fictional purpose can be represented by the existing PE/HP economy.

However, the exact adventure-specific conversion is not currently established.

**Readiness: 🟡 Adaptation Mapping Required**

This is therefore **not automatically evidence that a new fatigue subsystem must be designed.**

---

# 14. Healing

The adventure uses:

> First Aid or Physician

to restore HP after combat. Failure still produces 1 HP of recovery. 

Tiwas S-11 now provides a ruled healing architecture:

* explicit Skill Test;
* ordinary Core Test;
* wound magnitude modifies effective Skill;
* healing is an Extended Test;
* Margin accumulation;
* neutral failures;
* GM-set completion target.



### Result

The adventure's **healing requirement is now directly testable against S-11**.

The GURPS “restore 1d-3 HP / minimum 1” procedure itself should **not** be imported.

**Readiness: 🟢 Playtestable Now at subsystem level; adaptation required for the adventure-specific healing task.**

---

# 15. Incapacitation and Death

The adventure repeatedly distinguishes:

* still conscious;
* unconscious;
* death;
* permanent adventure termination.

Tiwas S-7 now has a ruled architecture:

* HP = 0 → forced incapacitation;
* no immediate death;
* incapacitation is HP-driven;
* revival uses skill tests;
* permanent loss occurs after failed revival attempts or voluntary permanent loss.



### Result

**Readiness: 🟢 Playtestable Now at subsystem level.**

This is another significant readiness improvement over the older Canonical v2.1 status.

---

# 16. Fear / Fright

The Blood Man requires a Fright Check, and the corpse scene requires a Fright Check at -2. 

The current Tiwas material does **not provide a fully enumerated fear/fright subsystem or established Condition implementation** sufficient to say that this is ready.

The fact that Tiwas has Mind attributes, MP, Skills and Effects does not itself constitute a Fright system.

### Result

**Readiness: 🔴 Missing / Design-stage dependency**

This is an important finding because the adventure is explicitly horror-inflected despite being mechanically simple.

---

# 17. Grappling

The Blood Man specifically relies upon:

1. wounding its target;
2. grappling;
3. applying Blood Seep;
4. applying ongoing corrosive damage.



This requires more than generic combat.

It needs a mechanical state representing:

> “Target is grappled.”

The current Tiwas material does not provide a complete grapple subsystem.

### Result

**Readiness: 🔴 Missing / Design-stage dependency**

This is one of the strongest concrete combat blockers exposed by the benchmark.

---

# 18. Time and Ongoing Effects

The adventure contains effects measured in:

* seconds;
* minutes;
* hours;
* days;
* spell durations;
* regeneration intervals.

Blood Seep, for example, operates per second, while Ice Troll regeneration operates per minute. 

The current Tiwas architecture identifies Time/Action Economy as a cross-cutting subsystem.

### Result

Because the adventure contains ongoing effects, **time is not optional if the full adventure is to be run faithfully as a Tiwas test**.

**Readiness: 🔴 Design-stage dependency**

---

# 19. Equipment and Encumbrance

The adventure depends heavily on equipment:

* swords;
* daggers;
* mace;
* axes;
* shield;
* crossbow;
* rope;
* grapple;
* torches;
* pitons;
* backpacks;
* clothing;
* armour;
* treasure.

The adventure also uses encumbrance mechanically, including climbing penalties per encumbrance level. 

The Tiwas Alpha corpus identifies Equipment and Tags as cross-cutting architecture, but the complete equipment system is not represented as a finished universal subsystem.

### Result

**Readiness: 🔴 Missing/Design-stage subsystem**

This is a major practical blocker because equipment is not incidental in this adventure—it is repeatedly used to determine what actions are possible.

---

# 20. Magic

The advanced pregenerated character is a spellcaster, and the character sheet contains spells such as:

* Fireball;
* Flame Jet;
* Minor Healing;
* Dark Vision;
* Light;
* Sense Danger;
* Warmth;
* Recover Energy.

The sheet also contains spell costs, durations and casting requirements. 

Tiwas's architectural direction is that magic should emerge through Advanced Skills rather than become a competing core engine.

But the actual **Magic/Special Ability grammar is not complete**.

### Result

**Readiness: 🔴 Missing / Design-stage subsystem**

The adventure can therefore serve as a strong future magic benchmark, but it cannot currently be run with the supplied spellcaster unchanged.

---

# 21. NPC and Creature Construction

The adventure contains explicit creature stat blocks.

The Ice Troll includes:

* ST;
* HP;
* Speed;
* DX;
* Will;
* IQ;
* Perception;
* HT;
* FP;
* DR;
* Dodge;
* Parry;
* attacks;
* traits;
* regeneration;
* skills.



The Blood Man has a similar block plus special Blood Seep behaviour. 

Tiwas S-12 has now established the architecture that automated systems use full 24-attribute generation, while tabletop/GM use an abbreviated representation, but actual creature templates remain content-authoring work. 

### Result

**Subsystem architecture: 🟢/🟡**

**Adventure-ready creature implementation: 🔴**

The benchmark therefore identifies **NPC content authoring**, rather than merely NPC architecture, as a real remaining workload.

---

# 22. Rewards and Progression

The adventure contains:

* treasure values;
* monetary reward;
* completion rewards;
* monster-defeat reward;
* loot-based reward;
* future-adventure currency.

The final treasure table lists values for the recovered objects, while completion awards additional characters. 

Tiwas already has General XP and quest/adventure rewards as a mechanism. 

### Result

**XP progression: 🟢 Playtestable Now**

**Currency/economy: 🔴 Missing/setting-content dependency**

The adventure's reward structure can therefore be used as a test of **General XP**, while the monetary economy should not be mistaken for an already-developed Tiwas economy.

---

# 23. Character Generation

The adventure provides pregenerated GURPS characters.

Their sheets contain:

* attributes;
* skills;
* advantages;
* disadvantages;
* racial traits;
* equipment;
* spells;
* wealth;
* languages;
* other GURPS-specific construction elements.

For example, King Coppertong has a large set of GURPS advantages/disadvantages, equipment and skills. 

Tiwas has the mathematical Core for:

* 24 attributes;
* Skill Caps;
* starting Skills;
* derived statistics;
* Advanced Skills.

But a complete **adventure-ready character-generation procedure and starting package system** is not equivalent to those mathematical rules.

### Result

**Readiness: 🟡 Core mathematics ready; character-generation implementation/content required.**

---

# 24. Playtestability Matrix

| System                     | Required by adventure? | Current Tiwas capability              | Status                  |  Full-adventure blocker? |
| -------------------------- | ---------------------: | ------------------------------------- | ----------------------- | -----------------------: |
| Core d100 resolution       |                    Yes | Locked                                | 🟢 Playtestable         |                       No |
| Standard skill tests       |                    Yes | Locked                                | 🟢 Playtestable         |                       No |
| Opposed tests              |                    Yes | S-1 locked                            | 🟢 Playtestable         |                       No |
| Difficulty                 |                    Yes | S-8 ruled                             | 🟡 Adaptation/interface |                       No |
| Combat                     |                    Yes | Partial architecture                  | 🔴 Design/integration   |                  **Yes** |
| Attack resolution          |                    Yes | Effects + contests exist              | 🔴 Combat dependency    |                  **Yes** |
| Defence                    |                    Yes | S-6 ruled                             | 🟢 Alpha-ready          |                       No |
| Damage                     |                    Yes | S-4 direction                         | 🟡 Integration          |                  **Yes** |
| Armour                     |                    Yes | S-5 ruled                             | 🟡 Content/interface    |               No/Partial |
| Hit location               |                    Yes | S-2/S-4/S-5 architecture              | 🟡 Integration          |                  Partial |
| Falling                    |                    Yes | Core + hazard direction               | 🟡 Integration          |                       No |
| Fatigue/exhaustion         |                    Yes | PE economy                            | 🟡 Adaptation           |                  Partial |
| Healing                    |                    Yes | S-11 ruled                            | 🟢 Alpha-ready          |                       No |
| Incapacitation/death       |                    Yes | S-7 ruled                             | 🟢 Alpha-ready          |                       No |
| Fear/Fright                |                    Yes | No complete system                    | 🔴 Missing              | **Yes for complete run** |
| Grappling                  |                    Yes | No complete system                    | 🔴 Missing              |                  **Yes** |
| Time/action economy        |                    Yes | Not complete                          | 🔴 Design-stage         |                  **Yes** |
| Equipment                  |                    Yes | Architecture only                     | 🔴 Design-stage         |                  **Yes** |
| Encumbrance                |                    Yes | Not complete                          | 🔴 Design-stage         |              Partial/Yes |
| Ranged combat              |                    Yes | Combat dependency                     | 🔴 Design-stage         |                  Partial |
| Magic                      |                    Yes | Architecture only                     | 🔴 Design-stage         |        **Yes for Enfys** |
| Creature construction      |                    Yes | S-12 architecture                     | 🟡 Content-authoring    |                  **Yes** |
| Creature special abilities |                    Yes | Incomplete grammar                    | 🔴 Design-stage         |                  **Yes** |
| Rewards / XP               |                    Yes | General XP                            | 🟢 Playtestable         |                       No |
| Money/economy              |                    Yes | Not developed                         | 🔴 Missing              |                       No |
| Adventure state flags      |                    Yes | Not a universal subsystem requirement | 🟡 Adaptation content   |                       No |
| Tactical map               |                    Yes | Requires combat/time/movement         | 🔴 Dependency           |                  **Yes** |
| Character generation       |                    Yes | Core maths                            | 🟡 Procedure/content    | Yes for fresh characters |

---

# 25. What the Adventure Actually Proves About Tiwas

## 25.1 The Core is not the problem

This benchmark strongly validates the value of the Tiwas Core architecture.

A large proportion of the adventure's apparently disparate GURPS tests reduce cleanly to:

> **ordinary Tiwas Core Tests.**

That includes climbing, searching, tracking, lockpicking, forced entry, survival, first aid, stealth, tactics, Will and DX-type tests.

This supports the architectural decision not to reproduce GURPS's numerous special-purpose resolution procedures.

---

## 25.2 S-1 is immediately useful

The adventure contains a genuine opposed contest:

> Stealth vs Perception.

That maps directly into S-1 without requiring a new contest engine.

This makes *Beyond the Vale of Madness* a practical S-1 playtest case rather than merely a theoretical validation case.

---

## 25.3 S-6 is now demonstrably relevant

The adventure's combat encounters require defensive response.

The new Active Defense ruling therefore directly services a real published adventure requirement. The defender's ability to decline defence is also compatible with the adventure's explicit All-Out Attack/no-defense concept at the architectural level, although the GURPS procedure itself should not be copied. 

---

# 26. The Most Important Gaps Exposed

## Priority 1 — Combat Integration

This is the dominant blocker.

The adventure cannot be fully played without:

* attack procedure;
* combat sequencing;
* movement/action timing;
* attack Skill/content;
* Effect application in combat;
* damage;
* grappling;
* ongoing effects.

---

## Priority 2 — Time / Action Economy

The adventure explicitly contains:

* tactical combat;
* seconds-long effects;
* minutes-long regeneration;
* spell durations;
* movement;
* prolonged activity.

Therefore Time is not merely a future convenience.

**This adventure demonstrates that Time is an actual playtest dependency.**

---

## Priority 3 — Equipment

The adventure assumes equipment is mechanically meaningful.

The player must know:

* what equipment exists;
* what it weighs;
* what it permits;
* what armour protects;
* what weapons do;
* what equipment can be used for climbing;
* how carrying capacity works.

The adventure therefore provides a concrete justification for completing the Equipment interface.

---

## Priority 4 — Grappling / Conditions

The Blood Man is particularly diagnostic.

Its intended combat loop is:

**Attack → Wound → Grapple → ongoing Blood Seep → possible death.**

That cannot be represented adequately with only generic HP damage.

This is precisely the kind of compound interaction that exposes whether Tiwas's universal subsystem architecture is actually complete.

---

## Priority 5 — Magic / Special Abilities

Enfys makes this unavoidable.

The adventure contains a complete spellcasting character rather than merely a magical treasure.

Therefore Tiwas needs a usable setting-level Special Ability/Magic interface before that character can be used without substantial substitution.

---

## Priority 6 — Fear / Mental Conditions

The adventure has explicit Fright Checks.

That makes fear a concrete test case for:

* Mind-domain resolution;
* Conditions;
* mental consequences.

It should not be allowed to remain hidden as an undefined “future condition system” if this adventure becomes a Tiwas benchmark.

---

# 27. Development Dependency Graph Exposed by the Adventure

The adventure suggests the following practical dependency chain:

```text
CORE TEST
    │
    ├── ordinary tests ──────────────── READY
    │
    ├── S-1 opposed contests ───────── READY
    │
    └── Difficulty ─────────────────── READY / INTERFACE
             │
             ▼
        COMBAT CORE
             │
      ┌──────┼─────────┐
      ▼      ▼         ▼
   Effects  Defence   Damage
      │      │         │
      │      │       Wounds
      │      │         │
      └──────┼─────────┘
             │
             ▼
       Grapple / Conditions
             │
             ▼
       Time / Action Economy
             │
      ┌──────┼──────────┐
      ▼      ▼          ▼
 Equipment  Magic     NPCs
      │      │          │
      └──────┼──────────┘
             ▼
      Full Adventure Run
```

This is not a proposal to change Tiwas architecture. It is the **dependency structure exposed by this particular benchmark**.

---

# 28. Readiness Verdict

## 28.1 Can *Beyond the Vale of Madness* be playtested in Tiwas now?

### **Not as a fully self-contained, mechanically complete Tiwas conversion.**

But:

### **Yes, as a staged Tiwas readiness playtest.**

This distinction is important.

The adventure already provides enough ordinary tests and opposed tests to exercise the Core and S-1.

The newly ruled S-4 through S-11 material also means substantial portions of the adventure can now serve as **alpha subsystem tests**.

What is not yet available is the complete integrated layer required to run the adventure from paragraph 1 through paragraph 60 without making significant mechanical substitutions or GM inventions.

---

# 29. The Benchmark's Most Valuable Finding

The experiment has produced a better development metric than a simple checklist of “Tiwas systems remaining.”

The useful metric is:

> **Can a real published adventure encounter be resolved without the GM inventing a mechanic?**

Applied here:

| Benchmark layer       | Result                            |
| --------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| Core resolution       | **Pass**                          |
| Ordinary tests        | **Pass**                          |
| Opposed tests         | **Pass**                          |
| Difficulty            | **Near-pass / interface work**    |
| Harm                  | **Partial**                       |
| Defence               | **Pass at alpha-rule level**      |
| Armour                | **Partial / content integration** |
| Healing               | **Pass at alpha-rule level**      |
| Incapacitation/death  | **Pass at alpha-rule level**      |
| Combat                | **Fail — incomplete integration** |
| Grappling             | **Fail**                          |
| Time/action economy   | **Fail**                          |
| Equipment/encumbrance | **Fail**                          |
| Magic                 | **Fail**                          |
| Fear/conditions       | **Fail**                          |
| NPC content           | **Partial / authoring required**  |
| Full adventure        | **Fail**                          |

---

# 30. Conclusions

## 30.1 Primary conclusion

**Beyond the Vale of Madness is a valid and useful Tiwas readiness benchmark.**

It is small enough to analyse completely, but mechanically broad enough to expose weaknesses that a simple Core-test exercise would miss.

## 30.2 What is already demonstrated

Tiwas has now demonstrated usable foundations for:

* universal d100 resolution;
* resource-costed testing;
* failure-driven progression;
* ordinary task tests;
* opposed contests;
* difficulty-side modification;
* location generation;
* Effects architecture;
* wounds/injury architecture;
* armour architecture;
* Active Defense;
* incapacitation/death;
* Extended Tests;
* healing.

The Alpha corpus explicitly records the current S-1 through S-11 table-play material and distinguishes it from Canonical status. 

## 30.3 What the benchmark says must still be developed

The highest-value remaining development targets exposed by this adventure are:

1. **Combat integration**
2. **Time / Action Economy**
3. **Equipment / Encumbrance**
4. **Grappling**
5. **Conditions**
6. **Fear / Fright**
7. **Magic / Special Abilities**
8. **NPC/creature content**
9. **Complete combat damage integration**
10. **Adventure-ready character-generation/content packages**

These are not arbitrary roadmap items. **This specific published adventure demonstrates why each is required.**

---

# 31. Final Readiness Statement

> **Tiwas has passed the “generic resolution engine” portion of the adventure benchmark, but has not yet passed the “complete RPG system” benchmark.**

The most significant result is that the benchmark has moved the project from:

> “Which RPG systems haven't we designed yet?”

toward the more useful question:

> **“Which real play situations still force a Tiwas GM to invent a rule?”**

For *Beyond the Vale of Madness*, the answer is now relatively precise:

**Combat, time/action sequencing, grappling/conditions, equipment/encumbrance, fear, magic, and complete NPC/creature implementation are the major remaining blockers.**

That makes this adventure suitable not merely as a future converted adventure, but as a **repeatable Tiwas development acceptance test**: when the remaining blockers have been resolved, the same adventure can be rerun and the benchmark can determine whether Tiwas has actually become capable of running a complete published scenario rather than merely possessing a large collection of individually designed subsystems.

---

# 32. Source and Authority Notes

### Primary sources

* *Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS*, J.C. Connors, v1.2. The adventure explicitly contains the numbered solo-adventure procedures, creature statistics, pregenerated characters, equipment, spells and reward structures. 
* `Tiwas-Alpha-Playtest-Corpus-2026-09-01.md`, which states that it is a one-off compilation of the currently Locked and Ruled material for alpha table use and does not itself alter authority. 

### Authority caution

The Alpha corpus contains **Non-canonical designer rulings** as well as Canonical/Locked rules. Those rulings are therefore treated in this report as **alpha-playtestable design**, not as promoted Canonical Tiwas rules. The corpus explicitly preserves that distinction. 

This distinction is important: **“playtestable now” does not mean “Canonical.”**

### Assumptions

1. All GURPS skill/attribute tests are replaced by the Tiwas Core Test, per your explicit instruction.
2. GURPS-specific statistics such as ST, DX, IQ, FP, Dodge, Parry, DR and GURPS damage dice are **requirements to be functionally replaced**, not Tiwas mechanics to be imported.
3. Adventure narrative, paragraph branching, named objects and setting material are not counted as Tiwas subsystem gaps unless they require a mechanical interface.
4. No attempt has been made to invent missing Tiwas mechanics merely to make the adventure playable.
5. The 1 September Alpha corpus is treated as the latest supplied working snapshot for the current design state, while preserving its Canonical/non-canonical distinction.

```

## btvmadness-adapt-report-chatgpt-5-6-2.md
> **LLM attribution:** ChatGPT 5.6 - report 2

```markdown
# Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit

## *Beyond the Vale of Madness* as a Practical Systems Benchmark

**Audit date:** 2 September 2026
**Primary adventure source:** *Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS*, v1.2
**Primary Tiwas source:** *Tiwas — Alpha-Playtest Corpus*, 1 September 2026
**Assessment basis:** supplied sources only

---

# 1. Executive Summary

## 1.1 Overall finding

**Tiwas is not yet capable of running *Beyond the Vale of Madness* as a mechanically complete adventure.**

However, the adventure is a **very useful readiness benchmark**, because a substantial portion of its non-combat resolution can already be expressed through the Tiwas Core Test.

The adventure repeatedly uses ordinary skill/attribute tests for:

* climbing;
* survival;
* searching;
* tracking;
* stealth;
* lockpicking;
* forced entry;
* perception;
* first aid/physician;
* merchant appraisal;
* physical coordination;
* willpower/fear;
* opposed stealth;
* combat;
* damage;
* healing;
* fatigue/exhaustion;
* armour;
* incapacitation/death;
* magic;
* equipment;
* NPC/creature statistics;
* tactical positioning.

Under the audit rule that all GURPS skill and attribute tests become **ordinary Tiwas Core Tests**, the Core itself is already capable of carrying a large portion of the adventure's resolution load.

The principal blockers are therefore **not the basic resolution engine**.

They are the unresolved or insufficiently complete Universal Play systems surrounding it.

### 1.2 Readiness result

| Area                             | Current assessment                                                                   |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Basic d100 resolution            | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| Ordinary skill/attribute tests   | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| Failure XP / advancement         | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| Resource expenditure             | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| S-1 opposed resolution           | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| Difficulty modifiers             | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| Third-party adjudication         | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| Extended Tests                   | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| S-2 Zero-Step provider           | **Playtestable Now**, where invoked                                                  |
| Non-attack location provenance   | **Playtestable Now**, subject to existing ruling                                     |
| Basic HP injury                  | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| S-4 localized wounds             | **Design-Stage Dependency** for this adventure                                       |
| S-5 armour                       | **Design-Stage Dependency / mapping dependent**                                      |
| S-6 Defense                      | **Design-Stage Dependency for practical combat use**                                 |
| S-7 incapacitation/death         | **Playtestable Now at rule level; NPC/content implementation remains incomplete**    |
| S-8 difficulty                   | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| S-9/S-10 Extended Tests          | **Playtestable Now**                                                                 |
| S-11 healing                     | **Playtestable Now at subsystem level; adventure-specific healing mapping required** |
| Equipment                        | **Missing Subsystem**                                                                |
| Encumbrance                      | **Missing Subsystem**                                                                |
| Conditions                       | **Missing/Design-Stage dependency**                                                  |
| Combat attack/effect mapping     | **Design-Stage / Missing content**                                                   |
| NPC/creature construction        | **Design-Stage Dependency**                                                          |
| Magic                            | **Missing Subsystem**                                                                |
| Tactical movement/action economy | **Missing Subsystem**                                                                |
| GURPS-specific damage model      | **Must be replaced by Tiwas mapping**                                                |
| Adventure branching/content      | **Adventure Content Only**                                                           |

The most important conclusion is:

> **The adventure does not demonstrate that Tiwas needs a better Core Test. It demonstrates that Tiwas needs its surrounding consequence, combat, equipment, condition, magic, and NPC/content layers completed.**

---

# 2. Audit Objective and Method

## 2.1 Objective

The adventure is treated as an **external functional benchmark**.

The question is not:

> "How do we convert GURPS into Tiwas?"

The question is:

> "What does this adventure require a game system to accomplish, and can current Tiwas accomplish that function?"

This distinction is critical.

The adventure uses GURPS terminology such as DX, ST, FP, Dodge, Parry, DR, damage dice, Fright Checks and spell levels. These are **not imported into Tiwas**.

They are treated as evidence of required functionality.

For example:

> GURPS "make a Climbing roll"

becomes:

> **Tiwas must be able to resolve a climbing task through the ordinary Core Test.**

The GURPS procedure itself has no normative authority over Tiwas.

---

## 2.2 Tiwas authority hierarchy

The Alpha-Playtest Corpus explicitly states that it is a dated table-ready assembly rather than a new source of authority. Canonical/Locked material remains authoritative, while the Part B subsystem material consists of recorded **non-canonical designer rulings**. 

This audit therefore distinguishes:

1. **Canonical / Locked**
2. **Ruled non-canonical**
3. **Open / not table-ready**
4. **Adventure-specific adaptation**
5. **Pure adventure content**

The existence of a subsystem name does **not** automatically establish readiness.

---

# 3. Source Adventure Mechanical Inventory

## 3.1 Resolution demands

The adventure contains numerous explicit tests.

| Adventure function       | Examples                                                |
| ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| Will test                | Removing mace; searching bloody room                    |
| DX/physical coordination | Lighting torch; climbing; jumping; slippery stairs      |
| Survival                 | Cold trek                                               |
| Strength/physical force  | Digging through snow; breaking door                     |
| Search                   | Courtyard, skeleton room                                |
| Tracking                 | Ice maze                                                |
| Stealth                  | Sneaking past troll                                     |
| Lockpicking              | Guardhouse                                              |
| Merchant/appraisal       | Tapestries                                              |
| First Aid / Physician    | Injured hunter; post-combat healing                     |
| Fright Check             | Blood creature; corpse                                  |
| Tactics                  | Tactical combat positioning                             |
| Combat attacks           | Troll and blood man                                     |
| Defence                  | GURPS Dodge/Parry                                       |
| Damage                   | Falling, weapons, monster attacks, environmental injury |
| Fatigue                  | Cold travel and digging                                 |
| Healing                  | First Aid, Physician, magical healing                   |
| Armour                   | DR and soft/hard armour interaction                     |
| Encumbrance              | Climbing penalties                                      |
| Equipment requirements   | ST, weapon skill, armour, carrying capacity             |
| Magic                    | Enfys's spell list                                      |
| NPC statistics           | Troll, blood man, pregenerated characters               |
| Tactical positioning     | Battle maps and starting positions                      |
| Death                    | Adventure-ending character death                        |
| Rewards                  | Treasure values and completion rewards                  |

The adventure explicitly permits tactical combat using provided battle maps. For example, the ice troll encounter defines starting positions, initiative-like sequencing and a tactical option. 

---

# 4. System-by-System Tiwas Compatibility Assessment

## 4.1 Core Resolution

### Requirement

The adventure expects ordinary attribute and skill tests.

Examples include DX, Will, Survival, Search, Tracking, Stealth, Lockpicking, Merchant, First Aid and Physician.

### Tiwas

The canonical Core Test already provides:

1. d100;
2. determine domain;
3. success/failure;
4. natural-roll resource cost;
5. Overflow;
6. Failure XP;
7. failed Double;
8. recovery;
9. end.

### Classification

**Playtestable Now**

This is one of the strongest findings of the audit.

The adventure's numerous simple tests are not blocked by Tiwas resolution architecture.

---

# 4.2 Skill Mapping

The adventure uses many GURPS skills that have no one-to-one Tiwas skill names.

Examples include:

* Climbing
* Survival (Mountains)
* Lockpicking
* Search
* Tracking
* Stealth
* First Aid
* Physician
* Merchant
* Tactics
* Forced Entry
* Brawling
* Axe/Mace
* Shield
* Staff
* Broadsword
* Innate Attack
* Thaumatology

The GURPS pregenerated characters demonstrate the breadth of the skill inventory.

### Tiwas implication

This does **not** automatically mean Tiwas lacks the mechanics.

The Tiwas system deliberately separates the **mechanical skill architecture** from adventure-specific skill/content authoring.

Therefore:

| Issue                                                 | Assessment                               |
| ----------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- |
| Can Tiwas resolve a skill test?                       | Yes                                      |
| Does Tiwas already contain every required skill name? | No                                       |
| Is that necessarily a missing subsystem?              | No                                       |
| What is required?                                     | Adventure-specific skill mapping/content |

### Classification

**Adaptation Mapping Required**

This distinction prevents an unnecessary expansion of Tiwas's universal skill system merely to reproduce GURPS's vocabulary.

---

# 4.3 Difficulty

The adventure repeatedly modifies tests:

* Lockpicking +2;
* Climbing −3;
* Tracking +3;
* Fright Check −2;
* Climbing penalties from encumbrance;
* various default-skill penalties.

For example, Lockpicking is explicitly +2, while the climbing wall is −3.

Tiwas S-8 now provides named difficulty tiers with fixed additive **Skill-side** modifiers, with symmetric bonuses and penalties. The modified Skill affects success/failure, Failure XP and the Skill Roll Pool cascade. 

### Classification

**Playtestable Now**

The remaining work is **mapping GURPS modifiers into Tiwas difficulty grades**, not designing a new resolution mechanic.

---

# 4.4 Opposed Tests

The adventure uses a Quick Contest of Stealth against the troll's Perception 12. 

Tiwas S-1 is specifically designed for this function.

Both participants perform ordinary Core Tests and the contest then resolves through the success/failure matrix and Quality where required. 

### Classification

**Playtestable Now**

This is an important validation point:

> *Beyond the Vale of Madness* provides a real adventure use case for S-1 rather than a hypothetical laboratory case.

---

# 4.5 Mutual Failure / Third-Party Adjudication

The adventure itself does not appear to make heavy explicit use of the specialized mutual-failure adjudication structure, but its general contest architecture can potentially generate the condition.

Tiwas has already ruled the generalized S-8 third-party adjudication procedure: same skill by default, ordinary Core Test, binary adjudicator outcome and inverted Failure-Margin comparison. 

### Classification

**Playtestable Now**

Not a major adventure blocker.

---

# 4.6 Extended Tests

The adventure contains activities that naturally function as repeated effort:

* digging through snow;
* breaking through frozen passages;
* climbing;
* prolonged survival;
* potentially repeated physical efforts.

The explicit adventure text often handles these as single tests or fixed resource deductions rather than formal GURPS Extended Tests.

Tiwas nevertheless already has S-9/S-10:

* successful interval → Margin;
* failures neutral;
* accumulated Margin never decreases;
* GM sets completion target.

### Classification

**Playtestable Now**

But individual adventure passages require **mapping decisions** about whether a particular activity remains a single Core Test or becomes an Extended Test.

That is adventure adaptation, not necessarily a Tiwas gap.

---

# 4.7 Location

This adventure is particularly useful because physical consequences occur repeatedly:

* falls;
* climbing failures;
* weapon wounds;
* environmental injuries;
* creature attacks.

Tiwas has the canonical Zero-Step Tier-1 provider. 

The non-canonical DEC-037 also provides a provenance rule for physical non-attack consequences: a failed governing physical-risk test can provide the Zero-Step source roll. 

However, the broader location subsystem remains dependent on DEC-041's location-tier and anatomical mapping rules.

DEC-041 establishes coarse Tier-1 zones and the Skill-Tier 2+ gate for location-referencing Effects. 

### Important consequence

A simple GURPS fall such as:

> "Take 2d-2 crushing damage"

cannot simply be declared to be a Tiwas located wound.

The adventure requires a **consequence mapping**.

### Classification

**Design-Stage Dependency** for location-dependent consequences.

The **Zero-Step mathematical provider itself is Playtestable Now**.

---

# 4.8 Injury / Wounds

The adventure uses two materially different forms of injury.

### A. Direct damage

Examples:

* falling;
* cuts;
* crushing damage;
* corrosive damage;
* monster attacks.

### B. Persistent injury consequences

The injured hunter has a broken leg and remains in a dangerous state. The blood man specifically requires its victim to become wounded before its Blood Seep ability can operate. 

Tiwas distinguishes Injury from Wound:

> Injury = HP damage
> Wound = localized, lasting numerical state.

The current S-4 ruling establishes Wounds as a selectable Effect and requires location infrastructure. 

But OPEN-007 wound consequences remain **not table-ready**. 

### Classification

**Design-Stage Dependency**

This is a genuine blocker for mechanically reproducing persistent localized injury.

---

# 4.9 Basic HP Damage

Tiwas already has:

`HP = Σ all Body attributes`

and Overflow directly applies HP damage. The S-3 base Effect is also explicitly **Inflict Injury (HP-only)**. 

Therefore the adventure's ordinary "take damage" functionality has a Tiwas home.

### But:

The adventure's actual damage quantities are GURPS dice expressions such as:

* `1d-1`;
* `1d+2`;
* `2d-2`;
* `1d-4`;
* `2d`;
* corrosive damage.

Those are not Tiwas mechanics.

### Classification

**Adaptation Mapping Required**

The underlying HP consequence exists; the damage-generation model does not.

This should **not** be interpreted as permission to import GURPS damage dice.

---

# 4.10 Armour

The adventure explicitly uses armour.

The ice troll has DR 2.
The blood man has DR 1.
The pregenerated characters have leather, mail and other armour.

Tiwas S-5 has ruled armour architecture, including armour Tags and interaction with Effects, but its practical content and mapping remain dependent on the current subsystem state. The corpus specifically identifies S-5 as a ruled subsystem and notes that Armor Tags and location interaction are involved. 

Crucially, Tiwas Overflow cannot be modified by armour.

### Classification

**Design-Stage Dependency / Adaptation Mapping Required**

Armour is definitely required by the adventure.

It therefore becomes a **real readiness dependency**, unlike some Tiwas subsystems that the adventure never meaningfully exercises.

---

# 4.11 Defense

This is one of the clearest blockers.

The adventure explicitly supplies:

* Dodge;
* Parry;
* All-Out Attack;
* "no defense" consequences;
* tactical combat.

For the ice troll, the adventure says its All-Out Attack grants two attacks **but no defense**. 

Tiwas S-6 is architecturally settled as Active Defense:

* defender rolls;
* Defense is voluntary;
* Defense is uncapped;
* it is a genuine Core Test;
* Effect first auto-applies;
* Defense then mitigates it;
* each Effect gets its own Defense roll.

### Critical distinction

**S-6 architecture is ruled.**

But the adventure needs practical combat implementation:

* what Advanced Skill is used?
* how do combat abilities interact with Defense?
* what Effect is being defended against?
* how do attacks map into S-3 Effects?
* what does "two attacks" mean mechanically?
* how does tactical combat interact with the action structure?

Those questions are not solved merely by having selected Active Defense as the architecture.

### Classification

**Design-Stage Dependency**

This is a **major blocker** to full combat playtesting.

---

# 4.12 Incapacitation and Death

The adventure explicitly terminates the adventure when the character dies or becomes unconscious. The ice troll encounter also distinguishes defeat, unconsciousness and death. 

Tiwas now has:

`HP = 0 → forced incapacitation`

and permanent loss occurs through the two-branch rule:

1. incapacitated + all revival attempts fail; or
2. voluntary permanent loss.



### Classification

**Playtestable Now — with an important procedural caveat**

The mechanical trigger exists.

The adventure-specific handling of incapacitated characters and monster death must still be mapped.

That is mostly **adaptation content**, not a reason to redesign S-7.

---

# 4.13 Healing

The adventure uses First Aid/Physician after the troll encounter.

The rule is:

* success → restore `1d-3 HP`;
* failure → restore 1 HP. 

Tiwas S-11 now requires healing during Rest to be an explicit Core Test and makes S-11 a literal Extended Test instance. 

### Important mismatch

The adventure's First Aid healing occurs immediately after combat.

Tiwas S-11 is specifically **Rest/Healing**.

Therefore one cannot simply declare the GURPS First Aid rule equivalent to S-11.

### Classification

**Adaptation Mapping Required**

The Tiwas healing architecture exists, but the adventure needs a mapping of:

> "First Aid immediately after injury"

into the Tiwas healing framework.

This should be treated as an adventure-specific adaptation question unless the desired answer exposes a general Tiwas healing gap.

---

# 4.14 Fatigue / Exertion

This adventure heavily relies on GURPS FP.

Examples include:

* losing FP during cold travel;
* losing FP while digging;
* reaching zero FP and collapsing;
* reaching zero FP and dying from cold.

Tiwas does **not** have a separate Fatigue Point resource.

Instead, every Core Test already costs its natural d100 roll from Physical Energy or MP. Overflow becomes HP damage. 

This is exactly the kind of mechanic that should **not** be imported merely because GURPS uses it.

### Functional question

Can Tiwas represent:

> "The character is expending physical resources through sustained exertion"?

**Yes.**

Can current Tiwas reproduce:

> "subtract 1 FP as a separate resource independent of a test"?

**No.**

### Classification

**Adaptation Mapping Required**

This is **not necessarily a missing Tiwas subsystem**.

The adventure can be tested by determining which exertion events should be represented by:

* ordinary Core Tests;
* Physical Energy expenditure;
* Overflow;
* Extended Tests.

That mapping remains to be authored.

---

# 4.15 Encumbrance

This is a genuine gap.

The adventure explicitly states:

> Climbing rolls are penalized by encumbrance level.



The pregenerated characters also list:

* body armour;
* weapons;
* backpacks;
* ropes;
* food;
* weight;
* carrying capacities.



Tiwas's reserved systems explicitly include **equipment and encumbrance**. 

### Classification

**Missing Subsystem**

This is a concrete blocker because the adventure mechanically invokes encumbrance rather than merely mentioning equipment.

---

# 4.16 Equipment

Equipment is pervasive:

* swords;
* mace;
* dagger;
* shield;
* armour;
* rope;
* grapple;
* pitons;
* torches;
* tinderbox;
* crossbow;
* bolts;
* backpack;
* clothing;
* magical items.

The adventure also has explicit requirements such as:

> mace requires Axe/Mace skill and ST 12;

and a crossbow provides one shot because only one bolt is found.

The adventure's treasure list contains 13 distinct items with values. 

### Classification

**Missing Subsystem**

There is no sufficiently complete Tiwas equipment system in the current corpus.

This is distinct from merely having to author adventure treasure.

---

# 4.17 Conditions

The adventure contains functional conditions even where GURPS expresses them through traits or consequences:

* wounded;
* unconscious;
* exhausted;
* darkness;
* fear;
* being grappled;
* being unable to continue;
* cold/hypothermia;
* impaired function.

The Tiwas corpus explicitly places conditions outside the locked Core and S-3 has only a structural Condition tier whose detailed contents remain incomplete.

### Classification

**Missing / Design-Stage Dependency**

This is significant because the adventure's combat and environmental encounters cannot be represented purely as HP loss.

---

# 4.18 Fear / Fright

The adventure explicitly requires Fright Checks:

* against the blood man;
* against the corpse;
* Will modified by Fearlessness in another scene.

Tiwas can certainly perform a **Will/mental Core Test**.

But there is no sufficiently complete canonical/rule-ready fear-condition subsystem establishing what failure means.

### Classification

**Design-Stage Dependency**

The test itself is available.

The **consequence model** is not.

---

# 4.19 Combat

Combat is the largest functional dependency cluster.

The adventure requires:

* attacks;
* multiple attacks;
* defence;
* tactical positioning;
* grappling;
* wounds;
* damage;
* armour;
* special attacks;
* movement;
* retreat;
* incapacitation;
* death.

The ice troll encounter alone explicitly supports either freeform combat or tactical combat using a map, with positional starting conditions and special behaviour. 

The blood man adds:

* claws;
* grappling;
* a wounded prerequisite;
* continuous Blood Seep;
* corrosive damage;
* large-area injury;
* absorption of dead victims.



### Classification

**Design-Stage Dependency**

Combat cannot currently be considered fully playtestable simply because S-1 and S-6 exist.

The combat stack needs:

**Core Test → S-1 → Effect → Location → Armour → Defense → Damage/Wound → Conditions → Incapacitation**

with the remaining pieces actually table-ready.

---

# 4.20 Tactical Movement / Action Economy

The adventure's battle maps require meaningful positional play.

The ice troll starts at a defined location; the character starts at another; an ambush changes starting position and who moves first. 

The blood man encounter likewise uses starting-position consequences based on Tactics. 

Tiwas does not currently provide a sufficiently complete universal tactical action economy.

### Classification

**Missing Subsystem**

This is independent of Defense.

S-6 answers:

> "How is an incoming Effect defended against?"

It does **not** by itself answer:

> "Who acts when, how far do they move, how many attacks/actions occur, and what does tactical position mechanically do?"

---

# 4.21 NPC / Creature Construction

The adventure contains explicit creature stat blocks.

### Ice Troll

Includes:

* ST;
* HP;
* Speed;
* DX;
* Will;
* IQ;
* Per;
* HT;
* FP;
* DR;
* Dodge;
* attacks;
* traits;
* regeneration;
* regrowth;
* skills.



### Blood Man

Includes:

* attributes;
* HP;
* Speed;
* DR;
* Dodge;
* Parry;
* attacks;
* special ability;
* traits;
* skills.



Tiwas S-12 explicitly remains an **open content-authoring track**. Its ruling establishes different automated and tabletop approaches, but actual creature templates are still to be developed through Tiwa-led playtesting. 

### Classification

**Design-Stage Dependency**

This is a significant blocker for running the actual adventure.

---

# 4.22 Magic

This adventure includes a pregenerated pyromancer with:

* Magery;
* Fireball;
* Flame Jet;
* Apportation;
* Create Fire;
* Dark Vision;
* Extinguish Fire;
* Fireproof;
* Flaming Weapon;
* Heat;
* Ignite Fire;
* Keen Vision;
* Lend Energy;
* Lend Vitality;
* Light;
* Minor Healing;
* Night Vision;
* Sense Danger;
* Sense Emotion;
* Sense Foes;
* Shape Fire;
* Warmth;
* Recover Energy.

The adventure therefore does not merely mention magic. It contains an actual playable magic-enabled pregenerated character.

Tiwas explicitly lists magic/special-ability implementation as reserved. 

### Classification

**Missing Subsystem**

This is one of the clearest hard blockers to supporting the supplied Enfys character as written.

It does **not** mean the adventure requires Tiwas magic to play if the non-magical pregenerated character is used.

But the audit requirement is to inspect the adventure's actual mechanical demands rather than its shortest path.

Therefore Magic must be recorded as a genuine dependency.

---

# 4.23 Traits / Special Abilities

The creature and pregenerated character sheets use traits such as:

* regeneration;
* regrowth;
* Combat Reflexes;
* Injury Tolerance;
* Night Vision;
* Magery;
* Fearlessness;
* Luck;
* racial abilities;
* special weaknesses;
* magical abilities.

The Tiwas corpus does not yet contain a sufficiently complete universal special-ability implementation.

### Classification

**Missing / Design-Stage Dependency**

This becomes particularly important for creatures.

---

# 4.24 Rewards / Advancement

The adventure awards:

* treasure;
* silver values;
* a $500 reward for rescuing the warrior;
* completion rewards;
* additional character awards for defeating monsters and finding sufficient loot.



Tiwas already has General XP and explicit GM/quest/adventure awards. 

The monetary/economic system itself is not currently a Core system.

### Classification

**Adaptation Mapping Required**

The advancement mechanism exists.

The exact adventure reward schedule is content.

---

# 5. Playtestability Matrix

## 5.1 Primary matrix
