6256da3d7)

```markdown
A Tiwas playtest requires a standard way to represent:

- Attributes.
- Skills.
- HP and MP.
- Physical Energy.
- Movement.
- Attack and defence capabilities.
- Damage.
- Conditions.
- Special abilities.
- Behaviour and tactics.

**Readiness:** Design-stage dependency unless an established adversary-construction system exists outside the supplied corpus.

***

## 6.2 Medium-priority dependencies

| Dependency | Reason |
|---|---|
| Difficulty and penalty framework | The adventure uses DX-5 and encumbrance penalties. A universal modifier policy is needed for consistent tests. |
| Initiative and ambush | The adventure changes who acts first and how close the combatants begin. |
| Light and visibility | Torch use and darkness affect exploration, but no complete light procedure is evidenced. |
| Falling and environmental damage | The adventure requires damage from failed climbs and physical hazards. |
| Fear and horror conditions | Basic Will tests work, but persistent mental effects need formal conditions. |
| Recovery and healing | The character can suffer damage and fatigue across multiple numbered paragraphs. |
| Range and movement | Required for the tactical map and for escaping or approaching creatures. |
| Resource persistence across paragraphs | The solo adventure expects equipment, health, fatigue, and special states to persist between entries. |

***

# 7. Systems That Are Already Usable

The following systems can be tested immediately without creating a new Tiwas subsystem:

| System | Current use in this adventure |
|---|---|
| d100 roll-under resolution | Resolve ordinary tests. |
| Standard mental and physical tests | Replace GURPS Will, DX, ST, and similar checks. |
| Tiwas attributes and skill framework | Define the basis of character actions, subject to mapping decisions. |
| Mental and physical resource costs | Apply the Core Test Transaction to standard tests. |
| Basic exploration decisions | Run the adventure’s numbered branching structure. |
| Failed hazard-test provenance | Use DEC-037 for failed climbs, traps, and similar physical risks. |
| Basic opposed contests | Replace selected opposed GURPS interactions where the outcome is contest-based. |
| Narrative fear responses | Let failed mental tests direct the player to retreat or abandon an option without needing a full panic subsystem. |
| Adventure-specific equipment discovery | Record discovered items, while postponing exact Tiwas statistics where necessary. |

The most defensible immediate playtest is therefore a **rules-light exploration playtest** in which combat, damage, and fatigue are deliberately marked as provisional.

***

# 8. Recommended Playtest Scope

## 8.1 Playtestable now

A first Tiwas test should include:

1. Character creation using the available Tiwas attributes and skills.
2. The opening travel and castle approach.
3. Standard tests for climbing, perception, will/resolution, and physical exertion.
4. Item discovery and inventory tracking.
5. Hazard-test failure handling using the Tiwas Location Index procedure.
6. Narrative choices and branching outcomes.
7. A deliberately simplified creature encounter, if combat is not the primary test objective.

This would measure whether the core Tiwas test transaction is understandable, sustainable, and useful in an adventure context.

## 8.2 Playtestable with substitutions

The following can be included if the substitutions are documented before play:

- GURPS Will → Tiwas Willpower, Resolve, or Composure.
- GURPS DX → Tiwas Agility, Reflexes, Quickness, or Grace.
- GURPS ST → Tiwas Might or Brawn.
- GURPS FP → Tiwas Physical Energy.
- GURPS Climbing → a standard Tiwas Climbing test.
- GURPS opposed combat or resistance → S-1 opposed contest.
- GURPS fearlessness → a Tiwas mental skill or temporary situational modifier.
- GURPS falling damage → a provisional hazard damage value.
- GURPS weapon damage → temporary Tiwas weapon entries.

These substitutions would be valid for alpha observation, but their results should not be treated as evidence that the underlying subsystems are complete.

## 8.3 Defer until system completion

Do not use the adventure as a full-system readiness test until the following are resolved:

- Tactical combat.
- Damage and armour.
- Wounds and conditions.
- Fatigue/Physical Energy depletion.
- Equipment and encumbrance.
- Creature statistics.
- Initiative and movement.
- Complete S-3 effect contents.
- Death, unconsciousness, and recovery.

***

# 9. Development Backlog

## Priority 1 — Required for a complete module playtest

| ID | Work item | Completion criterion |
|---|---|---|
| TTRPG-BTV-001 | Define standard combat exchange | A player can resolve an attack, defence, movement, and combat result without GM invention. |
| TTRPG-BTV-002 | Define damage and injury | Crushing, cutting, falling, armour, HP loss, wounds, unconsciousness, and death are operational. |
| TTRPG-BTV-003 | Define Physical Energy/fatigue | A fixed interpretation of GURPS FP replacement handles exertion and zero-resource collapse. |
| TTRPG-BTV-004 | Define equipment model | Weapons, shields, armour, torches, weight, and encumbrance have usable statistics. |
| TTRPG-BTV-005 | Define adversary construction | The ice troll and similar creatures can be represented consistently. |
| TTRPG-BTV-006 | Complete relevant Conditions | Wounded, exhausted, frightened, unconscious, and incapacitated states have procedures. |

## Priority 2 — Required for consistent results

| ID | Work item | Completion criterion |
|---|---|---|
| TTRPG-BTV-007 | Lock difficulty modifiers | Penalties such as DX-5 and encumbrance penalties have a Tiwas-standard expression. |
| TTRPG-BTV-008 | Define initiative and ambush | Starting order, surprise, and ambush are resolved consistently. |
| TTRPG-BTV-009 | Define movement and range | Tactical positions, adjacency, pursuit, and escape are operational. |
| TTRPG-BTV-010 | Define light and visibility | Torch use, darkness, and cave visibility have consistent effects. |
| TTRPG-BTV-011 | Enumerate S-3 effect tiers | Position, Condition, Equipment, Defense, and Location effects are fully listed. |

## Priority 3 — Useful refinement

| ID | Work item | Completion criterion |
|---|---|---|
| TTRPG-BTV-012 | Define fear/horror procedure | Repeated or severe supernatural encounters produce consistent mental consequences. |
| TTRPG-BTV-013 | Define healing and recovery | Damage and resource loss persist and recover according to explicit procedures. |
| TTRPG-BTV-014 | Define solo-adventure state tracking | Conditions, inventory, resources, and flags such as AMBUSHED can be recorded consistently. |
| TTRPG-BTV-015 | Create a Tiwas adventure-adaptation worksheet | Future modules can be audited using the same readiness categories. |

***

# 10. Final Readiness Statement

## Current conclusion

The adventure demonstrates that Tiwas already has a viable **general resolution foundation**. Its d100 roll-under system, attribute matrix, skills, resource transaction, opposed-contest structure, and hazard-location ruling are sufficient to begin a constrained exploratory playtest. 

However, *Beyond the Vale of Madness* also exposes the difference between having a core resolution engine and having a complete playable role-playing game. The adventure expects combat, damage, fatigue, equipment, encumbrance, conditions, creatures, movement, and death to work together. Those dependencies are not all established as complete Tiwas systems in the supplied corpus.

### Readiness rating

| Area | Rating |
|---|---|
| Ordinary tests | **Playtestable now** |
| Exploration and branching choices | **Playtestable now** |
| Basic mental tests | **Playtestable now** |
| Hazard-test resolution | **Playtestable with substitution** |
| Location-index consequences | **Playtestable with substitution** |
| Opposed contests | **Playtestable with substitution** |
| Combat | **Design-stage dependency** |
| Tactical combat | **Design-stage dependency** |
| Damage and injury | **Design-stage dependency** |
| Fatigue and exhaustion | **Design-stage dependency** |
| Equipment and encumbrance | **Design-stage dependency / possible missing subsystem** |
| Conditions | **Design-stage dependency** |
| Creature construction | **Design-stage dependency** |
| Death and recovery | **Missing or design-stage subsystem** |
| Adventure structure | **Adventure-specific content** |

> **Overall result:** Tiwas is ready for a **restricted exploration-and-resolution playtest** using this adventure, but not yet ready for a **complete mechanical playtest** of the adventure. The first development milestone should be a unified combat–damage–conditions–resource package, followed by equipment, encumbrance, and adversary construction.

***

# 11. Assumptions and Limitations

1. I treated the user-provided answers to the five interview questions as the authoritative scope definition.
2. I treated all ordinary GURPS ability checks as standard Tiwas skill tests, as requested.
3. I did not assume that the Alpha Playtest Corpus itself is canonical; its own status says it is a dated, one-off compilation and not a ruling artifact. 
4. Where the corpus identified a ruled subsystem but the available extract did not provide all operational details, I classified the dependency conservatively rather than promoting it to “playtestable now.”
5. The adventure’s exact page-level inventory may require a second document pass for a publication-grade traceability table covering every numbered paragraph. The present report identifies the mechanically significant requirements evidenced in the supplied extraction, but should be followed by paragraph-by-paragraph verification before being treated as the final audit baseline.
6. The proposed mappings from GURPS ST, DX, Will, FP, and Fearlessness to specific Tiwas statistics are provisional design assumptions, not settled Tiwas rulings.
```

## btvmadness-adapt-report-perplexity-2.md
> **LLM attribution:** Perplexity - report 2

```markdown
# Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit

## Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS

**Assessment date:** 1 September 2026  
**Assessment subject:** *Beyond the Vale of Madness*  
**Tiwas baseline:** *Tiwas—Alpha-Playtest-Corpus-2026-09-01.md*, version 1.0  
**Assessment purpose:** Determine which Tiwas systems can currently support playtesting and which systems must be completed before the adventure can function as a complete Tiwas benchmark.

***

# 1. Executive Summary

*Beyond the Vale of Madness* is a suitable practical benchmark for Tiwas because it exercises more than ordinary skill resolution. Although it is a relatively short solo adventure, it assumes systems for character capability, exploration, environmental hazards, climbing, fatigue, equipment, encumbrance, fear, combat, tactical positioning, damage, injury, conditions, creatures, and death. [Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS](Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf)

Tiwas can already support the adventure’s **basic resolution layer**. Ordinary GURPS skill and attribute rolls can be replaced by the Tiwas Core Test without creating a second resolution engine. Tiwas also has usable foundations for attributes, skills, physical and mental resources, opposed contests, and hazard-related Location Index generation. 

However, Tiwas is not yet ready for a complete mechanical playtest of the adventure without temporary rulings. The principal blockers are:

- Combat procedure.
- Tactical movement and action order.
- Damage, armour, injury, and death.
- Fatigue and Physical Energy interpretation.
- Equipment, weapons, shields, and encumbrance.
- Creature construction.
- Conditions.
- Complete S-3 effect contents.

### Overall finding

> **Tiwas is Playtestable Now for a restricted exploration-and-resolution test, and Playtestable with Adaptation Mapping for several hazard and mental-resolution functions. It is not yet fully playtestable as a complete mechanical adaptation because the adventure exposes unresolved combat, injury, fatigue, equipment, and adversary systems.**

The adventure therefore provides a useful development diagnostic, but the result is not a binary “ready/not ready” outcome. Tiwas has a functioning core test architecture while still lacking several interconnected procedures required for a complete adventure.

***

# 2. Audit Objective and Method

## 2.1 Objective

This audit answers:

> Which systems required by *Beyond the Vale of Madness* are currently available in Tiwas, which can be supported through adaptation mapping, and which unresolved Tiwas systems prevent a complete playtest?

The purpose is not to reproduce GURPS procedures. The assessment instead asks:

1. What mechanical function does the adventure require?
2. Does Tiwas already provide that function?
3. If so, is the Tiwas mechanic authoritative and operational?
4. If not, is the problem an adventure-specific mapping task or a Tiwas design gap?

## 2.2 Source authority

The adventure PDF is treated as authoritative for what the adventure uses or assumes. The Tiwas Alpha Playtest Corpus is treated as authoritative for the dated baseline supplied for this assessment.

The corpus explicitly states that it is a one-off, table-ready compilation rather than a new canonical rules document. It preserves the distinction between:

- Canonical / Locked rules.
- Non-canonical designer rulings.
- Draft-pending material.
- Open items.
- Confirmed-closed items.

Only the first two categories are presented as table-ready mechanics in the corpus. 

## 2.3 Readiness classifications

| Classification | Definition |
|---|---|
| **Playtestable Now** | Tiwas already provides sufficient operational mechanics. |
| **Adaptation Mapping Required** | The underlying Tiwas function exists, but an adventure-specific mapping, statistic, or content decision must be authored. |
| **Design-Stage Dependency** | Tiwas has a recognised direction or subsystem, but it is not sufficiently complete or authoritative for dependable use. |
| **Missing Subsystem** | No adequate Tiwas procedure is currently established. |
| **Adventure Content Only** | Fictional, structural, or module-specific material rather than a Tiwas system. |

A result may be “playtestable” in a deliberately provisional alpha test while still being unsuitable as evidence that the underlying subsystem is complete.

***

# 3. Source Adventure Mechanical Inventory

## 3.1 Adventure structure

The adventure is a solo, numbered-paragraph “Choose Your Path” scenario. The player reads a paragraph, makes choices, records discovered bold-faced items, and proceeds to another numbered entry. It is presented as an introductory GURPS Fantasy or Dungeon Fantasy adventure and assumes either a 150-point fantasy character or one of two pregenerated characters. [Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS](Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf)

The following are **adventure procedures or content**, not Tiwas subsystems:

- Numbered paragraph navigation.
- Branching choices.
- Castle, caves, snow, and supernatural setting.
- Enfys and other named characters.
- The Castle of Madness.
- The ice troll and other fictional entities as named inhabitants.
- The adventure’s specific treasure and item placements.
- Flags such as writing “AMBUSHED” on the character sheet.
- The module’s paragraph references and alternate routes.

These still require adaptation work, but they do not indicate missing Tiwas rules by themselves.

## 3.2 Mechanical requirements identified

| Requirement | Adventure use |
|---|---|
| Character capability | The adventure assumes a fantasy character with attributes, skills, equipment, health, fatigue, and combat capability. |
| Ordinary tests | Will, DX, ST, Climbing, and other checks are explicitly called for. |
| Difficulty modifiers | Examples include DX-5 and encumbrance-based penalties. |
| Exploration | The character searches ruins, caves, doors, tunnels, weapons, and concealed areas. |
| Perception | The character notices routes, creatures, openings, unusual objects, and environmental details. |
| Climbing | The character climbs a dangerous cliff or castle approach. |
| Falling | Failed climbing may cause crushing damage. |
| Environmental exposure | Snow, freezing winds, darkness, and extreme cold are persistent threats. |
| Physical exertion | Digging and movement reduce fatigue. |
| Fatigue depletion | The adventure subtracts FP and states that reaching zero causes collapse. |
| Equipment | Torches, cloaks, weapons, shields, armour, and recovered objects matter. |
| Encumbrance | Encumbrance modifies climbing. |
| Weapon skills | The mace requires Axe/Mace skill. |
| Weapon statistics | The mace has damage, a strength requirement, and weight. |
| Combat | The player may attack creatures, including an ice troll. |
| Tactical combat | The adventure provides positions, adjacency, a map, and tactical alternatives. |
| Action order | Ambush conditions affect who moves first. |
| Damage | Crushing and cutting damage are used, including falling damage. |
| Armour | Soft armour and shield/equipment protection are referenced. |
| Fear and mental resistance | A Will roll modified by Fearlessness may be required. |
| Opposed interaction | Creature and hazard encounters may require opposed resolution. |
| Conditions | Fear, exhaustion, injury, collapse, and possible incapacitation are represented. |
| Death | Severe injury or collapse can end the adventure. |
| Recovery | Persistent HP and FP consequences must be tracked between paragraphs. |
| Creature statistics | The ice troll must be represented as an opponent. |
| Magic | The adventure supports a pregenerated character using GURPS Magic, creating a possible magic-system dependency. |

The PDF evidence directly shows Will, DX, Climbing, ST, falling damage, fatigue-point depletion, weapon statistics, tactical combat, and the ice-troll encounter. [Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS](Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf)

***

# 4. System-by-System Tiwas Compatibility Assessment

## 4.1 Core resolution and character capability

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Ordinary skill rolls | Tiwas uses a d100 roll-under test. A test succeeds when the roll is at or below Skill, with a universal roll of 100 failure. | Canonical / Locked, DEC-001 | **Playtestable Now** | Low | Map each adventure action to a Tiwas skill. |
| Core test procedure | Tiwas requires the nine-step Core Test Transaction, including resource cost, overflow, failure XP, failed doubles, and recovery. | Canonical / Locked, DEC-006 and related core rules | **Playtestable Now** | Medium | Ensure every adapted test uses the Core Test rather than a GURPS substitute. |
| Physical and mental abilities | Tiwas has 24 Body and Mind attributes and associated Tier-1 skills such as Might, Agility, Reflexes, Quickness, Willpower, Perception, Resolve, and Composure. | Canonical / Locked, DEC-003 to DEC-005 | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Define the adventure’s mapping from ST, DX, Will, and Perception to Tiwas skills. |
| Untrained fallback | The adventure allows DX-5 when the character lacks Climbing. | GURPS adventure procedure; no confirmed Tiwas equivalent identified | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Medium | Establish whether untrained actions use a related Tiwas skill, attribute, or fixed penalty. |
| Difficulty modifiers | The adventure uses DX-5 and other penalties. | Adventure requirement; complete universal Tiwas modifier policy not established in available evidence | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Medium | Define how penalties modify the Core Test without violating the Core Test architecture. |
| Resource cost | Every Tiwas test costs the natural roll from the relevant resource domain. | Canonical / Locked | **Playtestable Now** | Low | Decide whether this cost applies unchanged to all mapped adventure checks. |

Tiwas’s core test is a strong compatibility point. The main unresolved issue is not resolution itself, but mapping GURPS attributes, untrained checks, and modifiers into the Tiwas architecture.

## 4.2 Exploration and environmental hazards

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Searching and noticing features | Standard tests can resolve investigation and perception. | Canonical core plus attribute/skill framework | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Low | Assign relevant Tiwas skills. |
| Hidden doors and traps | DEC-037 expressly covers environmental hazards, obstacles, and examples such as Spot Trap. | Non-canonical designer ruling, ruled | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Low | Create the specific trap or door content. |
| Climbing | A failed Climbing or physical-risk test can use the Tiwas Core Test. | Canonical core; hazard-location ruling | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Define the Tiwas Climbing skill and consequence severity. |
| Hazard failure provenance | A failed hazard test supplies the digits for Zero-Step Location Index generation. | Non-canonical designer ruling, DEC-037 | **Playtestable Now** for the ruled alpha baseline | Medium | Confirm use in the module’s specific failures. |
| Extended physical tests | DEC-039 states that the final roll of an Extended Test governs Location Index generation. | Non-canonical designer ruling, ruled | **Design-Stage Dependency** unless the adventure is treated as single-roll | Medium | Determine whether any adventure sequence qualifies as an Extended Test. |
| Extreme cold | Systemic threats such as extreme temperature bypass Location Index and apply direct HP/Conditions. | Non-canonical designer ruling, DEC-037 | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | High | Define exposure frequency, severity, and recovery. |
| Falling | The adventure applies \(1d-1\) crushing damage after a failed climb. | GURPS adventure procedure | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Establish Tiwas falling damage and physical-consequence procedure. |
| Darkness and torches | The adventure assumes torch use and limited cave visibility. | Adventure requirement; no complete Tiwas light subsystem established in evidence | **Missing Subsystem** or design-stage dependency | Medium | Define visibility, torch duration, and darkness effects if mechanically tested. |
| Navigation and route-finding | Narrative exploration and ordinary perception tests can support route choices. | Core resolution available | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Low | Author any required navigation tests. |

DEC-037 is especially relevant because it provides a direct mechanical bridge between failed environmental tests and physical consequences. It also explicitly treats extreme temperature as a systemic threat rather than a location-indexed injury. 

## 4.3 Fatigue, energy, and persistence

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Exertion costs | Tiwas has Physical Energy and Energy Regen. | Canonical / Locked, DEC-004 | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | High | Decide whether GURPS FP is replaced by Physical Energy. |
| Fixed fatigue loss | Digging subtracts 1 FP. | Adventure procedure | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define whether fixed exertion costs are permitted under the Core Test architecture. |
| Zero-resource collapse | The adventure states that reaching 0 FP causes collapse. | Adventure procedure; Tiwas zero-energy consequence not established in supplied evidence | **Missing Subsystem** | High | Define zero Physical Energy consequences. |
| Repeated resource tracking | The solo structure requires resources to persist between paragraphs. | Core resources exist, but adventure-state procedures are not fully specified | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Create a character-state tracking procedure. |
| Recovery | Tiwas includes Energy Regen and recovery as the final Core Test step. | Canonical / Locked | **Playtestable Now** for test-linked recovery | Medium | Define between-scene and injury recovery. |

The existence of Physical Energy does not automatically make GURPS Fatigue Points available in Tiwas. The replacement relationship must be explicitly decided.

## 4.4 Equipment, weapons, armour, and encumbrance

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Inventory | The adventure expects players to record discovered bold-faced items. | Adventure procedure | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Low | Use a Tiwas inventory record. |
| Weapon skill | The recovered mace requires Axe/Mace skill. | Adventure content plus GURPS rule | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define Tiwas weapon-skill categories. |
| Weapon damage | The mace deals \(sw+2\) crushing damage. | GURPS weapon statistic | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define Tiwas damage values and remove GURPS swing notation. |
| Strength requirement | Mace requires ST 12. | GURPS weapon statistic | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Map to a Tiwas physical-power requirement. |
| Weight | Mace weighs 5 lb. | GURPS equipment statistic | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Medium | Define Tiwas weight and carrying model. |
| Shields | A medium shield is found and used as equipment. | Adventure content; Tiwas defensive function not established in evidence | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define shield protection or defensive effects. |
| Armour | Falling damage references soft armour protection. | GURPS combat/equipment procedure | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define armour reduction or injury mitigation. |
| Encumbrance | Climbing rolls receive a penalty per encumbrance level. | GURPS procedure | **Missing Subsystem** or design-stage dependency | High | Establish encumbrance levels and their effects. |
| Torch | Torch provides light and heat in caves. | Adventure content with mechanical implications | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Define duration and visibility effects. |

This is one of the clearest areas where an ordinary Tiwas skill roll is insufficient. A standard test can determine whether the character attempts or uses an item successfully, but weapon damage, armour, weight, shields, and encumbrance require additional systems.

## 4.5 Combat and tactical interaction

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Attack a creature | Tiwas has standard tests and opposed contests, but no complete combat exchange is established in the available evidence. | Core canonical rules; S-1 ruled/locked core | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Define attack, defence, damage, and turn procedures. |
| Tactical combat | The adventure provides a tactical combat option, a map, starting positions, and adjacency. | Adventure requirement | **Missing Subsystem** or design-stage dependency | Critical | Define tactical movement, range, adjacency, actions, and positioning. |
| Initiative/action order | Ambush changes starting distance and allows the troll to move first. | Adventure-specific combat procedure | **Adaptation Mapping Required** if initiative exists; otherwise design dependency | High | Define initiative, surprise, and ambush. |
| Creature movement | The ice troll rushes toward the character. | Adventure requirement | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define Movement Speed use in combat. |
| Retreat and escape | The adventure permits fleeing, bypassing, or tactical withdrawal. | Narrative choices plus possible S-3 effects | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Define when retreat is automatic, tested, or opposed. |
| Opposed contests | Tiwas has S-1 opposed contests and a quality tie-breaker. | Canonical / Locked core with non-canonical designer ruling for Quality | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Decide which combat interactions are opposed contests. |
| Combat effects | S-3 provides a tiered effect structure, including injury, retreat, position, conditions, equipment, defence, and location concepts. | Non-canonical designer rulings; gated contents incomplete | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Enumerate effect contents and establish combat application. |
| Defence | Defense appears as a gated effect category, but a complete defence procedure is not demonstrated. | Non-canonical/ruling direction; incomplete contents | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Complete defence mechanics. |
| Grappling or close control | May be represented by opposed contests and condition/equipment effects. | Partial S-1/S-3 support | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Medium | Define applicable effects. |

The ice-troll encounter is the principal full-playtest blocker. The adventure does not merely ask whether the character succeeds at an attack; it assumes an integrated combat model with movement, positioning, attack order, damage, and creature behaviour. [Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS](Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf)

## 4.6 Damage, injury, conditions, and death

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Health tracking | Tiwas has HP derived from the twelve Body attributes. | Canonical / Locked, DEC-004 | **Playtestable Now** | Medium | Define how adventure damage reduces HP. |
| Crushing damage | Used for mace and falling. | Adventure/GURPS requirement | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Define Tiwas damage expression. |
| Cutting damage | Used by weapons or hazards. | Adventure/GURPS requirement | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define damage types and consequences. |
| Armour mitigation | Soft armour is relevant to falling damage. | GURPS procedure | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Define armour protection. |
| Location of injury | Tiwas has Zero-Step Location Index generation. | Canonical S-2 core plus DEC-037 ruling | **Playtestable Now** for location generation | Medium | Connect location output to injury effects. |
| Wounded condition | DEC-037 refers to Wounded as a possible hazard consequence. | Non-canonical designer ruling; condition framework incomplete in supplied evidence | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Define Wounded thresholds and effects. |
| Other conditions | The adventure needs exhaustion, fear, collapse, impairment, and possibly immobilisation. | S-3 includes Conditions conceptually, but gated contents are not fully enumerated. | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Critical | Enumerate and operationalise conditions. |
| Unconsciousness | Collapse at zero FP and severe injury may incapacitate the character. | Adventure requirement; no complete Tiwas threshold identified | **Missing Subsystem** | Critical | Define incapacitation and unconsciousness. |
| Death | The adventure can end in fatal injury or collapse. | Adventure requirement; no complete death procedure identified | **Missing Subsystem** | Critical | Define death, dying, and terminal consequences. |
| Healing and recovery | Persistent damage must be resolved across the adventure. | Partial resource recovery exists; injury recovery not established | **Design-Stage Dependency** | High | Define injury recovery and healing. |

Tiwas HP and Location Index provide important foundations, but they do not by themselves specify what happens when the character takes crushing or cutting damage.

## 4.7 Fear, horror, and mental effects

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Will test | The adventure explicitly calls for a Will roll. | Tiwas has Willpower, Resolve, Discipline, and other Mind skills. | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Low | Select the governing Tiwas skill. |
| Fearlessness modifier | GURPS Fearlessness modifies the Will roll. | No corresponding established Tiwas trait identified in evidence | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Replace with a Tiwas skill, condition, or temporary modifier. |
| Fear response | Failure may make the character abandon an investigation. | Narrative consequence can be handled through the module’s choices. | **Playtestable Now** for simple narrative consequences | Low | Define only if persistent fear is required. |
| Persistent fear condition | A complete condition system is not evidenced. | Conditions are a recognised but incompletely enumerated subsystem | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Medium | Define fear, panic, or avoidance conditions if used. |
| Mental resource cost | Mind tests draw from MP under the Core Test. | Canonical / Locked | **Playtestable Now** | Low | Apply consistently. |

The adventure can test basic mental resolution immediately, but it does not yet prove that Tiwas has a complete horror or fear subsystem.

## 4.8 Magic

| Adventure requirement | Tiwas capability | Authority/status | Classification | Blocking significance | Remaining work |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Optional magical pregenerated character | The adventure offers an advanced character using GURPS Magic. | Adventure appendix/content; Tiwas magic subsystem not established in available evidence | **Missing Subsystem** for the magical character | Low for non-magical route; High if magical character is used | Exclude the magical pregenerated character or define Tiwas magic first. |
| Supernatural atmosphere and creatures | The adventure contains supernatural fiction. | Adventure content | **Adventure Content Only** | None | Author creature/magic effects if required. |

The optional magical character should not be included in the first Tiwas benchmark unless the Tiwas magic system has separately reached playtest readiness.
```
