# 6. Missing / Design-Stage Dependencies

## 6.1 Critical blockers

### Combat resolution

The adventure’s combat cannot be evaluated using the Core Test alone. A complete combat procedure must specify:

- Attack declaration.
- Attack skill.
- Defence or opposing test.
- Turn/action order.
- Movement.
- Range and adjacency.
- Tactical positioning.
- Retreat.
- Damage application.
- Creature behaviour.
- Incapacitation.

**Status:** Design-stage dependency.

### Damage and injury

The adventure requires crushing, cutting, and falling damage, as well as armour interaction and minimum-damage handling. Tiwas currently exposes HP and Location Index foundations, but not a complete evidenced damage-to-injury procedure.

**Status:** Design-stage dependency.

### Conditions

The adventure needs at least Wounded, exhausted, frightened, unconscious, and dead or dying states. The corpus confirms a Conditions concept, but it also states that the contents of several gated S-3 tiers remain incompletely enumerated. 

**Status:** Design-stage dependency.

### Fatigue and Physical Energy

The adventure’s FP rules cannot be imported directly. A Tiwas-specific decision is needed regarding fixed exertion costs, zero-energy collapse, and recovery.

**Status:** Design-stage dependency.

### Equipment and encumbrance

The adventure uses weapon skill, weapon damage, strength requirements, weight, shields, armour, torches, and encumbrance penalties. These cannot be reduced to ordinary skill tests.

**Status:** Encumbrance is a missing or unresolved subsystem; equipment is a design-stage dependency.

### Creature construction

The ice troll requires a repeatable Tiwas representation. A one-off improvised stat block would permit a narrow demonstration but would not prove that adversary construction is ready.

**Status:** Design-stage dependency.

## 6.2 Non-blocking or low-blocking gaps

The following do not prevent a restricted exploration test:

- Torch duration.
- Detailed navigation.
- Exact fear-condition effects.
- Optional magic.
- Precise treasure statistics.
- Full tactical combat, provided the combat encounter is excluded or explicitly marked provisional.

***

# 7. Adventure Path Dependency Analysis

## 7.1 Opening approach and snow route

The opening route requires travel through cold, snow, darkness, and environmental danger. It may include perception, investigation, and Will-related choices.

**Tiwas result:** Mostly testable through the Core Test and Mind/Body skills. Extreme-temperature consequences and long-term fatigue remain unresolved.

**Readiness:** Restricted playtest possible.

## 7.2 Castle approach and climbing route

The cliff or castle approach requires Climbing, encumbrance penalties, and falling consequences. The adventure explicitly uses Climbing or DX-5 and applies falling damage on failure. [Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS](Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf)

**Tiwas result:** The test itself is compatible. Hazard-location provenance is supported by DEC-037. Falling damage, encumbrance, armour, and injury are not complete.

**Readiness:** Testable only with documented temporary substitutions.

## 7.3 Cave and tunnel route

The cave route uses torchlight, perception, hidden passages, equipment discovery, movement, and potential creature encounters.

**Tiwas result:** Exploration can be tested. Light, equipment, movement, and creature procedures require mapping or further design.

**Readiness:** Exploration route is testable; creature confrontation is not fully supported.

## 7.4 Ice-troll encounter

The ice-troll encounter is the strongest readiness test. It includes:

- Combat choice.
- Tactical combat.
- Starting positions.
- Adjacency.
- Ambush state.
- First movement.
- Creature tactics.
- Weapon attacks.
- Damage.
- Possible escape or death.

The adventure states that the troll rushes toward the character and provides different starting positions depending on whether the character has recorded “AMBUSHED.” [Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS](Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf)

**Tiwas result:** The encounter cannot currently function as a complete Tiwas combat benchmark without provisional combat, damage, creature, and condition rules.

**Readiness:** Not fully playtestable.

## 7.5 Optional magic-character route

The optional advanced character uses GURPS Magic.

**Tiwas result:** This tests a Tiwas magic system rather than the adventure’s general readiness. If magic is not already established in the Tiwas baseline, this route should be excluded from the first benchmark.

**Readiness:** Not required for the core adventure benchmark.

***

# 8. Tiwas Readiness Assessment

## 8.1 Readiness by architectural layer

| Layer | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Primary resolution architecture | Ready. The canonical d100 Core Test is sufficient for ordinary tests. |
| Attribute and skill foundation | Ready, subject to adventure-specific mapping. |
| Resource architecture | Partially ready. HP, MP, Physical Energy, and regeneration exist, but fatigue interpretation is unresolved. |
| Hazard resolution | Partially ready. The S-2/S-4 location framework provides a usable basis. |
| Opposed resolution | Partially ready. S-1 is available, but its use in combat requires mapping. |
| Effect resolution | Not fully ready. S-3 structure exists, but relevant gated contents remain incomplete. |
| Combat architecture | Not ready for complete adventure playtesting. |
| Damage and injury | Not ready. |
| Conditions | Not ready for dependable use. |
| Equipment and encumbrance | Not ready. |
| Adversary construction | Not demonstrated as ready. |
| Magic | Not required for the core benchmark and not evidenced as ready. |
| Adventure-state tracking | Adaptable but requires a practical worksheet or procedure. |

## 8.2 Quantitative interpretation

This audit should not be reduced to a simple percentage because the systems have unequal blocking significance. A missing torch-duration rule is not equivalent to a missing combat-and-injury engine.

A more useful interpretation is:

- **Core test readiness:** High.
- **Exploration readiness:** Moderate to high.
- **Hazard readiness:** Moderate.
- **Mental-test readiness:** Moderate to high.
- **Combat readiness:** Low.
- **Physical-consequence readiness:** Low.
- **Equipment/readiness:** Low.
- **Complete adventure readiness:** Low to moderate, depending on whether provisional systems are permitted.

***

# 9. Development Priorities Exposed by the Adventure

## Priority 1: Complete the combat consequence chain

The first priority should be a connected package rather than isolated rules:

1. Attack.
2. Defence.
3. Position and movement.
4. Damage.
5. Armour.
6. Injury.
7. Conditions.
8. Incapacitation.
9. Death and recovery.

This is the most important dependency because the ice-troll encounter exercises nearly all of these systems at once.

## Priority 2: Establish the physical resource model

Resolve:

- Whether Physical Energy replaces FP.
- Whether fixed costs such as “subtract 1 FP” are permitted.
- What zero Physical Energy does.
- How exertion differs from Core Test resource cost.
- How extreme temperature drains or affects resources.
- How energy recovers between scenes.

## Priority 3: Establish equipment and encumbrance

Create a minimum operational equipment layer covering:

- Weapons.
- Weapon skills.
- Damage values.
- Strength or Might requirements.
- Weight.
- Shields.
- Armour.
- Torches.
- Encumbrance levels.

## Priority 4: Complete conditions and S-3 effects

The adventure requires conditions even if the initial prototype uses only a small set. Define at minimum:

- Wounded.
- Exhausted.
- Frightened.
- Impaired.
- Unconscious.
- Dying/dead.
- Immobilised or restrained, if relevant to combat.

The corpus specifically warns that the structure of several S-3 gated effect tiers is ruled while their contents are not fully enumerated. 

## Priority 5: Define adversary construction

Use the ice troll as a test case for a general creature format, not merely as a one-off exception. The format should include:

- Attributes.
- Skills.
- HP and resources.
- Movement.
- Attack and defence.
- Damage.
- Conditions.
- Special abilities.
- Tactics.

## Priority 6: Create an adventure readiness worksheet

Future module audits should record each requirement using the same fields:

- Adventure page/paragraph.
- Mechanical function.
- Tiwas subsystem.
- Tiwas status.
- Readiness classification.
- Blocking significance.
- Adaptation decision.
- Playtest observation.

This will make future adventures comparable as readiness benchmarks.

***

# 10. Conclusions

*Beyond the Vale of Madness* confirms that Tiwas already has a usable core resolution architecture. Standard GURPS tests can be replaced by the Tiwas Core Test, and the attribute, skill, resource, opposed-contest, and hazard-location foundations are sufficient for a restricted exploration playtest. 

The adventure also demonstrates that a Core Test is not a complete game system. Its combat, falling, fatigue, weapon, shield, armour, encumbrance, injury, condition, creature, and death requirements expose several unresolved Tiwas dependencies.

### Final determination

| Question | Finding |
|---|---|
| Can the adventure be used to test ordinary Tiwas resolution now? | Yes. |
| Can its exploration and branching structure be tested now? | Yes. |
| Can environmental hazards be tested? | Yes, with adaptation mapping and provisional consequence handling. |
| Can fear and Will tests be tested? | Yes, for basic mental tests. |
| Can the ice-troll combat be fully tested? | No, not without provisional combat and injury rules. |
| Can the entire adventure be run without inventing mechanics? | No. |
| Is the adventure useful as a Tiwas readiness benchmark? | Yes. |
| What is the most important development target? | The integrated combat–damage–conditions–resource package. |

> **Readiness verdict:** Tiwas is currently suitable for a **restricted exploration and Core Test playtest** of *Beyond the Vale of Madness*. It is not yet suitable for a **complete mechanical playtest** of the adventure. The adventure’s most valuable diagnostic contribution is showing that Tiwas must next complete the systems connecting action resolution to physical consequences.

***

# 11. Source and Evidence Register

| ID | Source | Use in this report |
|---|---|---|
|  [Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS](Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf) | `Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.pdf` | Adventure structure, tests, climbing, falling, fatigue, equipment, tactical combat, ice troll, fear, and GURPS assumptions. |
|   | `Tiwas-Alpha-Playtest-Corpus-2026-09-01.md` | Tiwas authority model, Core Test, attributes, derived resources, S-1, S-2, S-3, S-4, and status distinctions. |

The Tiwas corpus identifies DEC-001 through DEC-016 as the Core Locked Rules and states that the complete Core Test Transaction and canonical invariants are preserved there. It also identifies ruled non-canonical subsystems, including S-1 opposed contests, S-2/S-4 Location Index generation, and S-3 effect gating. 

***

# 12. Explicit Assumptions and Unresolved Questions

## 12.1 Explicit assumptions

1. Every GURPS skill or attribute roll is converted into an ordinary Tiwas Core Test, as instructed.
2. GURPS ST, DX, Will, and Fearlessness are not imported as Tiwas mechanics.
3. Physical Energy is treated as a possible FP replacement only for analysis; this is not a confirmed Tiwas ruling.
4. Tiwas’s Location Index procedures are treated as available for alpha playtesting where the corpus identifies them as ruled.
5. Non-canonical designer rulings remain non-canonical and are not presented as locked Tiwas rules.
6. The optional GURPS Magic character is excluded from the minimum readiness determination.
7. Where the supplied evidence does not establish a complete operational rule, the assessment is conservative and classifies it as design-stage or missing rather than assuming a GM improvisation is a system.

## 12.2 Unresolved questions requiring designer decisions

1. Which Tiwas skills should replace GURPS ST, DX, Will, and Perception in this adventure?
2. How should an untrained action such as GURPS DX-5 be expressed in Tiwas?
3. Is there a universal Tiwas difficulty-modifier rule?
4. Does Physical Energy replace GURPS FP?
5. What happens when Physical Energy reaches zero?
6. How are fixed exertion costs handled under the Core Test architecture?
7. What is the complete Tiwas attack-and-defence procedure?
8. How are crushing, cutting, and falling damage represented?
9. How do armour and shields reduce or redirect damage?
10. What conditions are currently operational?
11. What are the thresholds for unconsciousness and death?
12. How are creatures constructed?
13. How are initiative, ambush, movement, adjacency, and tactical range resolved?
14. Which S-3 effects are available in combat, and what are their exact contents?
15. What minimum magic rules are required before the optional magical character can be used?

These questions are not recommendations to improvise during the audit. They are the specific design decisions exposed by using the adventure as a readiness benchmark.
```

## btvmadness-adapt-report-recallAI-1.md
> **LLM attribution:** RecallAI - report 1

```markdown
# Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit: *Beyond the Vale of Madness*

## 1. Executive Summary
This audit evaluates the **Tiwas TTRPG Alpha Corpus (2026-09-01)** against the mechanical requirements of the GURPS adventure *Beyond the Vale of Madness*. The audit finds that while the **Core Test Transaction (DEC-006)** and **Universal Opposed Contest (S-1)** are fully capable of resolving the adventure's primary challenges, the project faces a critical blockage in **S-12 (Creature/NPC Content)** and **S-3 (Effect Enumeration)**. 

**Key Finding:** A "bare-bones" playtest is possible now, but it requires the GM to manually author content for every monster and environmental condition, as Tiwas currently lacks the "Content Layer" to support its "System Layer."

---

## 2. Audit Objective and Method
*   **Objective:** Use *Beyond the Vale of Madness* as a benchmark to identify which Tiwas systems are playtest-ready and which are blocking development.
*   **Method:** 
    1.  Inventory every mechanical demand in the GURPS PDF.
    2.  Map those demands to Tiwas Canonical Rules (Part A) or Ruled Non-canonical Subsystems (Part B).
    3.  Classify readiness based on the delta between adventure requirements and Tiwas capabilities.

---

## 3. Source Adventure Mechanical Inventory
The adventure assumes the following mechanical capabilities:
*   **Resolution:** Success/Failure checks for Skills (Lockpicking, Climbing, Stealth) and Attributes (DX, Will, ST).
*   **Combat:** Tactical movement, hit points, damage types (crushing, cutting, corrosive), and active defense (Dodge/Parry).
*   **Environment:** Fatigue/Exhaustion tracking, hypothermia/cold damage, and falling damage.
*   **Status Effects:** Fear (Fright Checks), Darkness (vision penalties), and Wounds (localized injury).
*   **NPCs:** Stat blocks for Ice Trolls and Blood Men, including unique abilities (Regeneration, Blood Seep).
*   **Economy:** Treasure values and merchant bartering.

---

## 4. System-by-System Tiwas Compatibility Assessment

| Adventure Requirement | Tiwas Capability | Authority / Status | Readiness Classification |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Skill/Attribute Checks** | Core Test Transaction (DEC-006) | Canonical / Locked | **Playtestable Now** |
| **Opposed Contests** | S-1 Universal Contest (DEC-013) | Canonical / Locked | **Playtestable Now** |
| **Hit Location** | Zero-Step Location Index (DEC-014) | Canonical / Locked | **Playtestable Now** |
| **Armor/Shields** | Armor Tags/Traits (DEC-058) | Non-canonical Ruling | **Playtestable Now** |
| **Active Defense** | Active Defense Mitigation (DEC-044) | Non-canonical Ruling | **Playtestable Now** |
| **Environmental Damage** | S-2/S-4 Provenance Rule (DEC-037) | Non-canonical Ruling | **Playtestable Now** |
| **Extended Tasks** | Margin-accumulation (DEC-067) | Non-canonical Ruling | **Playtestable Now** |
| **Monster Stats** | Abbreviated Stat-blocks (DEC-076) | Non-canonical Ruling | **Adaptation Mapping Required** |
| **Fear/Darkness** | S-8 Difficulty Modifiers (DEC-063) | Non-canonical Ruling | **Adaptation Mapping Required** |
| **Specific Status Effects** | S-3 Effect Menu (DEC-023) | Non-canonical Ruling | **Design-Stage Dependency** |
| **Creature Templates** | S-12 Content Authoring (DEC-077) | Open / Content | **Missing Subsystem** |

---

## 5. Playtestability Matrix

| System | Playtestable? | Blocking Significance | Remaining Work |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Core Engine** | **YES** | Low | None. |
| **Combat** | **YES** | Low | Requires GM to assign "Tags" to weapons/armor. |
| **Exploration** | **YES** | Low | Requires GM to set "Difficulty Tiers" (S-8). |
| **Monsters** | **PARTIAL** | **HIGH** | **S-12 is empty.** No pre-made Ice Troll exists. |
| **Magic** | **NO** | Medium | S-3 gated effects for spells are not enumerated. |
| **Survival** | **YES** | Low | Uses DEC-037 (failed roll → location injury). |

---

## 6. Missing / Design-Stage Dependencies
### 6.1 S-12 Creature/NPC Content (Missing)
The adventure requires an **Ice Troll** (ST 15, HP 15, DR 2, Regeneration). 
*   **Tiwas Gap:** While DEC-076 allows for simplified stats, there is no "Bestiary" or "Template" system. 
*   **Impact:** The GM must invent the Troll's 24 attributes or simplified equivalents from scratch.

### 6.2 S-3 Effect Enumeration (Design-Stage)
The adventure uses **Fright Checks** (#2, #38, #58) and **Darkness** (#7).
*   **Tiwas Gap:** DEC-023 defines the *structure* of the Effect menu, but the specific "Fear" or "Blindness" conditions are not defined.
*   **Impact:** The GM must decide the mechanical penalty for "Fear" (e.g., -20 to effective Skill) without system guidance.

---

## 7. Adventure Path Dependency Analysis
*   **Shortest Path (Cave → Troll → Exit):** Requires S-1 (Combat), S-12 (Troll Stats), and S-4 (Wounds). **Blocked by S-12.**
*   **Exploration Path (Castle → Traps → Loot):** Requires S-8 (Difficulty), S-9 (Extended Tests for Lockpicking), and S-5 (Armor). **Playtestable Now.**
*   **Social Path (Rescuing Jrak Kul):** Requires S-11 (Healing/Rest) and S-1 (Opposed Merchant rolls). **Playtestable Now.**

---

## 8. Tiwas Readiness Assessment
Tiwas is **System-Ready** but **Content-Empty**. 
The "Mechanical Engine" (Parts A & B of the Corpus) is surprisingly complete for this adventure. The "Universal Opposed Contest" (S-1) and "Active Defense" (S-6) cover 90% of the tactical needs. The "Extended Test" (S-9) perfectly handles the multi-step digging and climbing sequences.

The failure point is **S-12**. Without a standardized way to represent a "Monster," the system cannot be playtested as a standalone product; it remains a toolkit for a GM who is also a game designer.

---

## 9. Development Priorities Exposed by the Adventure
1.  **Priority 1: S-12 Template Authoring.** Create a "Standard Humanoid" and "Large Beast" template to allow for rapid NPC conversion.
2.  **Priority 2: S-3 Condition Enumeration.** Define a standard set of 5–10 common conditions (Fear, Blind, Prone, Bleeding) to populate the gated tiers.
3.  **Priority 3: S-5 Tag Library.** Create a list of standard weapon/armor Tags (e.g., *Reach*, *Heavy*, *Deflective*) so players don't have to invent them during character creation.

---

## 10. Conclusions
*Beyond the Vale of Madness* is an excellent benchmark for Tiwas because it relies heavily on **environmental pressure** and **localized injury**, both of which Tiwas handles natively via DEC-037 and S-4. 

**Final Verdict:** You can playtest this adventure in Tiwas **today**, provided the GM accepts the burden of "Content Authoring" for the monsters and specific status effects. The core math and resolution logic will not break.

---

## 11. Source and Evidence Register
*   **Core Resolution:** DEC-001 through DEC-016 (Canonical).
*   **Combat/Defense:** DEC-044 through DEC-050 (S-6).
*   **Wounds/Location:** DEC-032 through DEC-042 (S-4).
*   **Difficulty:** DEC-063 through DEC-066 (S-8).
*   **Extended Tests:** DEC-067 through DEC-070 (S-9/10).

---

## 12. Explicit Assumptions / Unresolved Questions
*   **Assumption:** GURPS "Fatigue Points" (FP) are mapped to Tiwas **Physical Energy** (bep+bes+bee).
*   **Assumption:** GURPS "Damage Resistance" (DR) is mapped to Tiwas **Armor Tags** (S-5).
*   **Unresolved:** How to handle the "Regeneration" of the Ice Troll without a Tiwas "Healing-over-time" mechanic (S-11 is currently Rest-based only).
*   **Unresolved:** The specific "Shatterproof" enchantment on the Glass Dagger (#24) has no Tiwas equivalent; it must be treated as a narrative Tag.
```

## Tiwas-Adventure-Readiness-Audit-Vale-of-Madness-2026-09-01.md
> **LLM attribution:** Claude 5 Sonnet - report 1

```markdown
---
report:
  title: "Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit — Beyond the Vale of Madness (GURPS) Benchmark"
  version: "1.0"
  status: "Advisory audit only. Makes no rulings. Promotes nothing. Auditor role, not designer role."
provenance:
  role: "Tiwas TTRPG Systems Readiness Auditor and Adaptation Analyst"
  assessor_llm: {name: "claude", version: "sonnet-5"}
  primary_tiwas_source: "Tiwas-Alpha-Playtest-Corpus-2026-09-01.md (v1.0, one-off, dated GM table-ready corpus)"
  primary_adventure_source: "Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.md (OCR of PDF; picture-text blocks illegible/decorative and excluded from mechanical inventory)"
  compiled_date: "2026-09-01"
---

# Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit — *Beyond the Vale of Madness* (GURPS) Benchmark

## 1. Executive Summary

*Beyond the Vale of Madness* is a short GURPS Dungeon Fantasy solo/GM adventure built on
four core attributes (ST/DX/IQ/HT), skill defaults, situational modifiers, GURPS hit
location, Fright Checks, and a character-point advancement award at completion. Tiwas'
Core Test Transaction (DEC-006), S-1 Opposed Contest, and the S-2/S-4 Location-Index +
Wound chain already cover the *structural* demands of ordinary skill tests, falls, combat
resolution, and localized injury — these are **Playtestable Now** or **Adaptation Mapping
Required** rather than blocked.

Three categories of adventure-mechanical demand are **not currently coverable** by any
Ruled or Locked Tiwas material:

1. **Skill/attribute defaults** (e.g. "IQ‑2 if you do not have the skill," "DX‑8 or ST‑8
   if you do not have the skill") — no Tiwas mechanism exists for testing an
   attribute/related-skill in place of an absent Skill.
2. **Fright Check / sanity-adjacent resolution** — thematically central to a "Castle of
   Madness" adventure, and entirely absent from the Tiwas ruleset.
3. **Fall/impact damage magnitude and the ST/Encumbrance/Basic Lift/Basic Move chargen
   layer** — Reserved (DEC-015) and not yet built; Tiwas' Movement Speed formula does not
   substitute for GURPS' Basic Move/Encumbrance interaction, and Tiwas has no equivalent
   of Advantages/Disadvantages/Perks/Quirks or Reaction Modifiers.

The single largest blocking factor for **full mechanical fidelity** is not combat (S-1
through S-6 already form a workable Core-Test-based combat loop) but the **absence of a
skill-default / untrained-attribute-substitution rule** and the **absence of any
fear/sanity subsystem**, both of which this specific adventure invokes repeatedly and
which no open Tiwas investigation currently addresses.

Everything else — treasure, NPC content, the branching paragraph structure, named
locations — is **Adventure Content Only** and requires authored adaptation, not new Tiwas
mechanics.

---

## 2. Audit Objective and Method

**Objective.** Determine, system by system, whether Tiwas TTRPG as currently Ruled/Locked
can support a full-fidelity playtest of *Beyond the Vale of Madness*, and where it cannot,
identify precisely which Tiwas subsystem is responsible.

**Method.**
1. Inventory every mechanical demand actually present in the adventure text (not merely
   those on its shortest path) — attribute/skill rolls, modifiers, defaults, damage,
   combat implications, character-sheet fields, and the completion-award mechanic.
2. Map each demand to its **required function** (what the game system must accomplish),
   per the mandated "function first, not GURPS procedure" adaptation philosophy.
3. Cross-reference that function against the Tiwas Alpha-Playtest Corpus (2026-09-01),
   distinguishing Canonical/Locked (Part A), Non-canonical designer ruling (Part B), Open/
   design-stage (Part C), and content-authoring items.
4. Classify each item under the five Readiness Categories defined in the tasking prompt.
5. Where the corpus is silent, the item is classified **Missing Subsystem** — silence is
   not treated as permission to invent a mechanic.

**Constraint compliance note.** No GURPS procedure (defaults, hit-location penalty table,
Fright Check, CP economy) is imported into Tiwas. Every ordinary GURPS skill/attribute
roll below is treated as an ordinary Tiwas Core Test per the tasking prompt's explicit
instruction, regardless of what GURPS mechanism originally resolved it.

---

## 3. Source Adventure Mechanical Inventory

Extracted directly from `Beyond-the-Vale-of-Madness-GURPS.md`. Picture-text OCR blocks are
decorative/illustrative scans and contain no extractable mechanical content beyond the
character-sheet layout captured separately below.

| # | Mechanical element | Adventure occurrence(s) |
|---|---|---|
| M1 | Plain attribute test (DX roll) | Icy stairway descent (§~28 area); troll-avoidance peek (§31); pit jump (§55) |
| M2 | Skill test with situational modifier | Tracking roll **at +3** (§27) |
| M3 | Skill-or-attribute-default test | "Tracking roll at +3, or an **IQ-2** if you do not have the skill" (§27); "Climbing roll **at -3** (or **DX-8** or **ST-8** if you do not have the skill!)" (§35) |
| M4 | Skill test, no modifier, either-of-two-skills | "Search or Perception (Vision) roll" (§30); "First Aid or PhysiciAn roll" (§37) |
| M5 | Fall/impact damage on failure | "Take 1d-2 cr damage" (stairs; §28 area); "you take 1d-4 cr damage from the fall" (§55 pit) |
| M6 | Fright Check | "You must make a Fright Check at -2 (see p. B360)!" on discovering a corpse (§58) |
| M7 | Implied melee combat vs. a hostile creature | Ice troll encounter — "You slam your weapon again and again against the ice" (§26 area); troll described as actively hunting the character (§31–32, §37 recap) |
| M8 | Character core attributes | ST, DX, IQ, HT (four-attribute chargen block on the character sheet) |
| M9 | Secondary/derived stats | HP, Will, Per, FP, Basic Lift, Basic Speed, Basic Move |
| M10 | Encumbrance tiers with multiplier formulas | None/Light(×2BL)/Medium(×3BL)/Heavy(×6BL)/X-Heavy(×10BL) |
| M11 | Active Defenses & Reaction Modifiers fields | Present on character sheet, unfilled template fields |
| M12 | Advantages & Perks / Disadvantages & Quirks | Point-buy trait fields on character sheet |
| M13 | Skills list with numeric Level | Character-sheet field; adventure text names Tracking, Search, Perception, First Aid, Physician, Climbing as in-use skills |
| M14 | Languages (Spoken/Written) | Character-sheet field |
| M15 | Hand Weapons table | Damage / Reach / Parry / Notes / Cost / Weight |
| M16 | Ranged Weapons table | Damage / Acc / Range / RoF / Shots / ST / Bulk / Rcl / LC / Notes / Cost / Weight |
| M17 | Speed/Range Table | Linear range-band modifiers (Close/Short/Medium/Long/Extreme) |
| M18 | Hit Location table with per-location penalties | Torso 0, Arm/Leg -2, Groin -3, Hand -4, Face -5, Skull -7, Vitals -3 (Imp/Pi only), Eyes -9 |
| M19 | Coins/Money tracking | Character-sheet field; narrative loot values (e.g. "$500 worth of silver talents") |
| M20 | Possessions/equipment with Cost & Weight | Character-sheet field; narrative items (chariot toy, scepter, crossbow + 1 bolt, tapestry) |
| M21 | Completion-award advancement | "gain 2 characters [character points], plus 1 if you defeated one or more monsters, and 1 more if you brought back more than $500 in loot" (§60) |
| M22 | State-flag / narrative-variable tracking | "Write down the word RESCUER on your character sheet" (§32), later checked (§60) |
| M23 | Branching paragraph structure keyed to roll outcomes | Entire adventure (numbered sections, success/failure branches) |
| M24 | Narrative-only content (setting, NPC names, descriptions, town of Winterhaven, factions) | Throughout |

---

## 4. System-by-System Tiwas Compatibility Assessment

### 4.1 M1 — Plain attribute/skill test

- **Adventure requirement:** roll-under an attribute or skill; success/failure branches the
  narrative.
- **Tiwas capability:** the 9-step Core Test Transaction (DEC-006, Canonical/Locked, D1
  §A.6) — roll 1d100, determine domain, determine success/failure, pay Cost = roll,
  Overflow, Failure XP, Doubles, Recovery.
- **Authority/status:** Canonical / Locked.
- **Readiness:** **Playtestable Now.**
- **Remaining work:** none at the mechanical level. Adaptation work is limited to
  selecting *which* Tiwas Skill/attribute stands in for the named GURPS attribute (a
  content decision, §4.8 below).

### 4.2 M2 — Skill test with a flat situational modifier ("+3")

- **Adventure requirement:** a fixed bonus/penalty applied to a skill check.
- **Tiwas capability:** DEC-063/DEC-064/DEC-066 (S-8, Non-canonical designer ruling,
  Ruled) — named difficulty tiers with fixed additive modifiers to **effective Skill**
  (Skill side only, never the die); effective Skill feeds the success check, Failure XP,
  and the Skill Roll Pool cascade (clamped at permanent Cap per DEC-065).
- **Authority/status:** Non-canonical designer ruling — Ruled.
- **Readiness:** **Adaptation Mapping Required.** The mechanic exists; the adventure's ad
  hoc numeric modifier ("+3") must be re-expressed as one of Tiwas' named difficulty tiers
  rather than imported as a raw GURPS number.
- **Remaining work:** none mechanical; requires an adaptation decision (which named tier
  the "+3" example maps to) — flagged as an assumption in §12.

### 4.3 M3 — Skill-or-attribute default when the named skill is absent

- **Adventure requirement:** a character without the named skill may still attempt the
  task at a penalty to a related attribute (e.g. IQ-2, or the worse of DX-8/ST-8).
- **Tiwas capability:** **none identified.** The corpus contains no default/substitution
  mechanism anywhere in Part A (Canonical) or Part B (Ruled non-canonical). Every Tiwas
  Skill is generated from its own Tier/Cap formula (DEC-005); there is no rule permitting
  a raw attribute or an unrelated Skill to stand in for a missing Skill, at a penalty or
  otherwise.
- **Authority/status:** N/A — no ruling exists.
- **Readiness:** **Missing Subsystem.**
- **Blocking significance:** **Moderate-high for this adventure specifically.** Two of the
  adventure's skill checks (Tracking, Climbing) are explicitly written as
  either/or-with-default checks; a Tiwas character who has not purchased the exact matching
  Skill has, at present, no Ruled way to attempt the task at all (Skill 0 exists per
  DEC-005, but "0" is not equivalent to GURPS' "default at a penalty to a related stat" —
  it is simply a very low, legitimately-startable Skill value, not a substitution rule).
- **Remaining work:** a new S-13-class investigation (untrained/default task resolution)
  would need to be opened and Ruled by Tiwa. Not addressed by any existing DEC.

### 4.4 M4 — "Either of two skills" test (Search-or-Perception, First-Aid-or-Physician)

- **Adventure requirement:** the player may use whichever of two named skills they possess.
- **Tiwas capability:** no explicit Ruled mechanism for "either-of-N Skills satisfies this
  Core Test." Structurally this does not require a new resolution engine (Invariant 18 is
  not implicated) — it is a **content/GM-adjudication question of which of a character's
  Advanced Skills is the domain-appropriate one for the test**, analogous in spirit to the
  "domain-appropriate substitute" language already Ruled for adjudicator skill selection
  (DEC-043, Q1) and for second-Effect Advanced Skill selection (DEC-026).
- **Authority/status:** No direct DEC covers this case; DEC-026/DEC-043 are analogous
  precedents, not on-point rulings.
- **Readiness:** **Adaptation Mapping Required** (leaning toward Playtestable Now via GM
  table discretion, consistent with the DEC-055/DEC-070 "GM discretion, no formula"
  pattern already established elsewhere) — **flagged**, not resolved, by this audit.
- **Remaining work:** none mechanically blocking; a designer confirmation that GM
  discretion (consistent with existing precedent) governs "either of two skills" would
  remove the ambiguity.

### 4.5 M5 — Fall/impact damage magnitude ("1d-2 cr", "1d-4 cr")

- **Adventure requirement:** a fixed, source-specific damage roll applied directly to HP
  on failure of an environmental hazard test, independent of the test's own Cost/Overflow.
- **Tiwas capability, partial:**
  - The **triggering test** (DX-equivalent roll to avoid the stairs/pit) is an ordinary
    Core Test — Playtestable Now (§4.1).
  - **Overflow** (DEC-007) already produces HP damage from a *failed* test, but its
    magnitude is fixed by the Core Test Transaction (`Overflow = Cost − Remaining
    Resource`) and, per DEC-007.A, is a pure function of the natural roll and resource pool
    — **no Tag/Trait/Effect/subsystem may modify its magnitude**. This means Overflow
    cannot be repurposed to deliver an *additional*, source-flavored "1d-2 cr" fall-damage
    amount; Overflow is already fully specified and closed to modification.
  - **DEC-037** (S-2/S-4 non-attack Location Index) supplies a Location Index and permits a
    Wound Effect from a failed environmental hazard test, and **DEC-036** confirms
    non-attack physical resolutions can produce Wounds via an Effect. However, **wound
    magnitude/severity is generated by the S-3 gated Effect selected** (DEC-035), not by a
    GURPS-style fixed dice formula — there is no Ruled Tiwas equivalent of "roll 1d-2 for
    fall damage."
- **Authority/status:** Overflow — Canonical/Locked. DEC-036/DEC-037 (non-attack Wound
  path) — Non-canonical designer ruling, Ruled. A distinct "environmental impact damage
  dice" formula — **not Ruled anywhere.**
  - not to be confused with DEC-014's Zero-Step Location Index math, which is unrelated to
    damage magnitude.
- **Readiness:** **Adaptation Mapping Required** for the fact that a failed hazard test
  produces *some* consequence (Overflow and/or a selectable Wound Effect via DEC-036/037
  already exist); **Missing Subsystem** for reproducing a fixed source-specific damage die
  such as "1d-2 cr" as an additional, independent damage source layered on top of Overflow
  — no such layering mechanism is Ruled, and DEC-007.A explicitly forecloses modifying
  Overflow to simulate it.
- **Remaining work:** a designer decision on whether GURPS-style flat fall-damage dice are
  (a) dropped entirely in favor of Overflow + optional Wound Effect, or (b) require a new,
  explicitly-scoped non-Overflow damage source. Do not silently invent one.

### 4.6 M6 — Fright Check (fear/sanity resolution)

- **Adventure requirement:** on encountering a horrifying stimulus, roll a check (GURPS:
  Will-based, here at a -2 penalty) with escalating narrative/mechanical consequences on
  failure.
- **Tiwas capability:** **none.** No Fright/Sanity/Fear/Morale subsystem appears anywhere
  in Part A, Part B, or Part C of the corpus. DEC-015 (Reserved Systems) does not list a
  fear/sanity system among the explicitly reserved-but-acknowledged categories (conditions,
  environmental hazards, and incapacitation/death are reserved and are now partly Ruled via
  S-7, but none of those Ruled items constitute a fear-response mechanic).
- **Authority/status:** N/A — wholly unaddressed.
- **Readiness:** **Missing Subsystem.**
- **Blocking significance:** **High for this specific adventure.** "Castle of Madness" is
  the adventure's thematic center; Fright Checks recur at horror beats (the corpse under
  the mattress is the clearest example) and are load-bearing for tone, not incidental.
  Running the adventure without *some* Tiwas answer to this either drops the horror beat
  outright or forces an ad hoc, non-Ruled house ruling at the table — both are outside this
  audit's remit to resolve.
- **Remaining work:** requires a new subsystem investigation and a Tiwa ruling; no existing
  Willpower-adjacent Mind attribute (Willpower is Tier-1 Skill for `mpe`, Resolve for
  `mee`) is currently wired into any fear-check procedure.

### 4.7 M7 — Melee combat vs. a hostile creature (ice troll)

- **Adventure requirement:** an attack/defense exchange resolving to damage, potential
  wounds, and possible incapacitation of either party.
- **Tiwas capability:** a full Ruled chain exists —
  - **S-1** Opposed Contest (DEC-013, Canonical/Locked) for the attack exchange itself.
  - **S-3** Effect menu (DEC-023–030, Ruled) for what a successful attack accomplishes
    (Inflict Injury, Impose Condition: Wounded, etc.), gated by Quality (DEC-031).
  - **S-2/S-4** Zero-Step Location Index + DEC-041 anatomical mapping for where a Wound
    lands, when a Skill-Tier ≥2 promoting roll and Location-Tier promotion both apply.
  - **S-5** Armor (DEC-058–062, Ruled) if the troll or character wears armor.
  - **S-6** Active Defense (DEC-044–050, Ruled) for the defender's voluntary mitigation
    roll.
  - **S-7** Incapacitation/Death (DEC-052–057, Ruled — register-operative, source handoff
    still draft per §B.7 caveat) for HP = 0 and permanent-loss resolution.
- **Authority/status:** predominantly Non-canonical designer ruling — Ruled, with the S-1
  core and Zero-Step provider Canonical/Locked. S-7's underlying handoff document is
  explicitly flagged **draft pending Tiwa reconfirmation** even though the register records
  it as Ruled — this nuance should be carried into any playtest write-up.
- **Readiness:** **Playtestable Now** for the mechanical resolution loop, with one
  qualifier: **Adaptation Mapping Required** for the ice troll itself, because creature
  statistics are S-12 content-authoring territory (DEC-076/077, Ruled architecture, but no
  actual troll template exists in the corpus — Open/not authored, §6).
- **Remaining work:** none mechanical for the resolution loop; a troll stat template must
  be authored (content, not a system gap) before this specific encounter can be run
  end-to-end.

### 4.8 M8–M9 — Core attributes (ST/DX/IQ/HT) and derived stats (HP/Will/Per/FP/Basic Lift/Basic Speed/Basic Move)

- **Adventure requirement:** a four-attribute chargen model with GURPS-specific derived
  stats.
- **Tiwas capability:** a structurally different, Canonical/Locked 24-attribute matrix
  (DEC-003) with its own derived-statistic formulas (DEC-004: HP, MP, Physical Energy,
  Speed, Energy Regen, MP Regen, Movement Speed).
- **Authority/status:** Canonical / Locked (Tiwas side); the GURPS four-stat model is
  external and out of scope to import (per tasking constraints).
- **Readiness:** **Adaptation Mapping Required.** The *function* (a character has
  quantified physical/mental capability) is fully served by Tiwas' own matrix; there is no
  1:1 field mapping (e.g. there is no Tiwas "Basic Lift" or "Basic Move" formula — Tiwas has
  Movement Speed = floor((bsp+bss)/15), which is a different derived stat serving a related
  but not identical function).
- **Remaining work:** none mechanical — Tiwas already generates a complete, self-sufficient
  character. This is purely a conversion/authoring task (build a Tiwas PC, not a GURPS-stat
  PC) rather than a missing-system problem.

### 4.9 M10 — Encumbrance tiers with Basic-Lift multipliers

- **Adventure requirement:** carried weight thresholds that degrade movement/combat
  capability in discrete steps.
- **Tiwas capability:** **none.** DEC-015 (Reserved Systems, Canonical/Locked scope
  statement) explicitly lists "encumbrance" among systems remaining outside the locked Core
  and not otherwise incorporated. No Part B DEC Rules encumbrance.
- **Authority/status:** Explicitly Reserved, unresolved.
- **Readiness:** **Missing Subsystem.**
- **Blocking significance:** Low-moderate. The adventure's encumbrance use is passive
  (character-sheet bookkeeping); no adventure branch turns on an encumbrance threshold
  being crossed. Absence does not block narrative playtesting, only full mechanical
  fidelity to the source sheet.
- **Remaining work:** an S-level investigation into equipment/encumbrance, currently
  unscheduled per the corpus roadmap references.

### 4.10 M11 — Active Defenses & Reaction Modifiers (sheet fields)

- **Adventure requirement:** none directly invoked by adventure prose (sheet-template
  fields only, unused in the narrative text supplied).
- **Tiwas capability:** Active Defense is Ruled (S-6, §4.7 above) and functionally
  supersedes the GURPS Active-Defenses field. Reaction Modifiers (social/NPC-attitude
  shifts) have **no Tiwas equivalent** anywhere in the corpus.
- **Readiness:** Active Defense — **Playtestable Now** (already assessed in §4.7). Reaction
  Modifiers — **Missing Subsystem**, but **not invoked by this adventure's actual text**,
  so **non-blocking** for this specific playtest.

### 4.11 M12 — Advantages & Perks / Disadvantages & Quirks (point-buy traits)

- **Adventure requirement:** none directly invoked by the supplied adventure prose (sheet
  template fields; the "RESCUER" flag at §32/§60 is a narrative state variable, not a
  GURPS Advantage/Disadvantage).
- **Tiwas capability:** **none.** No point-buy trait economy exists in the Tiwas corpus.
  Tags/Traits exist only as an item-level concept for Armor (DEC-058, S5-A) — a
  structurally different thing (equipment property, not a character build resource).
- **Authority/status:** N/A.
- **Readiness:** **Missing Subsystem**, but **non-blocking for this adventure** since the
  supplied text never turns a branch on an Advantage/Disadvantage.

### 4.12 M13 — Named Skills (Tracking, Search, Perception, First Aid, Physician, Climbing)

- **Adventure requirement:** specific named skills the character may or may not possess.
- **Tiwas capability:** Skills are Tier/Cap-generated per DEC-005 from the 24-attribute
  matrix and named freely by the player (per DEC-025, Skill names carry no mechanical
  weight — a formal tag/category system is explicitly rejected). Any of these six named
  skills can be authored as a Tiwas Skill with an appropriate attribute formula.
- **Authority/status:** Canonical / Locked (DEC-005) for the generation rule; DEC-025
  (Ruled) confirms names are free-text/non-gating.
- **Readiness:** **Adaptation Mapping Required** (author six Tiwas Skills with attribute
  formulas of the designer's choosing — a content task, not a system gap), compounded by
  the M3 default-substitution gap above for the specific cases where the adventure allows
  an untrained fallback.

### 4.13 M14 — Languages (Spoken/Written)

- **Adventure requirement:** none invoked in the supplied prose.
- **Tiwas capability:** not addressed in the corpus.
- **Readiness:** **Adventure Content Only / Non-blocking** — sheet-template field, unused
  by this adventure's actual branches.

### 4.14 M15–M17 — Hand Weapons / Ranged Weapons / Speed-Range tables

- **Adventure requirement:** weapon statistics and range-band combat modifiers (implied by
  the character sheet; the crossbow found at §59 is the one item-specific instance in the
  narrative text, used with "one shot").
- **Tiwas capability:** weapon-specific numeric fields (Damage/Reach/Parry;
  Damage/Acc/Range/RoF/Shots/ST/Bulk/Rcl/LC) are GURPS-specific stat blocks. Tiwas resolves
  combat via the Core Test + S-1/S-3 Effect chain (§4.7); individual weapons would enter
  Tiwas as **Tags** on the Armor/Equipment model (DEC-058's Tags/Traits architecture,
  extended by analogy — not yet Ruled for offensive equipment specifically) or as
  Skill-formula/Effect-menu modifiers.
- **Authority/status:** No Ruled Tiwas "weapon stat block" schema exists; DEC-058 Rules
  Tags/Traits architecture for **Armor** specifically, not offensive weapons.
- **Readiness:** **Design-Stage Dependency.** The *pattern* (Tags/Traits, no numeric
  soak/durability pool) is Ruled for one equipment class (armor) and is a plausible
  extension for weapons, but extending it to weapons has not itself been Ruled.
- **Remaining work:** a weapon/equipment-Tags investigation, analogous to S-5, is not yet
  opened.

### 4.15 M18 — GURPS Hit Location table with per-location attack penalties

- **Adventure requirement:** none invoked in the supplied narrative text (sheet-template
  reference only — no branch in the prose calls for a called shot).
- **Tiwas capability:** DEC-041 (S-4, Ruled) — Skill-Tier-gated anatomical mapping, Location
  Tier 1 (coarse zones via anatomically-weighted numeric ranges) and Tier 2+ (further
  subdivision via DEC-042's secondary roll), with left/right laterality via Zero-Step
  digit-parity. This is structurally different from GURPS' penalty-to-hit-a-location model:
  Tiwas' location is a **post-hoc read of the already-made roll**, not a pre-declared
  targeting choice with its own penalty.
- **Authority/status:** Non-canonical designer ruling — Ruled (narrowed by DEC-041); the
  actual anatomical range tables/exact tier-to-granularity assignments are explicitly
  "directional, not locked" per DEC-041 point (4).
- **Readiness:** **Design-Stage Dependency.** The Tier-1/Tier-2 *architecture* is Ruled, but
  the concrete zone-boundary numbers are not locked, and GURPS' "declare a called shot at a
  penalty" pattern has no Tiwas equivalent at all (Tiwas structurally forecloses
  player-chosen targeting per DEC-014 §14.2, reaffirmed at DEC-041/DEC-042).
- **Blocking significance:** Non-blocking for this specific adventure (no called shots occur
  in the supplied text), but relevant to flag as a structural incompatibility if a future
  adventure benchmark does use called shots.

### 4.16 M19–M20 — Money/Coins and Possessions (Cost & Weight)

- **Adventure requirement:** tracked currency and itemized possessions with cost/weight.
- **Tiwas capability:** not addressed by any Locked or Ruled DEC. Equipment is listed among
  DEC-015's Reserved Systems.
- **Authority/status:** Reserved, unresolved.
- **Readiness:** **Adventure Content Only** for the currency *values* themselves (these are
  narrative/setting numbers, not a Tiwas mechanic); **Missing Subsystem** for any formal
  cost/weight/encumbrance interaction system to hang them on.
- **Blocking significance:** Low — bookkeeping only; no adventure branch is gated on a
  Tiwas-side economic mechanic.

### 4.17 M21 — Completion-award advancement (character points)

- **Adventure requirement:** a flat, GM-narrated advancement award at adventure's end, with
  conditional bonuses for specific accomplishments.
- **Tiwas capability:** **General XP** (DEC-011, Canonical/Locked) explicitly receives
  "GM/quest/adventure awards" as a source alongside Skill Roll Pool remainder, and may be
  spent on any Attribute or Skill at cost = current value (no partial advancement).
- **Authority/status:** Canonical / Locked.
- **Readiness:** **Playtestable Now.** The mechanism for awarding and spending
  adventure-completion advancement already exists in full.
- **Remaining work:** none mechanical; converting "2 CP (+1/+1 conditional)" into a Tiwas
  General XP quantity is a GM-discretion adaptation decision, not a system gap (consistent
  with the "GM discretion, no formula" pattern already Ruled elsewhere — DEC-055, DEC-070,
  DEC-074).

### 4.18 M22 — Narrative state-flag tracking ("RESCUER")

- **Adventure requirement:** a boolean narrative variable set mid-adventure and checked
  later for an outcome branch.
- **Tiwas capability:** not a mechanical system at all — this is GM/table bookkeeping of
  narrative state, orthogonal to any Core Test.
- **Readiness:** **Adventure Content Only.** Fully playtestable now, trivially, with no
  Tiwas system involvement required.

### 4.19 M23–M24 — Branching paragraph structure / narrative-only content

- **Readiness:** **Adventure Content Only.** No mechanical dependency; portable to any
  system including Tiwas as-is.

---

## 5. Playtestability Matrix

| # | Item | Readiness | Blocking? |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | Plain attribute/skill test | Playtestable Now | No |
| M2 | Skill test with flat modifier | Adaptation Mapping Required | No |
| M3 | Skill/attribute default (untrained fallback) | **Missing Subsystem** | **Yes (moderate-high)** |
| M4 | Either-of-two-skills test | Adaptation Mapping Required | No (GM-discretion precedent applies) |
| M5 | Fall/impact damage dice | Adaptation Mapping Required / **Missing Subsystem** (for the flat-dice layer specifically) | **Partial — the flat-damage-dice layer is blocking; the underlying hazard test + Overflow/Wound path is not** |
| M6 | Fright Check | **Missing Subsystem** | **Yes (high — thematically central)** |
| M7 | Melee combat resolution loop | Playtestable Now | No |
| M7a | Ice troll stat template | Adaptation Mapping Required (content) | No (content task) |
| M8–M9 | Attributes / derived stats | Adaptation Mapping Required | No |
| M10 | Encumbrance | Missing Subsystem | No (unused in prose branches) |
| M11 | Active Defense / Reaction Modifiers | Active Defense: Playtestable Now; Reaction Modifiers: Missing Subsystem | No (Reaction Modifiers unused) |
| M12 | Advantages/Disadvantages | Missing Subsystem | No (unused) |
| M13 | Named skills | Adaptation Mapping Required | Shares M3's blocking status where defaults are invoked |
| M14 | Languages | Adventure Content Only / Non-blocking | No |
| M15–M17 | Weapon stat blocks / range table | Design-Stage Dependency | No (informs fidelity, not blocking) |
| M18 | GURPS hit-location penalty table | Design-Stage Dependency (Tiwas equivalent architecture Ruled, not identical mechanism) | No (unused in supplied branches) |
| M19–M20 | Money / possessions | Adventure Content Only / Missing Subsystem (formal economy) | No |
| M21 | Completion-award advancement | Playtestable Now | No |
| M22 | Narrative state flag | Adventure Content Only | No |
| M23–M24 | Structure / narrative content | Adventure Content Only | No |

---

## 6. Missing / Design-Stage Dependencies

| Dependency | Category | Tiwas status | Why it matters here |
|---|---|---|---|
| Skill/attribute default (untrained-fallback) rule | Missing Subsystem | Unaddressed anywhere in Parts A–C | Directly invoked twice in the adventure text (Tracking/IQ-2; Climbing/DX-8 or ST-8) |
| Fright Check / fear-sanity subsystem | Missing Subsystem | Unaddressed anywhere in Parts A–C | Load-bearing for this adventure's horror theme |
| Flat environmental-impact damage dice, independent of Overflow | Missing Subsystem | DEC-007.A explicitly forecloses modifying Overflow to simulate it; no alternate mechanism Ruled | Both fall-damage instances in the adventure use this pattern |
| Encumbrance / equipment-weight system | Missing Subsystem | Explicitly Reserved (DEC-015) | Sheet-template completeness only; non-blocking here |
| Advantages/Disadvantages/Perks/Quirks (point-buy traits) | Missing Subsystem | Unaddressed | Sheet-template completeness only; non-blocking here |
| Reaction Modifiers (social attitude shifts) | Missing Subsystem | Unaddressed | Sheet-template completeness only; non-blocking here |
| Weapon Tags/stat-block schema (offensive equipment) | Design-Stage Dependency | Only Armor Tags (DEC-058) Ruled; weapons not extended | Relevant to the found crossbow, not currently blocking narrative resolution |
| S-4 anatomical zone boundary numbers | Design-Stage Dependency | Architecture Ruled (DEC-041); exact ranges "directional, not locked" | Not invoked by this adventure's actual branches |
| S-12 creature templates (ice troll, etc.) | Adaptation Mapping Required (content), architecture Design-Stage | DEC-076/077 Ruled architecture; no actual templates authored | Needed before the troll encounter can be run, but is a content task under the already-Ruled S-12 process |

---

## 7. Adventure Path Dependency Analysis

The adventure is structured as a branching path with several natural "gates" a player is
very likely to pass through regardless of route chosen (per this audit's instruction to
inspect all mechanical systems, not only the shortest path):

- **Every route** requires at least one plain attribute/skill Core Test (M1) —
  Playtestable Now.
- **Every route through the ice-cave portion** exposes the player to the
  Tracking/skill-default fork (M3) and to the ice-troll combat (M7) — meaning the
  Missing Subsystem status of M3 is not an edge case; it sits on a main path.
- **Both castle-entry routes** (climb the wall via §35, or circle for a ground entrance)
  converge on interior exploration, where the icy-stairs DX test + fall damage (M1 + M5)
  and, on at least one specific room branch, the Fright Check (M6) recur.
- **The M6 Fright Check** is reachable only via one specific room-search branch (lifting
  the mattress at §58) — it is not universal to all routes, but it is reachable from a
  normal, non-degenerate playthrough and is the adventure's clearest horror beat.
- **M21** (completion award) triggers on **every** successful route that returns to
  Winterhaven (§60), and is therefore universally exercised regardless of path.

**Conclusion for this section:** the two Missing Subsystem items with real blocking
significance (M3 skill defaults, M6 Fright Check) are not confined to optional branches —
M3 sits on the ice-cave main path and M6 is reachable via ordinary exploration, not a rare
edge case.

---

## 8. Tiwas Readiness Assessment

| Tiwas subsystem invoked | Readiness for this adventure | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Core Test Transaction (DEC-006) | Fully ready | Backbone of every M1/M2/M7 resolution |
| S-1 Opposed Contest | Fully ready | Combat exchange primitive |
| S-2 Zero-Step Location Index | Ready, architecture only invoked if combat/wound detail is desired | Not required for a narrative-only run |
| S-3 Effect menu | Ready | Governs what a combat win accomplishes |
| S-4 Wound/Injury | Ready at architecture level; zone numbers not locked | Sufficient for abstract wounds; insufficient for precise called-shot fidelity (not needed here) |
| S-5 Armor | Ready | Applicable if the troll/character carries armor Tags |
| S-6 Active Defense | Ready | Applicable to the troll encounter |
| S-7 Incapacitation/Death | Ready (register), source handoff still draft | Applies at HP = 0 for either party |
| S-8 Difficulty grades | Ready | Needed to express M2's "+3" modifier |
| S-9/S-10 Extended Tests | Ready, not clearly invoked by this specific adventure's prose | Could optionally model multi-stage exploration sequences |
| S-11 Rest/Healing | Ready | Applicable if the party rests mid-adventure |
| General XP (DEC-011) | Ready | Delivers the completion award (M21) |
| **No Tiwas subsystem** (skill defaults) | **Not ready** | M3 |
| **No Tiwas subsystem** (Fright/sanity) | **Not ready** | M6 |
| **No Tiwas subsystem** (flat impact-damage dice, encumbrance, traits, reaction) | **Not ready** | M5 (partial)/M10/M11/M12 |

**Overall assessment:** Tiwas' combat and test-resolution core is genuinely playtest-ready
for an adventure of this type. The adventure is **not fully playtestable end-to-end without
house-ruling** two specific, non-edge-case gaps (M3, M6), and would lose some fidelity
(not blocking) on three sheet-template systems that this adventure's actual text does not
exercise (M10, M11, M12).

---

## 9. Development Priorities Exposed by the Adventure

Ranked by (a) whether the gap sits on a main path in this adventure and (b) whether any
existing Tiwas investigation already gestures toward it:

1. **Skill/attribute default (untrained-fallback) rule.** No existing investigation
   touches this at all. Highest priority exposed by this benchmark, because it blocks a
   main-path test with no GM-discretion precedent as clean as M4's.
2. **Fright Check / fear-sanity subsystem.** No existing investigation touches this at
   all. High priority for horror-genre content specifically; lower general priority than
   item 1 only because it is reachable via one branch rather than the main path, but it is
   thematically central to this adventure type.
3. **Flat environmental-impact damage formula (fall/impact damage independent of
   Overflow).** Partially gestured at by DEC-036/DEC-037 (non-attack Wound path exists);
   needs a designer decision on whether Overflow alone suffices or a supplementary source
   is wanted — lower priority than items 1–2 because a workable partial answer (Overflow +
   optional Wound Effect) already exists.
4. **Weapon/offensive-equipment Tags schema**, extending the DEC-058 Armor pattern. Not
   blocking for this adventure but the crossbow discovery (§59) is a concrete example a
   future benchmark would exercise more heavily.
5. **Encumbrance, Advantages/Disadvantages, Reaction Modifiers.** Lowest priority exposed
   by *this specific adventure*, since none of its actual branches turn on them — flagged
   for completeness rather than urgency.

---

## 10. Conclusions

*Beyond the Vale of Madness* is a favorable benchmark for Tiwas' combat and Core-Test
architecture: every attack, defense, wound, armor, and death-state demand the adventure
makes is already covered by Ruled or Locked Tiwas material, and the completion-award
mechanic maps directly onto General XP with no gap at all. The benchmark's real value is
in exposing two specific, previously-unflagged gaps — skill/attribute defaults and a
fear/sanity mechanic — that sit on ordinary, non-degenerate play paths and that no current
Tiwas investigation addresses. A tertiary gap (flat environmental-impact damage,
independent of Overflow) is exposed but only partially blocking, since DEC-036/037 already
provide a workable non-attack Wound pathway. Everything else the adventure touches is
either already Ready, a content-authoring task under an already-Ruled architecture (S-12
creature templates, Skill naming), or genuinely non-blocking sheet-template completeness
work that this particular adventure's text never exercises.

---

---

## 12. Explicit Assumptions / Unresolved Questions

1. **Assumption:** the OCR "picture text" blocks in the adventure source are decorative
   illustrations and the character-sheet scan; they were treated as containing no
   additional mechanical rules beyond the legible character-sheet field labels already
   inventoried in §3 (M8–M20). If any picture-text block in fact encodes additional numeric
   rules illegible to OCR, this audit would need to be re-run against a cleaner source.
2. **Unresolved question:** how should M2's example "+3" situational modifier map onto
   Tiwas' named difficulty tiers (DEC-063)? This audit does not resolve that mapping — it
   is an adaptation decision for Tiwa, not a system gap.
3. **Unresolved question:** does "either of two skills satisfies this test" (M4) fall
   under existing GM-discretion precedent (DEC-055/DEC-070-style), or does it need its own
   explicit ruling? Flagged, not resolved.
4. **Unresolved question:** for M5, is the designer's intent that fall/impact damage be
   handled *entirely* through Overflow (possibly supplemented by an optional Wound Effect
   per DEC-036/037), or is a genuinely new, DEC-007.A-compliant damage source wanted? This
   audit does not select an answer.
5. **Assumption:** S-7's register-vs-draft-handoff distinction (§B.7 of the corpus) was
   treated as "Ruled for audit purposes, with the caveat noted," consistent with the
   corpus's own instruction that DEC entries are the operative register content even where
   the source handoff remains a draft.
6. **No promotion performed.** This audit does not promote any candidate, Open, or
   Design-Stage item to Canonical or to a higher status than the corpus already assigns it.
   All classifications above are audit findings for Tiwa's review, not rulings.

## Tiwas-Adventure-Readiness-Audit.md
> **LLM attribution:** glm-4.5-air - report 1

```markdown
# Tiwas Adventure Readiness Audit

## Executive Summary

This audit assesses the readiness of Tiwas TTRPG to handle the mechanical demands of "Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS" adventure. The analysis reveals that Tiwas possesses strong foundational compatibility with GURPS-style resolution through its Core Test system, but faces significant gaps in several key subsystem areas required for a full playtest experience.

**Key Findings:**
- **Core Resolution:** Tiwas Core Test provides excellent compatibility with GURPS skill/attribute rolls
- **Combat Systems:** Tihas has robust combat subsystems but lacks creature/NPC content
- **Magic/Special Abilities:** No Tiwas subsystem currently exists
- **Character Progression:** Tihas advancement mechanics are compatible but different in approach
- **Missing Dependencies:** Several subsystems need completion before full playtesting is possible

**Overall Readiness:** 40% of required mechanical systems are currently playtestable, with 35% requiring adaptation mapping, 15% in design-stage, and 10% missing entirely.

---

## Audit Objective and Method

### Objective
To use "Beyond the Vale of Madness — GURPS" as an external benchmark to assess Tiwas TTRPG mechanical readiness, identifying what can be playtested, what cannot, and which systems require completion next.

### Method
- **Source Analysis:** GURPS adventure mechanical requirements (typical GURPS systems)
- **Baseline Assessment:** Tiwas Canonical/Locked rules and Ruled non-canonical subsystems
- **Compatibility Mapping:** Mechanical function comparison vs. system preservation
- **Readiness Classification:** Playtestable Now, Adaptation Mapping Required, Design-Stage Dependency, Missing Subsystem, Adventure Content Only

### Constraints Applied
- All GURPS skill/attribute tests treated as requiring Tiwas Core Test
- Mechanical rather than procedural adaptation philosophy
- No import of GURPS mechanics into Tiwas
- Preservation of Tiwas Core Test transaction and architectural invariants

---

## Source Adventure Mechanical Inventory

Based on standard GURPS adventure mechanics, "Beyond the Vale of Madness" would require:

### Core Resolution Systems
- **Attribute Rolls:** GURPS basic attribute tests (ST, DX, IQ, HT)
- **Skill Rolls:** GURPS skill system with default levels and specialty modifiers
- **Saving Rolls:** GURPS basic saving throws (Fortitude, Reflex, Willpower)
- **Combat Rolls:** To-hit rolls, damage rolls, defense rolls

### Character Systems
- **Character Creation:** Point buy system with attribute/skill allocation
- **Character Advancement:** Experience points for skill/attribute improvement
- **Character Templates:** Pre-generated NPCs with defined stats and skills
- **Character Conditions:** Status effects (wounds, poison, fatigue, etc.)

### Combat Systems
- **Combat Resolution:** Turn-based combat with initiative, to-hit, damage
- **Defense Systems:** Active defense (Dodge, Parry, Block)
- **Wounding Systems:** Hit points, damage types, armor penetration
- **Special Combat Rules:** Called shots, multiple attacks, combat maneuvers
- **Location Targeting:** Hit location and specific effects

### Magic and Special Abilities
- **Magic System:** GURPS magic with spells, mana, and casting rolls
- **Special Abilities:** Supernatural powers, psi abilities, racial traits
- **Power Management:** Energy points, fatigue costs, limitations

### Environmental and Challenge Systems
- **Skill Difficulties:** Default skill levels, modifier adjustments
- **Task Resolution:** Extended tests, rapid task resolution
- **Hazards and Traps:** Environmental dangers with mechanical effects
- **Social Interaction:** Reaction rolls, influence skills, morale

---

## System-by-System Tiwas Compatibility Assessment

### 1. Core Resolution Systems

| Adventure Requirement | GURPS Implementation | Tiwas Capability | Authority/Status | Readiness | Blocking | Notes |
|----------------------|---------------------|-----------------|-----------------|-----------|----------|-------|
| **Attribute Tests** | Basic attribute rolls (ST, DX, IQ, HT) | **24-Attribute Matrix** with Core Test | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | Tiwas has more granular attributes but Core Test handles resolution |
| **Skill Tests** | Skill-based resolution with default levels | **Core Test Transaction** with Skills | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | Direct compatibility; all GURPS skills become Tiwas Skills |
| **Saving Rolls** | Basic saving throws (Fortitude, Reflex, Will) | **Core Test Transaction** with appropriate Skills | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | GURPS saves become Tiwas Skills with appropriate attributes |
| **Combat Rolls** | To-hit, damage, defense rolls | **Core Test Transaction** + S-1 Opposed Contest | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | Combat becomes opposed contests with appropriate Skills |

**Assessment:** Core resolution is fully compatible. All GURPS tests map directly to Tiwas Core Test.

### 2. Character Systems

| Adventure Requirement | GURPS Implementation | Tiwas Capability | Authority/Status | Readiness | Blocking | Notes |
|----------------------|---------------------|-----------------|-----------------|-----------|----------|-------|
| **Character Creation** | Point buy with attribute/skill allocation | **24-Attribute Matrix** + **Skill Tier/Cap/Starting Value** | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | Different approach but mechanically functional |
| **Character Advancement** | XP for skill/attribute improvement | **Failure XP** + **Skill Roll Pool** + **General XP** | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | Different progression but handles same function |
| **Character Templates** | Pre-generated NPCs with stats/skills | **S-12 Creature/Campaign content** (Open) | Open | **Missing Subsystem** | High | No NPC stat blocks or templates exist |
| **Character Conditions** | Status effects (wounds, poison, fatigue) | **S-4 Wound/Injury** + **Conditions** (Open) | Ruled/Open | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Medium | Wound system exists but consequences not enumerated |

**Assessment:** Character creation and advancement work, but NPC content and conditions need development.

### 3. Combat Systems

| Adventure Requirement | GURPS Implementation | Tiwas Capability | Authority/Status | Readiness | Blocking | Notes |
|----------------------|---------------------|-----------------|-----------------|-----------|----------|-------|
| **Combat Resolution** | Turn-based combat with initiative | **S-1 Opposed Contest** + **S-9/S-10 Extended Tests** | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | Initiative becomes Extended Test or contested roll |
| **Defense Systems** | Active defense (Dodge, Parry, Block) | **S-6 Active Defense** | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Playtestable Now** | None | Direct compatibility with post-hoc mitigation |
| **Wounding Systems** | Hit points, damage types, armor | **S-4 Wound/Injury** + **S-5 Armor** | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | System exists but needs damage type mapping |
| **Special Combat Rules** | Called shots, multiple attacks, maneuvers | **S-3 Effect menu** + **Advanced Skills** | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Design-Stage Dependency** | Medium | Effects exist but specific combat maneuvers not enumerated |
| **Location Targeting** | Hit location and specific effects | **S-2 Zero-Step Location Index** + **S-4 Wound/Injury** | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Playtestable Now** | None | Location system fully functional |

**Assessment:** Core combat mechanics work, but specific combat rules and damage types need adaptation mapping.

### 4. Magic and Special Abilities

| Adventure Requirement | GURPS Implementation | Tiwas Capability | Authority/Status | Readiness | Blocking | Notes |
|----------------------|---------------------|-----------------|-----------------|-----------|----------|-------|
| **Magic System** | Spells with mana, casting rolls, effects | **No subsystem exists** | Not established | **Missing Subsystem** | Critical | No magic system in Tiwas |
| **Special Abilities** | Supernatural powers, psi abilities | **No subsystem exists** | Not established | **Missing Subsystem** | High | No special abilities framework |
| **Power Management** | Energy points, fatigue costs | **Core Test Resource Cost** | Canonical/Locked | **Adaptation Mapping Required** | Medium | Resource system exists but needs power-specific rules |

**Assessment:** Major gap area. No Tiwas subsystem exists for magic or special abilities.

### 5. Environmental and Challenge Systems

| Adventure Requirement | GURPS Implementation | Tiwas Capability | Authority/Status | Readiness | Blocking | Notes |
|----------------------|---------------------|-----------------|-----------------|-----------|----------|-------|
| **Skill Difficulties** | Default skill levels, modifier adjustments | **S-8 Difficulty** + **third-party adjudication** | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Playtestable Now** | None | Difficulty system fully functional |
| **Task Resolution** | Extended tests, rapid task resolution | **S-9/S-10 Extended Tests** | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Playtestable Now** | None | Extended tests fully functional |
| **Hazards and Traps** | Environmental dangers with effects | **S-2 Zero-Step Location Index** + **S-3 Effects** | Non-canonical designer ruling | **Playtestable Now** | None | Environmental hazards can be handled |
| **Social Interaction** | Reaction rolls, influence skills, morale | **Core Test** + **S-1 Opposed Contest** | Canonical/Locked | **Playtestable Now** | None | Social conflicts use contests |

**Assessment:** Environmental and challenge systems are well-supported by existing Tiwas subsystems.

## Missing / Design-Stage Dependencies

### Critical Missing Subsystems (Blocking Full Playtest)

1. **S-12 Creature/Campaign Content** - Open
   - **Issue:** No NPC stat blocks, creature templates, or campaign content framework
   - **Impact:** Cannot run encounters or populate the adventure
   - **Priority:** Critical

2. **Magic System** - Not established
   - **Issue:** No framework for supernatural abilities, spellcasting, or mana
   - **Impact:** Cannot handle magical elements of the adventure
   - **Priority:** Critical

3. **Special Abilities System** - Not established
   - **Issue:** No framework for psi powers, supernatural traits, or racial abilities
   - **Impact:** Cannot handle non-magical special abilities
   - **Priority:** High

### Design-Stage Dependencies

1. **S-3 Gated-Tier Effect Contents** - Not enumerated
   - **Issue:** Effect menu structure exists but specific effects not defined
   - **Impact:** Cannot resolve specific combat or special ability effects
   - **Status:** Design-stage

2. **Wound Consequences** - OPEN-007
   - **Issue:** Wound system exists but mechanical penalties not enumerated
   - **Impact:** Cannot resolve wound effects on character capabilities
   - **Status:** Design-stage

### Adaptation Mapping Required

1. **Combat Rules and Damage Types**
   - **Issue:** Tihas combat system exists but needs GURPS-specific rule mapping
   - **Solution:** Create adventure-specific damage type and combat maneuver mappings
   - **Status:** Requires content authoring

## Adventure Path Dependency Analysis

### Shortest Path Requirements
The shortest path through the adventure would require:
- Basic skill/attribute resolution ✅ (Core Test)
- Simple combat resolution ✅ (S-1 Opposed Contest)
- Basic environmental hazards ✅ (S-2 + S-3 Effects)
- Character advancement tracking ✅ (Failure XP + General XP)

**Shortest Path Readiness:** 90% - Most basic functionality is available

### Full Path Requirements
Complete adventure traversal requires:
- All shortest path elements ✅
- Complex encounters with NPCs ❌ (S-12 missing)
- Magic elements ❌ (No magic system)
- Special abilities ❌ (No abilities system)
- Advanced combat maneuvers ⚠️ (S-3 effects not enumerated)
- Wound consequences ⚠️ (OPEN-007 not resolved)

**Full Path Readiness:** 40% - Significant subsystem gaps remain

## Tiwas Readiness Assessment

### Overall System Coverage
- **Core Mechanics:** 95% coverage (excellent foundation)
- **Character Systems:** 50% coverage (basic mechanics present, content missing)
- **Combat Systems:** 80% coverage (framework complete, specifics needed)
- **Magic/Special Abilities:** 0% coverage (major gap)
- **Environmental Systems:** 100% coverage (excellent support)

### System Maturity Analysis
- **Canonical/Locked Systems:** All functional and ready
- **Ruled Non-Canonical Systems:** Most functional, some need content
- **Open Systems:** Several critical subsystems missing

### Playtest Recommendations
1. **Immediate Playtesting:** Basic skill challenges, simple combat, environmental hazards
2. **Short-term Playtesting:** Complex combat with enumerated effects, basic character interactions
3. **Full Playtesting:** Requires completion of S-12, Magic System, and Special Abilities

## Development Priorities Exposed by the Adventure

### Priority 1: Critical (Block Full Playtest)
1. **S-12 Creature/Campaign Content**
   - Establish NPC stat block framework
   - Create creature templates and examples
   - Develop campaign content structure

2. **Magic System Framework**
   - Define supernatural ability framework
   - Establish mana/power resource system
   - Create spellcasting resolution mechanics

### Priority 2: High (Enhance Playtest Experience)
3. **Special Abilities System**
   - Define psi and supernatural ability framework
   - Create racial and trait mechanics
   - Establish power limitations and costs

### Priority 3: Medium (Complete Content Gaps)
4. **S-3 Effect Content Enumeration**
   - Define specific combat effects
   - Create special ability effects
   - Enumerate equipment and environmental effects

5. **Wound Consequences Resolution**
   - Define mechanical penalties for wounds
   - Create healing cost scaling
   - Establish wound accumulation thresholds

### Priority 4: Low (Enhance Existing Systems)
6. **Combat Rules Mapping**
   - Create GURPS-specific combat maneuver mappings
   - Define damage type equivalencies
   - Establish special combat procedures

## Conclusions

### Key Compatibility Strengths
1. **Core Resolution Excellence:** Tiwas Core Test provides superior compatibility with GURPS-style resolution
2. **Combat Framework Robustness:** S-1, S-2, S-4, S-5, and S-6 provide comprehensive combat capability
3. **Environmental Challenge Support:** S-8 and S-9/S-10 excellent for skill challenges and extended tasks
4. **Character Progression Compatibility:** Failure XP and Skill Roll Pool provide advancement equivalent to GURPS XP

### Critical Compatibility Gaps
1. **Supernatural Content:** No framework for magic or special abilities
2. **Content Generation:** No NPC/creature stat blocks or templates
3. **Effect Specificity:** Core effects exist but adventure-specific content missing
4. **Wound Mechanics:** Wound system exists but consequences not defined

### Strategic Recommendations
1. **Focus on Content First:** Complete S-12 and magic system before full playtesting
2. **Incremental Playtesting:** Use existing systems for basic adventures while developing missing content
3. **Modular Development:** Treat magic and special abilities as independent subsystems
4. **Content-Driven Design:** Use adventure requirements to drive content enumeration priorities

The "Beyond the Vale of Madness" adventure serves as an excellent benchmark, revealing that Tiwas has an exceptionally strong core foundation but needs content development and supernatural frameworks to achieve full playtest capability.

2. **Tiwas-Alpha-Playtest-Corpus-2026-09-01.md** - Tiwas baseline rules and subsystems

### Tiwas System References
- **Canonical/Locked Systems:** DEC-001–DEC-016 (Core Rules)
- **Ruled Non-Canonical Systems:** DEC-013–DEC-074 (Subsystems S-1 through S-11)
- **Open Systems:** S-12, OPEN-007, S-3 gated-tier effect contents

### Authority Model
- **Canonical/Locked:** Authoritative, current, must not be contradicted
- **Non-canonical designer ruling:** Real human/designer decision, not Canonical
- **Open:** No settled decision exists, not table-ready

1. **GURPS Systems Compatibility:** Assumed standard GURPS mechanics based on common GURPS adventure patterns
2. **Adventure Content Requirements:** Inferred mechanical requirements from typical GURPS adventure design
3. **Tiwan Content Gaps:** Identified missing content based on subsystem status in live register

### Unresolved Questions
1. **Exact Adventure Content:** Without direct PDF access, specific mechanical requirements cannot be precisely enumerated
2. **Designer Intent for Missing Systems:** No specification exists for how magic or special abilities should be implemented
3. **Content Enumeration Priority:** No established priority for which S-3 effects should be developed first
4. **NPC Design Philosophy:** No established approach for creature/NPC design beyond S-12 framework

### Verification Needed
1. **Direct PDF Analysis:** Requires actual PDF content extraction for precise mechanical requirements
2. **Designer Consultation:** Needed for magic system design and special abilities framework
3. **Playtest Validation:** Required to confirm subsystem functionality in actual play
